# ompdesk — Development Rules

Read this before any edit in this repository.

## Repository identity

- Product: **OMPdesk** (`OMP` capitals, `desk` lowercase) is the display name in every user-visible string, the bundle (`OMPdesk.app`, `productName`), menus, tray, and window title. An unofficial desktop app for the omp agent. Identifiers stay lowercase: the package name, the bundle id `com.seuput.ompdesk`, `app.setName("ompdesk")` (which picks userData `~/Library/Application Support/ompdesk` (`%APPDATA%\ompdesk`) and the "ompdesk Safe Storage" keychain item), the repo, and the two release asset names `ompdesk-mac-arm64.dmg` / `ompdesk-windows-setup.exe` (README download links and the updater fetch them by exact name; `packaging-config.test.ts` pins them). The first launch moves the pre-rename profile from its sibling directory into userData before anything reads it (`src/main/legacy-user-data.ts`, skipped under `--user-data-dir`). Say "omp" only for the agent/CLI (`omp update`, "omp 18.7+"). The mark (an omp-style π: top bar and two legs, the shorter left leg ending in an accent-colored foot; full and ≤48px pixel-grid forms) is defined once in `src/shared/brand-mark.ts`; `bun run gen:icons` writes the SVGs in `resources/icon-source*.svg` and `resources/brand/` from it (`scripts/brand-svgs.ts`; never hand-edit them, `brand-mark.test.ts` fails on drift; the hand-made README buttons `resources/brand/download-*.svg` are exempt) and regenerates `icon.png`/`icon.icns`/`icon.ico`, the tray PNGs and the favicon. The in-app mark is `components/common/BrandMark.tsx`.
- Product repo: [`SeuPut0705/ompdesk`](https://github.com/SeuPut0705/ompdesk) (`origin`). All code, tags, and releases live here. The repo is **public** under the MIT License (`LICENSE`, `Copyright (c) 2026 SeuPut`; contributions come in under MIT too, no CLA). There is no `NOTICE` file: the attribution of vendored code (Mixdog-adapted files, REA's skill, the lobe-icons brand marks) and the license of every bundled dependency live in `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.txt`, generated from `node_modules` and `scripts/generate-third-party-notices.ts` by `bun run gen:licenses` (`bun run licenses:check` fails when it is stale; regenerate when production dependencies or vendored code change; the license texts of vendored code are in `LICENSES/`). `LICENSE`, `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.txt`, `LICENSES/` and Electron's `LICENSES.chromium.html` ship inside the app (`extraResources`, pinned by `packaging-config.test.ts`). Never commit a secret, a real e-mail, a tailnet or LAN address, or a screenshot showing one: the history is public.
- The agent is **stock omp** ([`can1357/oh-my-pi`](https://github.com/can1357/oh-my-pi)) installed on the user's machine. It is never built, vendored, or forked here. Users get agent updates with `omp update`.
- Platforms: **macOS and Windows only.** Developing on Windows (setup, Git Bash as the one shell for the bash scripts, what differs, known limits): `docs/windows-dev.md`.
- Brand surfaces, all generated by `bun run gen:icons` from the one mark (never hand-drawn; `brand-mark.test.ts` and `packaging-config.test.ts` pin them): the app icon (`icon.icns`, `icon.ico`, `icon.png`), the tray PNGs, `brand/app-icon.png` (256 px: `BrowserWindow` `icon` on Windows, the About panel's `iconPath` and Windows toasts through `src/main/app-icon.ts`; packaged as `extraResources` `app-icon.png`), `dmg-background.tiff` (the DMG window, `dmg.background`; the volume icon is `icon.icns`), the NSIS `installerIcon`/`uninstallerIcon`, the favicon and the first-paint shell in `#root` of `index.html` and `web.html` (`brandHtml` in `scripts/brand-svgs.ts` writes both from `scripts/boot-shell.ts`, the π small in its empty transcript; React replaces it), the PWA icons and the push `badge` (`icons/badge-96.png`), and `brand/social-preview.png` (1280×640; GitHub has no API to set it: upload it in repo Settings › Social preview). A new surface that shows the app's identity gets its asset from `scripts/brand-svgs.ts`, not a copy.

## Architecture

```
renderer (React) ── IPC ──▶ main (Electron)
                              ├─ src/main/local/*      fork-era commands served in main
                              │     (git/gh, fs, ~/.omp files, `omp` CLI, translation to stock RPC)
                              ├─ src/main/sidecar.ts   spawns `omp --mode rpc-ui -e <gui-bridge>/index.ts`
                              │                         (stock RPC over stdio)
                              └─ src/main/bridge-client.ts ◀── unix socket / named pipe ──▶
                                                         resources/gui-bridge/*  (omp extension,
                                                         live AgentSession access)
```

- The renderer speaks one command set. `RpcClient.command()` (`src/main/rpc-client.ts`) first looks for a **local handler** (`src/main/local/index.ts` merges `src/main/local/<area>.ts` maps; duplicate keys throw at startup). Commands without a local handler go to stock omp unchanged.
- A local handler (`LocalHandler`, `src/main/local/types.ts`) either does the work in main (git, gh, files, `ctx.omp.run([...])`), translates to stock RPC (`ctx.send(...)`), or calls the bridge (`ctx.bridge.request(method, params)`) when it needs live session state.
- Main's IPC handlers register through the one `IpcRegistry` (`src/main/web/ipc-registry.ts`, created in `index.ts`), never on `ipcMain` directly: `ipc.handle(channel, (caller, …args) => …)` / `ipc.on(…)` registers on `ipcMain` as before and also in the table a non-Electron caller (the planned web client) is dispatched from. A handler finds its window as `caller.win` (a `ClientWindow`, `src/main/web/client-window.ts`: the id, `send`, `isDestroyed` and the `closed` listener the sidecar pool, the window records and the handlers use), the calling page as `caller.sender`, and only a dialog sheet, the title-bar overlay or the preview take the native `caller.browserWindow`.
- `window.omp` is `createOmpApi(transport)` (`src/shared/omp-api.ts`): the preload hands it an `ipcRenderer` transport and adds only `fs.pathForFile`; a paired web page will hand it a WebSocket one. A new API member goes in `omp-api.ts`, never the preload. Every channel is classified in `src/main/web/ipc-policy.ts` (`IPC_POLICY`, typed over the whole channel union: a new channel does not compile until it has a direction, a `WebPermission` and a web mode; `ipc-policy.test.ts` matches it against the handlers main registers and the channels `createOmpApi` subscribes to). The web client's frames and permissions are `src/shared/web-protocol.ts`.
- Multi-viewer (`sidecar-pool.ts`): a tab has its owner window and may have viewer windows (`attachViewer`; only a paired web device's window, whose ids are negative, ever views another window's tab — `tab-spawn.ts` attaches one instead of refusing an owned session; env `OMP_GUI_TEST_VIEWER_WINDOWS=1` lets any window do it, for `e2e/multi-viewer.e2e.ts`). Each window keeps its own active/visible/split view; the full channel is wired once per tab and fans out to every window it is visible in; the light channel reaches owner and viewers. A viewer closing the tab only leaves it (`detachViewer`), the owner's close releases it for all (`TAB_LIST_CHANGED` to the viewers, which the renderer applies with `applyTabList`: `shared`/`viewerCount` on the tab, which drive behaviour only; the user's rule (19 Oct 2026): **nothing on screen says a tab is shared** — no title-bar chip, no line under the composer). The first window to answer an ask wins: the others get a synthetic `cancel` (`answeredElsewhere`, a toast "Answered on another device"), a later answer fails `already_answered` (also a toast, the card closes). Draft-empty reports count per window (`noteDraft`), the subagent stream level is the highest any window asked (`noteSubagentLevel`), a mode toggle tells the other windows to re-read (`noteModeChanged` → `config_update {modes: true}`), a shared tab's notification is deduped across its windows, its terminals are keyed by the owner window, and `PREFS_SET` pushes `PREFS_CHANGED` to the other windows. Renderer rules that keep a shared tab honest (`stores/messages.ts`, `composer-submit.ts`): on a shared tab or a web page a plain message goes as `prompt(streamingBehavior)` and the host decides steer/follow-up (`hostDecides`), an optimistic echo is claimed only by the delivery with its own text (`sharedTab`, `ownSends`) and any other user delivery is another viewer's (`fromOtherViewer`, "from another device"), the dequeue's `omp:fill-composer` names the asking `tabId`, quota resume runs in the owner window only, and a web page keeps its own done marks (`lib/unread-marks.ts`). Prompt origins: main sends the bridge `prompt_origin_set {origin, text}` (local command `prompt_origin_set`) before a prompt, steer or follow-up from a paired web device (every window under the test hook) reaches the omp; `resources/gui-bridge/prompt-origin.ts` journals that device for the user message with that text, `labelPromptOrigins(…, null)` marks every prompt of a local transcript with `promptFromId`, and `ipc.ts` localizes each `get_transcript_page` per window (`localizePromptOrigins`: its own prompts plain, the host's named with the computer's name). A live delivery on another window says only "from another device" until the transcript is read again.
- Mobile web server (`src/main/web/`; assembled by `WebHost` in `web-host.ts`, created in `index.ts`): HTTP and WebSocket on `127.0.0.1:47831` only (`tailscale serve` publishes it; the Tailscale side is `src/main/tailscale/`). `server.ts` serves the built renderer from an allowlist (`/` and `/pair` → `web.html`, `/assets/ /cmaps/ /standard_fonts/ /wasm/ /icons/`, the manifest, `sw.js`; traversal refused), `POST /api/pair` (no code: an allowed Tailscale login's first visit makes the device and its cookie), `POST /api/session` (the device signs a server nonce with its WebCrypto key, then gets a 30 s single-use ticket), `/ws`, `/blob/<token>` (Range; HTML/SVG are downloads only) and `POST /upload` (25 MiB a file, 200 MiB a device in 24 h, deleted after 24 h). `auth.ts` checks every request, in this order: loopback peer; any `Tailscale-Funnel-Request` refused; **the secret path prefix** (`stripServePrefix`, constant-time: a request without it, or before serve is published, is a bare 404 and none of its headers is read; the server takes it off, so the page never sees it); `Host`, `Origin`, `Sec-Fetch-Site`; then `Tailscale-User-Login` (RFC 2047-decoded) against the allowed logins — a login that is not allowed, or no login (a tagged node), gets `refusal-page.ts`'s HTML (which account to use, `Accept-Language` ko/en; JSON `tailscale_user_not_allowed` / `no_tailscale_user` for an API call). **Identity is Tailscale's**: those walls decide who may ask, and the device cookie is what every later call uses (`__Host-ompdesk` over HTTPS; `devices-store.ts`: `web-devices.json` 0600, only the secret's SHA-256, 14 days sliding / 90 absolute, the record keeps the Tailscale node `nodeId`). `POST /api/pair` asks `tailscale whois` about serve's `X-Forwarded-For` address (injected `whois`, cached 60 s): the node's stable ID and device name (the device is named after it, else the page's own name), refusing a login that whois does not put behind that address (`identity_mismatch`), a blocked node (`blocked`), and more than 10 tries a minute from an address or 20 new devices an hour (`PairingGuard`, `NEW_DEVICES_PER_HOUR`). With "Approve new devices on this computer" (pref `mobileWeb.confirmNewDevices`, default **off**) `pairing.ts`'s `PairingConfirmations` holds the visit until the desktop approves (`WEB_PAIRING_REQUEST` to the desktop windows only, 60 s); otherwise the desktop is told after the fact, once (`WEB_DEVICE_CONNECTED`, desktop windows only, a toast with Block). **Block** (`WEB_DEVICES_REVOKE`, `WebHost.block`) is a revoke that sticks: `blocks-store.ts` (`web-blocked.json` 0600) keeps the node's stable ID — never the login, since one login has many devices — and every record of that node is revoked (sockets close with 4401, the virtual window is destroyed); a cookie-less page load while a block exists and `/api/pair` look the visitor up (`whois`) and answer the "this device is blocked" page / `blocked`; a visitor whose node cannot be told while its login has a blocked node is held back too (`unidentified`, fail closed), and a device with no node identity is blocked by its login (`WebDeviceInfo.blockScope`, the confirm and the Blocked list say every device of that login), `WEB_BLOCKS_LIST`/`WEB_BLOCKS_REMOVE` list and lift blocks. Revoking by expiry only deletes the record. `ws-router.ts` turns `inv`/`snd` frames into `IpcRegistry.invoke/send` calls with the device's `WebClientWindow` (`web-client-window.ts`, negative id, parked while no socket is open) as caller once `IPC_POLICY` and the device's grants allow the channel (`web_forbidden` otherwise; `pairing-admin` is never served and a web device cannot write `mobileWeb.*` prefs), meters model commands through `PeerQuota` (`remote/peer-quota.ts` takes any `QuotaStore`), batches pushes every 75 ms (neighbouring slim `message_update` deltas of a reply are joined), stops the streams while the page reports itself hidden, and pings every 20 s (three quiet ticks end a socket). Calls only a web device has (`web:host.*` browse/save/open/reveal/blob, `web:models.config.read/write` for models.yml as text through `readModelsText`/`writeModelsText` (parser + schema check, atomic, refuses a file that changed since it was read), `web:device.uiPrefs.*`; `shared/web-host.ts`, `host-actions.ts`) are a table in the router, not in `IPC_POLICY`. `FS_READ_BINARY` answers `{dataBase64}` up to 512 KiB, else `{blobUrl}`; `FS_MEDIA_STREAM` answers a `/blob` URL served from `MediaStreams`. Tests drive time through injected clocks and `Timers`; `server.test.ts` runs the real server on an ephemeral port, behind the prefix and with serve's headers (a stubbed `fetch` adds them), with only the IPC registry, the desktop window and `whois` faked.
  - Coming back is cheap (`ws-router.ts`, `ws-transport.ts`): the page's return to the foreground sends `hello` again (and the page resyncs in place, no reload: see "Reloads of the web page" below) only when the host dropped stream pushes while it was hidden, and a reconnect offers `resume.<connId>` (its last connection's id) as a subprotocol; `WsRouter.attach` answers `resumed: true` when that connection ended with every event acknowledged, nothing queued or dropped, and no push reached the device since (`#pushes`, ended connections kept 10 min, `RESUME_WINDOW_MS`); `server.ts` first retires a still-open old connection of that id (the host had not seen the drop). A call the drop failed makes the page resync anyway. Measured with `e2e/web-perf.e2e.ts` (opt-in `OMP_GUI_WEB_PERF=<dir>`: the built app behind the fake serve, whose proxy plays a phone's link — 150 ms RTT, 9/1.5 Mbit/s, a handshake per new connection — Chromium at 4× CPU and WebKit at 390×844; `OMP_GUI_WEB_PERF_SUBJECTS=before=<checkout>,after=<checkout>` compares built checkouts, `_PHASES` load,ws,stream,input,memory, `_RUNS`, `_MINUTES`, `_PROFILE=1` adds a CPU profile and the call timeline of each warm load).
  - Pictures a page's call carries (a prompt's, a steer's or a queued message's image blocks, from the picker, a paste or a drop) never ride the socket: a frame holds 1 MB (`MAX_FRAME_BYTES`, the page refuses a bigger request) and one phone photo is larger even after the composer scales it to `IMAGE_MAX_SIDE`. `shim.ts` sends any call holding an inline `{type: "image", data, mimeType}` through `upload.ts` `liftInlineImages` first (each picture's bytes to `POST /upload`, the block becomes `WebUploadedImage {type, mimeType, upload: <host path>}` from `shared/web-protocol.ts`; a refused or lost upload fails the call with `web.upload.image*` and nothing is sent), and `ws-router.ts` `#landUploads` reads each one back into `data` before the call runs (`UploadStore.take`: only this device's `<device>/<uuid>/<name>`, deleted once read; a missing one fails the call `file_not_found`). Calls without such a block stay synchronous and untouched. Pinned by `shim.test.ts`, `ws-router.test.ts`, `uploads.test.ts` and `e2e/mobile-attach.e2e.ts` (a camera JPEG, a 12 MB JPEG and a HEIC through the fake serve prefix in Chromium and WebKit, recorded by the fake omp and shown in the sent bubble).
  - Attaching on a phone: on touch the composer's + menu has **Photos** (`accept="image/*"`: Android offers its gallery and camera only then; any-file opens the Files app, which lists no gallery) and **Attach files** (no `accept`). A phone's picker is another app, so the page goes hidden behind it and its `change` arrives just after the page is back, the moment the transport's `onResync` (stream pushes dropped while hidden, e.g. a reply streaming) or an update's `resume` wanted to reload: that reload dropped every pick (reproduced on Android Chromium 158 in an emulator; no upload ever reached the host). Every pick is tracked from the input's click until its files landed (`lib/file-pick.ts` `trackFilePick`/`filePickInProgress`; `cancel` ends it, or 3 s back in front without a `change` for a browser without `cancel`); `safeToReload` waits for it, the resync is now in place (no reload, see "Reloads of the web page" below), and its last-resort reload goes through `sw-update.ts` `reloadWhenUnused` (waits while a pick, a composer picture, an upload or an unsaved edit would be lost; typed text outlives it, saved on `pagehide`). A non-image file goes up through `fs.pathForFile` (the composer shows a `[data-upload-chip]` "Sending … to your computer" while it goes, then mentions its host path for the agent to read); a failed upload rejects with its worded reason (`web.upload.*`, 25 MB cap checked on the phone) and the composer shows it as an error, nothing inserted. Pinned by `file-pick.test.ts`, `sw-update.test.ts`, `safe-moment.test.ts`, `composer-files.test.ts`, `InputArea.queue-shorthand.test.tsx` and `e2e/mobile-attach.e2e.ts` (the + menu, a PDF, a text file and a photo picked while the page is hidden and a reply streams, and a 26 MB file refused, in Chromium and WebKit).
  - Reloads of the web page (`src/renderer/web/`; v0.36.1–v0.38.1 phones reloaded or crashed by themselves). **Every reload says why**: `reload-reason.ts` notes `{reason, at, details}` in `localStorage` just before it (`update`, `outdated`, `resync-failed`, `chunk-load-failed`, `error-boundary`) and `web-boot.ts` sends the kept reasons as `web-reload` runtime-log lines (source in `RuntimeErrorSource`, the page's negative windowId) on each connect; renderer code reloads only through `lib/page-reload.ts` `reloadPage(reason)` (`WebRuntime.reload`, which lets a waiting new build take over first). **Missed events never reload**: `ws-transport.ts` `onResync(cause)` (`missed-events`: the host could not resume, it dropped pushes or restarted; `lost-calls`) runs `resync.ts` `PageResync` (one pass at a time, a reconnect during a pass reruns it, a failed pass retried after 2 s × failures) over `resync-page.ts` (`reconcileTabs({dropUnlisted: true})`, the host's list as the whole truth since a restarted host gives its tabs new ids; `hydrateTabSession` of each visible ready/running tab, which merges while a reply streams; a reply still streaming gets the deltas it missed back because every hello that sends the page to resync (`resumed: false`) has the host send each of its tabs' next `message_update` whole again (`WsRouter` `onResync` → `SidecarPool.resnapshotWindow`, a fresh slimmer), and the messages store takes a snapshot of the message it streams when it is longer than its buffer; `settleTabExtensionUiRequests` shows missed asks and drops blocking asks answered meanwhile; `loadRemoteState`; `refreshHeldSessionLists`; then `WebRuntime.onResynced` listeners, e.g. the worktree prompts). Only after three failed passes in a row does it reload, through `reloadWhenUnused` (hidden, or a launch/return moment that is `safeToReload`). **Updates only while unused**: `sw-update.ts` `ResumeGate` is open at launch and on a return after ≥ 30 s hidden (`RESUME_AFTER_HIDDEN_MS`) for 15 s or until the first `pointerdown`/`keydown`/`wheel`/`touchstart` (a scroll alone does not count: the transcript scrolls itself); `applyUpdate` resumes a waiting worker only then. An `outdated` page (host's `minClient` newer) cannot talk to the host and waits only for `safeToReload`; before the app loaded it purges at once. **Chunks of an old build**: a page older than the host's update (left open, or booted from its worker's cached shell, which is stale-while-revalidate) asks for hashed lazy chunks the new build lacks; v0.38.1 crashed to the error screen ("Importing a module script failed." at `Lazy` under `App`: `BenchmarkDialog` was mounted, so loaded, at every launch; now mounted from its first open). Three layers: the host keeps the replaced builds' `/assets/` (`main/web/asset-archive.ts`: each build copied once at `WebHost.start` into `<userData>/web-assets/<build>/`, build = hash of `web.html`; replaced builds kept 7 days, 2 at most; `server.ts` serves a missing `/assets/` file from them by name only); the worker keeps every hashed `/assets/` file of its build it fetched (`sw-core.ts` `omp-assets-<build>`, `RUNTIME_LIMITS` 24 MB / 150, oldest out), retries a failed fetch once after `ASSET_RETRY_MS` and then takes another build's copy of the same name; the page's lazy views go through `lib/chunk-load.ts` `lazyChunk`/`loadChunk` (one retry, then the view renders nothing), every other failing Vite dynamic import counts through `vite:preloadError` unless a retry recovered it within 3 s, and a failure for good never asks the user anything (updates are automatic: no banner, no button, the user's rule): it marks the build stale (`useChunkFailureStore`) and `web-boot.ts` marks the page outdated for its `UpdateWatcher` (`markOutdated()`; `chunkStale`), so the update gate reloads it at the next unused, safe moment (launch or a return after ≥ 30 s hidden, `safeToReload`), reason `chunk-load-failed`; a page without a worker purges through the same gate. On a web page `RootErrorBoundary` shows a quiet empty screen for a chunk error (`data-app-stale`, no text, no button) and marks the app gone too (`appGone`), and the page then reloads through `reloadWhenUnused` (hidden, or a launch/return moment) over `appGoneMoment` (sw-update.ts): the typed text no longer holds it, because the composer saves its draft as it unmounts (InputArea's flush, `InputArea.draft-unmount.test.tsx`), while a picture, an unsaved edit, a pick, an upload and (visible) an open question still do, since none of them is saved; a desktop window keeps the ordinary crash screen. A locale chunk that fails is forgotten so the next use retries. **Muted errors**: every script tag carries `crossorigin`; a "Script error." with no file/line is logged with `details.muted` and the user agent (it comes from a script the browser or an extension injected). Separately, v0.36.1's "Maximum call stack size exceeded" at `place` was the session tree layout (`panels/session-tree-layout.ts`) recursing once per level and walking up from every entry (quadratic): both passes are iterative now (`session-tree-layout.test.ts`, a 100,000-entry chain). Pinned by `reload-reason`/`resync.test.ts`, `resync-page.test.ts`, `sw-update.test.ts`, `sw-core.test.ts`, `ws-transport.test.ts`, `chunk-load.test.tsx`, `RootErrorBoundary.test.tsx`, `tabs.test.tsx`, `asset-archive.test.ts`, `server.test.ts`, `ws-router.test.ts` and `messages.test.ts`; on the built app by `e2e/web-no-reload.e2e.ts` (Chromium and WebKit: a drop mid-reply catches up in place with no new page load and no gap in the reply; after the host restarts on another build without the Agents chunk, the never-loaded view opens from the kept assets and the draft survives; a view whose chunk neither build nor archive has stays empty with no prompt and no reload while in use, then reloads by itself on the return after ≥ 30 s hidden with the reason in the host's log), `e2e/mobile-autopilot-rows.e2e.ts` and `e2e/mobile-attach.e2e.ts`. The spec's lazy views are the feedback and model setup dialogs: the pages (Agents, Stats, Settings) and the side panel's views are preloaded once the page idles, so they are never the never-loaded view. A tab the page learns only from the host's list (its `TAB_STATUS` went by before the page knew the id, as after a host restart) takes the list's status into its runtime (`reconcileTabs`; without it the composer's Send stayed disabled after the restart). A page load in those specs is `domcontentloaded` (or a `window` marker), never `framenavigated`, which also fires for the page's same-document history entries.
  - A paired web device's tabs survive this computer's relaunch (`src/main/web/web-tab-layouts.ts`, pref `webTabLayouts`: device id → `PersistedTabLayout`). Its `WebClientWindow` id is new every launch, so `SidecarPool.onWindowTabsChanged` files a web window's layout under `deviceId` (never while `quitApproved()`: the quit's teardown is not the device closing its tabs; a window with no tab left forgets it); at launch, after the desktop windows, `restoreLayout` puts each still-paired device's layout into `webHost.windows.windowFor(deviceId)` (deferred tabs, before `turnResume.launch()`), so the page's next `tabs.list` finds them; a revoked, blocked or expired device's layout is dropped (`devices.onRevoked`), and one for a device no longer listed at launch is pruned. Desktop `tabLayouts` are untouched. One session, one tab, one omp on its file: a web window's layout also names the stored sessions it views (another window owns them; saved again when it starts or stops viewing one, or their owner closes it), and its restore views a session a desktop window already holds again (`attachViewer`) instead of opening a second tab; a desktop window's restore skips a session another desktop window holds. A page whose spawn is refused `owned` by a tab of its own window it has not listed yet reads the tab list and switches to it (`openTab`); a web page never answers a refusal with "open in a new window", which on the web is the same `openTab` again. Pinned by `web-tab-layouts.test.ts`, `sidecar-pool.test.ts` and `tabs.test.tsx`; the phone's real page across an Autopilot run, viewing a desktop session, a reconnect and a restart by `e2e/mobile-autopilot-rows.e2e.ts` (`e2e/fixture/phone-serve.ts` plays `tailscale serve` for a Chromium page on `http://localhost`).
  - Mobile web threat model (no pairing code; the user's rule: copy the tailnet URL, open it on any device signed in to the same Tailscale account, and it works). **Who may connect**: a device whose request reaches us through `tailscale serve` with a `Tailscale-User-Login` in `allowedUsers` (default: this computer's own login, set in Settings › Mobile web), over a Tailscale network only (serve, never Funnel: `service.ts` has no Funnel command and an `AllowFunnel` flag on our port stops the server; `Tailscale-Funnel-Request` is refused). Anyone who can sign in to an allowed account on a tailnet device, and anyone the user shared the computer's Tailscale node with while that account is allowed, can make a device and gets `DEFAULT_WEB_GRANTS` (never `pairing-admin`); the user's tools are "Approve new devices on this computer", the notice's Block and the Devices list (access tiers, limits, Block). **Forged headers**: a local process can write `Tailscale-User-Login` and `X-Forwarded-For` to `127.0.0.1:47831`. Serve joins its target's path to every request's path, so `service.ts` publishes `serve --bg --https=<port> http://127.0.0.1:<local>/<secret>` where the secret is 256 random bits (`serve-secret.ts`, `web-serve-secret.json` 0600, made on first use); only traffic through serve carries it, and without it nothing is served (404) and no header is read (`auth.test.ts`, `server.test.ts` "headers forged on the loopback"). The secret is never in prefs (the `mobileWeb.serve` record holds the local address only; `serve.ts` `ownProxy` owns a handler only when it proxies to the current secret path on any loopback port (the last run listened elsewhere when 47831 was busy: the secret is ours alone, so it is republished over on the same serve port), or to the bare recorded address for the one-time cutover from an earlier release; any other path on that address is the user's own handler and is never touched) and is never logged. **Residual risk**: the secret is in `tailscale serve status` and the 0600 file, so a process running as the same user can read it and then forge a login (and an `X-Forwarded-For` that whois accepts, so whois' cross-check only catches a forgery that contradicts itself); such a process can already read the cookie store, the prefs and the user's files, which is why a single-user Mac is not defended against it (a different user of the computer cannot read either file). `Host`/`Origin`/`Sec-Fetch-Site` stop a web page in a browser from reaching the loopback port. **Not defended**: a compromised allowed account or tailnet device (use Block, and the approval setting), and Tailscale's own control plane. **Block** is by node, not login (a login has many devices); a revoked device that returns is refused, not re-enrolled, until Unblock.
  - Mobile web UI for that flow: Settings › Mobile web (`settings/MobileWebSection.tsx`) shows, once ready, the link in a monospace line with a big **Copy link** button and one QR of it (`MobileWebConnect.tsx`, steps: install Tailscale, same account, open the link, the iOS Home Screen note), the "Approve new devices on this computer" switch (`set-confirm-new-devices`), the connected devices with **Block** (`MobileWebDevices.tsx`; the confirm names `revokeBodyLogin` when `blockScope` is `login`), the **Blocked** list with Unblock (`MobileWebBlocked`, `stores/mobile-web.ts` `blocked`, read again whenever the device list pushes) and the allowed accounts; there is no pairing-code step anywhere. The desktop shows a new device as one toast with a Block action (`dialogs/WebDeviceNotice.tsx`, mounted in `App.tsx`, `mobileWeb.devices.onConnected`) or, with approval on, `WebPairingDialog`. The web page's `/pair` (`web/PairScreen.tsx`) has no form: the page sends a browser whose session is refused there, it makes the device key, calls `POST /api/pair` (`pairing.ts` `pairFailureOf` maps blocked / account not allowed / declined / rate limited) and goes into the app, or shows the reason with Try again; a browser that already can get a ticket just goes on. `e2e/mobile-web.e2e.ts` plays the phone with the fake Tailscale's headers through the prefix (forged without it → 404, first visit → one notice, Block, still denied, Unblock, the approval dialog).
- Web host sheets (`src/renderer/web/host-ui/`, mounted as `<WebHostUi />` in `App.tsx`, nothing on the desktop): the web page has no native dialogs, so `shim.ts` calls the hooks of `web/host-hooks.ts` and `WebHostUi` registers them, queueing one sheet at a time (`host-ui-store.ts`): `PathActionsSheet` (open/reveal a file: view on the phone through the Files viewer, open on the computer, download), `SaveImageSheet` (phone or computer), `HostBrowserSheet` (the computer's folder/file/save browser over `web:host.listDirs`, which also replaces the project picker), `EditorSheet` ($EDITOR round trip, or "Edit on computer"), `ModelsConfigSheet` (models.yml editor, same), `ConfirmSheet` (restart for an update, quit; names the running turns). A link opens on the device at once with a toast to open it on the computer. A device without `desktop-control` gets `hostUi.computerOff`. Every sheet has a component test in `host-ui.test.tsx`. On touch (`data-input=touch`) a sidebar session row shows one ⋯ button (`data-sidebar-row-menu`) that opens its action sheet instead of rename/archive/delete, so the title keeps the width (`mobile-shell.e2e.ts`).
- Preview and Terminal on a phone (web viewers only; the desktop paths are unchanged). Preview: a paired web window has no native view, so `preview/host.ts` gives each of its tabs an offscreen `BrowserWindow` (`offscreen: true`, sandbox, contextIsolation; sessions `preview-web-<winId>` / `preview-visual-web-<winId>` with every rule of the desktop page: non-persistent, http(s) only, permissions/downloads/new windows refused, remote-tab loopback blocked, the visual session's CSP and egress block) and `main/web/preview-stream.ts` (`PageStream`) turns its paints into JPEG frames. The viewer's `preview.stream.poll({ack})` is the only transport (channels `PREVIEW_STREAM_*` in `shared/preview.ts`, all classified in `ipc-policy.ts`); the next frame goes out only after the viewer acknowledged the last (`preview-pacer.ts`: ≤ 12 fps, DPR ≤ 2, `toJPEG` only when the pacer sends, nothing for a still page, a page nobody polls for 15 s stops painting). The renderer's `layout` request carries the surface's `dpr`; `PreviewPanel` mounts `PreviewStreamView` where the desktop leaves a hole for the native view, and sends no cover-detection layouts there. Input is `webContents.sendInputEvent` (`preview-input.ts`, never a debugger, so `isTrusted` holds and `choose` stays allowed): `lib/preview-gestures.ts` reads fingers (tap, double tap, swipe and fling → wheel, long press → context, long press and move → drag, pinch → zoom), `lib/preview-keyboard.ts` maps the hidden `textarea`'s `keydown`/`beforeinput`/composition (soft keyboard and IME; a composition is sent once, committed, as `insertText`). **The one exception to "no preload"**: the two phone sessions get the fixed preload `main/web/preview-dialog.ts` (written by `writeDialogPreload` at launch, `ipcRenderer` only, no page-reachable API) that replaces `alert/confirm/prompt` so the page's dialog waits for the viewer's answer (`PreviewStreamView`'s bottom sheet; an unanswered one is dismissed after 60 s, more than 5 in 10 s are dismissed unseen; `beforeunload` just lets the page go). `savePdf` is overridden in `web/shim.ts`: the host prints (`PREVIEW_STREAM_PDF`), the phone keeps the bytes (share sheet or download). `features.preview` in the `hello` is `true` by default (`web-host.ts`). Terminal: for a web caller `terminal:request list` is a long poll in the terminals worker (`waitMs` ≤ 1 s: held until some listed session has output past its offset or exited; each answer is cut to a 64 KB `budget`), and `lib/terminal-poll.ts` makes the next request only after xterm has parsed the last output, so the request is the acknowledgement and an idle shell costs nothing; a desktop window sends neither field and keeps its 80 ms poll. PTY size lease (`main/terminal/lease.ts`, the S11 decision): a PTY has one size, so one viewer sizes it. A lone viewer holds it implicitly, the first `resize` or an explicit `claim` takes it, typing takes it only when the holder has been idle 15 s, a holder with no request for 10 s has let go. Every snapshot carries `lease: you | other | free` and the PTY's `cols`/`rows`; a viewer that is `other` draws the PTY at those columns × rows (xterm `resize`, scrolling sideways) and neither fits nor sends `resize`, and `TerminalPanel` shows a banner (ko "다른 기기가 크기를 정하고 있어요 · 여기서 맞추기") whose button sends `claim`. The key bar (`TerminalKeyBar.tsx`, touch only, keys from `lib/terminal-keys.ts`: Esc, Tab, sticky Ctrl/Alt for the next key from the bar or the soft keyboard, repeating arrows, punctuation, ^C/^D) and the text size 50–200 % (`localStorage["omp.terminalFontScale"]`) are renderer-only. A phone's Changes view shows the unified diff only (no split toggle), and Changes and Files are a list first, then one item; both poll only while mounted and the page is visible (the panel's sheet unmounts on close). `e2e/preview-stream.e2e.ts` (a real Electron OSR page and a fake WS client; measured ~10 fps, ~107 KB a frame, ~12 % of one core busy, 0 frames idle) and `e2e/mobile-panels.e2e.ts` (the dev harness's fixture frames, terminal, git and file data; iPhone and iPad × Chromium and WebKit; `OMP_GUI_MOBILE_PANELS=<dir>` writes captures) cover it.
- Mobile web's Tailscale side (`src/main/tailscale/`; contract `src/shared/mobile-web.ts`; created in `index.ts`, pushes `mobile-web:state` to desktop windows only, handlers `mobile-web:get-state` / `mobile-web:command` on the `IpcRegistry`): `MobileWebService` (`service.ts`) derives its `MobileWebPhase` from the world on every poll tick (15 s; 2 s while Settings watches or a step waits on the user or the OS) — `tailscale status --json` and `serve status --json`, never the human text (only a `login.tailscale.com` link is taken from stderr, and `openExternal` gets only that host or the control server's). `locate.ts` finds the CLI (env `OMP_GUI_TAILSCALE`, which never falls through to a real install, pref `mobileWeb.tailscaleBin`, the macOS app binary run with `TAILSCALE_BE_CLI=1`, `/usr/local/bin`, Homebrew, login-shell PATH; Windows Program Files, PATH) and the variant (bundle id), `cli.ts` runs it through an injectable executor over `spawn.ts` (arg arrays, 10 s), `status.ts`/`serve.ts`/`whois.ts` parse with zod keeping only the needed keys. Publishing is only `serve --bg --https=<port> http://127.0.0.1:<local>/<secret>` (the per-install secret path of `web/serve-secret.ts`, the only thing that tells serve's traffic from a local process's; see the Mobile web server's threat model), from 47831 up to the first port nothing uses, recorded in pref `mobileWeb.serve` (`{host, port, target}`, `target` the local address **without** the secret, which never reaches the prefs); turning off runs `serve --https=<port> off` only when that record and the handler (one `/` proxy to the current secret path on any loopback port, or to the bare recorded target an earlier release published for the one-time cutover: `ownProxy`; any other path on that address is the user's and is neither overwritten nor removed) still match exactly, then checks the user's other handlers are unchanged, else only the record is dropped. A handler an earlier release published without the secret is still ours and is published again over itself on the same port (`planPublish` reuses a port only when the handler already proxies to exactly the new target). There is no `serve reset` and no Funnel command in the source (`service.test.ts` greps for both); an `AllowFunnel` flag on our port is `funnelExposed` and stops the server. `allowedUsers` (pref `mobileWeb.allowedUsers`, default this computer's login) is read by the web server through `allowedUsers()`; `confirmNewDevices` (pref `mobileWeb.confirmNewDevices`, default **off**, request `set-confirm-new-devices`, `MobileWebState.confirmNewDevices`) through `confirmNewDevices()`; `whois(address)` (`tailscale whois --json <ip>`: the CLI is only ever given an IP address, read-only, 4 s, `parseWhois` keeps the stable ID, node name and owner login) answers the server's lookups while Mobile web is on. Tailscale is the optional setup item `tailscale` (`setupItemAutomatic` is false: never installed at launch or in the background pass, no once-per-launch rule, no back-off ledger; only `installForConnection` and Retry): macOS = the Standalone zip from `pkgs.tailscale.com/stable/?mode=json` plus its `.sha256`, then `codesign --verify --deep --strict`, bundle id and team `W5364U7YZB` before `ditto` to /Applications; Windows = winget, else the arch's MSI plus `.sha256` plus a Valid `Tailscale Inc.` Authenticode signature, then elevated `msiexec /qn`. Tests never touch a real daemon: vitest uses `fakeTailscaleExecutor` (`test-fake-tailscale.ts`; peers carry `ip`/`stableId` for `whois`), e2e the fake CLI `e2e/fixture/fake-tailscale.ts` (`fixtureTailscale(dir)` in `fake-tailscale-install.ts`; env `OMP_GUI_TAILSCALE`, `OMP_GUI_TEST_TAILSCALE_OPEN` = the stand-in for "start the Tailscale app", `FAKE_TAILSCALE_STATE` scenario JSON, `FAKE_TAILSCALE_LOG` call log). The real Mac's own `tailscale serve` (443 → 3010) must stay untouched.
- Web Push, host side (contract `src/shared/web-push.ts`; `main/web/{vapid-store,push,web-push-notifier}.ts`, wired by `WebHost` and `index.ts`): `VapidStore` keeps the VAPID keypair in `<userData>/web-push.json` (0600, atomic; a missing or corrupt file makes a new pair and `WebHost` then drops every stored subscription, which were made against the old key). `PushSender` is the `web-push` package behind an injectable `PushTransport` (tests put a fake push service there): one attempt, TTL (asks 6 h, news 1 h, a `clear` 2 min) and urgency (asks `high`), 404/410 → `forgetPushSubscription` (only while it is still the refused endpoint), anything else logged. `GET|POST /api/push` (device cookie, `device-self` grant, JSON, Origin like every POST, 20 a minute a device) stores `WebDevice.push` (`devices-store.ts`; removed with the device on revoke, a damaged block costs only the push settings); an endpoint must be a push service's `https` DNS name on the default port (`isPushEndpoint`: the host sends the request, so loopback, LAN, tailnet and IP literals are refused). `WebPushNotifier` is driven by `SidecarPool.observeTabs` (`TabActivity`: busy/idle with the run's outcome, ask raised/closed, released; `tabFacts` for the debounce's second look; a provider sign-in's requests are never asks), through `WebHost.tabActivity` only while the server runs: a settle is announced 1.5 s later unless the tab ran again, was Stopped, waits for the connection or for stock's retry, or the same run was already announced. It reuses `NotificationBatcher` unchanged in rules (one extra batcher whose "windows" are the paired devices, fed only the categories each device switched on; `show` now also gets `ShownInfo` and the batcher has `retract` for an answered ask): ask > error > completion, the first event at once, one summary after 4 s that replaces the first banner (same tag, `silent`). A device with a visible socket (`WsRouter.isDeviceVisible`, the page's `presence`) is never pushed, not even a `clear` (its page shows a toast and closes its own notice); `hideContent` sends generic titles and no body; text is the host's UI language (`push.*` and `batch.*` in `i18n-text.ts`). Tags are `tab:<tabId>` (a summary of one tab keeps its `tabId`, of several has none).
- Mobile web as an installed app (PWA; `src/renderer/web/`, `scripts/pwa-plugin.ts`): `sw.ts` is the service worker served at `/sw.js` (scope `/`; built by the renderer's `pwaPlugin` in `electron.vite.config.ts` with the precache list and a build id baked in; `scripts/pwa-plugin.ts` is a `BUILD_SCRIPTS` input of `build-cache.ts`). Its logic is `sw-core.ts`, every browser object injected and unit-tested with fakes: `push` parses a `WebPushPayload` (`shared/web-push.ts`; an unknown `v` is ignored) and shows it (`tag`, `silent`, `data{tabId,url}`), `clear` closes the notifications of its tag and shows nothing, `notificationclick` focuses an open page (posting `{type:"notification-click",payload:{tabId}}`, which `notify.ts` routes) or opens `notificationOpenUrl`. The shell cache is bounded (`PRECACHE_LIMITS`: bytes and count; the page's eager chunks plus the app's static closure, never lazy chunks): cache-first for those hashed `/assets/` files and, in `omp-assets-<build>`, for every other hashed `/assets/` file of its build the page fetched (lazy chunks included, with a retry and another build's copy as fallback; see "Reloads of the web page"), stale-while-revalidate for `/` (this build's cached shell answers at once and a fetch refreshes it behind, `waitUntil`; the network when nothing is cached; a newer host build arrives through its new worker below), and it never touches `/api /ws /blob /upload /healthz`; one `omp-shell-<build>` and one `omp-assets-<build>` cache per build, older ones deleted on activate. The page registers the worker only after the app (`../main`) has loaded, and the install revalidates `/` but takes the hashed assets from the HTTP cache (`cache: "default"`), so a first visit downloads its shell once, not twice side by side. Updates apply by themselves, with no banner and no strings (the user's rule): a new worker waits, and `sw-update.ts` `UpdateWatcher.resume(moment)` (called only while `ResumeGate` is open: at launch once the app's stores are loaded and on a return after ≥ 30 s hidden (`RESUME_AFTER_HIDDEN_MS`), for `RESUME_WINDOW_MS` 15 s or until the first touch, key or wheel, so a build that finishes installing within that window still applies; never while the page is in use) sends it `skip-waiting` and reloads once it controls the page; a host whose `minClient` is newer (`outdated`) is marked by `markOutdated()` and waits only for `safeToReload` (it can no longer talk to the host), with `purgeAndReload` (unregister the worker, clear the caches, load from the network; the cleanup is bounded by `PURGE_CLEANUP_MS`, 3 s, because WebKit has left a cache delete unsettled and the page then never reloaded, and the safe moment is checked again right before the reload: a page that became unsafe meanwhile keeps the purge and reloads at the next `resume`) instead. The gate is `safeToReload(SafeMoment)`: page visible, composer draft and attachments empty, no ask/approval/plan card open, no upload in flight (`uploadsInFlight()` in `upload.ts`), no unsaved Files editor change (`fileEditDirty`/`pendingFileSwitch`) and no host sheet or dialog on screen (the host editor and models-config drafts live in those sheets; `safe-moment.ts` `pageSafeMoment` reads it all from the stores), so a reload never lands mid-typing; the draft is persisted per session anyway. One reload per watcher (`#applying`). Unit-tested with fakes in `sw-update.test.ts`. `/manifest.webmanifest` and the icons (`icons/icon-192|512|maskable-512.png`, root `apple-touch-icon.png`) live in `src/renderer/public/`; the PNGs come from `bun run gen:icons` (`scripts/pwa-icons.ts` from `PWA_ICON_SVGS` in `brand-svgs.ts`; `bun scripts/pwa-icons.ts` writes only them). Page side: `push-client.ts` (`PushClient` over `GET|POST /api/push`; the permission prompt is the first await of `enable()` so it keeps the click's user gesture; a changed VAPID key resubscribes), `install.ts` (iOS has push only for Home Screen apps, so Safari in a tab gets Share › Add to Home Screen steps; Chromium's `beforeinstallprompt` is kept for an Install button), `pwa.ts` (`webPwa()`, non-null only on the web page). On the web page Settings › Mobile web renders `WebNotificationsSection` (this device's notifications: on/off, hide content, categories, why it cannot work, install guidance) instead of the computer's panels; the desktop section keeps its own iOS step (`mobileWeb.step4`).
  - The phone's first screen stays small: the side panel's views are a lazy chunk (`layout/PanelViews.tsx`; `PanelContainer` loads it at once on the desktop and AppShell's idle preload fetches it, with Settings, 5 s after a web page's start), and `scripts/check-main-bundle.ts` fails a build whose first-screen JavaScript (`web.html`'s entry, `../main` and their static imports) passes `WEB_FIRST_SCREEN_JS_BYTES` (3.27 MB raw; 3.255 MB on 19 Oct 2026) or holds the panel views.
  - The manifest sets no `orientation` (pinned by `manifest.test.ts`): Chromium's WebAPK maps `"any"` (and the sensor values) to a full-sensor lock that overrides the user's rotation lock, and `natural`/`portrait` would stop tablets rotating, so the system setting decides; never call `screen.orientation.lock`. An installed app picks up a manifest change only after Chrome's WebAPK update check (up to about a day) or a reinstall.
  - Installing it (`install.ts` pure decision, `install-ui.ts` hook + action, `install-hint.ts`, `host-ui/InstallSheet.tsx`; all en/ko under `web.install.*`): `installGuidance(env, {promptAvailable, installed})` is `installed` (standalone via `display-mode: standalone` / iOS `navigator.standalone`, or `appinstalled` in this tab), else `ios-share` (every iOS/iPadOS browser: Share › Add to Home Screen), `prompt` (Chromium kept `beforeinstallprompt`, used once), `samsung-menu` / `firefox-menu` (Android menu steps) or `none` (a desktop browser that cannot install). The entry points show only when `installOffered`: the Help menu's "Install app" item in the sidebar footer (`useHelpItems`), Settings › Mobile web on the page (`WebNotificationsSection`, this device) and one quiet toast on the second visit or later (`recordVisit` counts one visit per browser session in `localStorage`, `showInstallHintIfDue` marks it shown before showing, so it never comes back, and waits 6 s for the prompt event). `startInstall()` calls `prompt()` or pushes the steps sheet (`HostPrompt` kind `install`). The manifest (`id`/`start_url`/`scope` `/`, standalone, 192/512/maskable icons, narrow + wide `screenshots/` for Chrome's richer install dialog, a New session shortcut `/#/open?new=1`) and `web.html`'s iOS metas are pinned by `manifest.test.ts`; `STATIC_DIRS` in `server.ts` serves `/screenshots/`. Static files and the manifest need only the serve walls, never the device cookie (the browser fetches the manifest without credentials). An installed app starts with an empty storage jar on iOS (its `__Host-` cookie and device key are not Safari's): `/` → `/api/session` 401 → `/pair` re-pairs silently through `POST /api/pair` with the Tailscale identity, no dead end. `e2e/pwa-install.e2e.ts` (Chromium Pixel 7 + WebKit iPhone 15 behind the fake serve; captures in `/tmp/pwa-install/`) asserts the manifest, the worker controlling the page, no CDP `Page.getInstallabilityErrors`, the item calling `prompt()`, the silent standalone first launch and the iOS steps; `OMP_GUI_PWA_SCREENSHOTS=1` rewrites `public/screenshots/*.png` from that run.
- **No loading screens** (the user's rule: nothing waits on a spinner or a blank window; the app appears at once and fills in; measured by `e2e/no-loading.e2e.ts`, numbers in the last bullet of this group). Each rule with its owner:
  - **Last known state** (`lib/snapshot-cache.ts`, localStorage on both surfaces, because the first React render reads it synchronously; the desktop's lives in userData's profile, the phone's in its origin): one key per kind × namespace × key (`omp.snap.<SNAPSHOT_VERSION>.…`; a version bump needs no migration, the old generation is never read and `pruneStale` deletes it). Namespace = whose data: `local` or `device:<id>` of a paired host, so a host's snapshot never paints as this computer's (`clearNamespace`, `clearAll` on an unpair). Bounded: an entry ≤ `ENTRY_MAX_CHARS`, each kind keeps its newest `KIND_LIMITS`, the whole cache ≤ `TOTAL_MAX_CHARS`, least recently written out first, a full storage evicts once and retries; a snapshot older than 30 days is not read. **Private**: `scrub` runs on every write (keys naming a credential dropped, token-, JWT-, PEM- and bearer-shaped strings replaced, inline picture bytes dropped); nothing the cache holds is a secret, and a setting's value (which can be one) is held in memory only (`settings-data.tsx` `lastRead`). Never authoritative: a reader paints it and the live read replaces it in place.
  - **What is cached** (kind → reader): `sessions` the sidebar index, newest 150 rows (`stores/session-list.ts`, which also keeps its rows when the last holder lets go: the next mount paints them and reads again, the skeleton shows only with nothing known); `transcript` a session's newest ≤ 120 committed rows (`lib/transcript-snapshot.ts`, key = the session file, a file the tab is about to open, or the id): `installTranscriptSeeding` (App) seeds every listed tab the moment its file is known, before its omp is ready, rebuilds the tool cards from the rows, and marks the store `restoredAt`; `commitHydration` then replaces the seed wholesale (a seed is a guess, never "loaded history": no `prevCursor`, rows with the same content keep their objects so nothing re-renders or moves); `models` the catalog and each session's selection (`lib/model-snapshot.ts`: the picker opens on its list, the composer chip names the model; the catalog generation is never restored, and `resetSessionSurface` keeps the catalog); `usage` the last reports per host (`hooks/use-usage-reports.ts`); `panel` a page's last read (`lib/last-read.ts`: Stats per query, ai-memory). Writes are idle and coalesced (a stream appends a row every few ms), never on the render path.
  - **Still, quiet cues, never a spinner for content**: a stale item shows the `omp-updating-hint` note (text over the top edge, hidden for its first 0.7 s, one opacity fade in, nothing running after it, still under software compositing); older history streams in above through `TranscriptHistoryStatus` (the same still note, out of the flow, the viewport anchored as before); a first visit shows a static skeleton shaped like the content (`TranscriptSkeleton` for a session file with no snapshot, `PageFrameSkeleton`, `SkeletonRows`), **no shimmer** (`.omp-skeleton` is a flat fill: the sweep repainted every frame, `background-position` is no compositor property). A connecting or reconnecting session is a hollow still dot in the title bar (`StatusTone` `pending`), not a spinner; a spinner turns only for work that is happening (a running turn, a `Button`'s own action, a row's save or toggle: `<Spinner spin>`); `Spinner` is a still hollow ring by default (`Spinner.test.tsx`), so a content wait that has no better form (Terminal starting, Preview loading, a dialog's probe) is a still frame, and `IconButton loading` (a panel's auto refresh) is still too. Anything with a shape is a snapshot or a static skeleton instead (below). `AsyncSection`'s first load is `SkeletonRows`; and under `html[data-gpu="software"]` even `spin` holds still. A new, empty tab (`status` `starting`, no session file) shows the empty canvas at once; a tab opening an existing file shows the skeleton only until its rows come.
  - **Preload before the click** (`AppShell.tsx`): Settings, Agents and Insights load in an idle moment (≤ 3 s after start; a paired web page waits `WEB_PRELOAD_DELAY_MS` first) and also on intent (pointer over, focus or touch start on a sidebar destination, the panel toggle or a rail pin: `preloadForIntent`), and are rendered from `loadedPages` directly, never through `lazy()` (its ~300 ms Suspense throttle); the panel's views follow the same intent. The first-screen budget in `check-main-bundle.ts` is unchanged (nothing here is in the first screen's JS).
  - **Transitions** (`lib/view-transition.ts`): a page and a Settings section that replace another cross-fade through the View Transitions API (`::view-transition-*(root)` at `--sc-duration-fast`, opacity only) and otherwise change at once: reduced motion, `html[data-gpu="software"]`, a hidden document, no API, a swap inside a running transition. The side panel's views are not transitioned (the document is not tappable while a transition runs, and a panel opens to be used at once); a page's content fades in once (`omp-page-in`).
  - **Panels and pages paint their last read** (`lib/last-read.ts`, kind `panel`, per host; keys name the folder, never another repository's rows; `lib/focused-scope.ts` is the focused tab's `{device, cwd}`): `useGithubLoad` (`lib/github.ts`: every GitHub list and detail of the Agents page, but not a run's log), the PR Center (`stores/pr-center.ts`: `enter(scope)` seeds repo and list before the first paint, a selected PR, its comments and diffs; a slower answer for an earlier PR never replaces the one shown), Changes (`scm:changes`, the open diff in memory only), Commit history (`scm:log`, a commit and its diff in memory only), the branch picker, the agent editor's authoring data, Files (the tree per folder, memory plus the snapshot up to 1500 entries; an opened text file ≤ 100k characters in memory: `viewers/use-file-read.ts`). A first visit is a static shape: `PrDetail`'s skeleton, `DiffSkeleton` (`scm/DiffLines.tsx`), `ViewerLoading`, `SkeletonRows` for a tree, a list, Jobs, a dialog's list; a diagram that is rendering is a flat box inside its `Figure`. Spinners that remain are still rings (Terminal, Preview, a dialog's probe) or action feedback.
  - **The tab strip is a snapshot too** (`lib/tab-snapshot.ts`, kind `tabs`, one entry per window `?slot=` that main gives each window in creation order, `web` for a phone; written 500 ms after the strip last changed, never from ghosts): `useTabsStore` starts from it, so the first frame has the strip, the open tab's title, the sidebar's selected row (a row is current by the open tab's session file even before the session store has an id) and, through `installTranscriptSeeding` (called in `main.tsx`, before the first render), the open tab's last rows. Those tabs are **ghosts** (`SessionTab.ghost`): their ids are last run's, nothing runs behind them; `reconcileTabs` adopts each ghost into the real tab of the same session file (a blank one takes the next blank ghost; same place, same selection) and drops the rest with their runtimes, `switchTab` and `closeTab` ignore a ghost.
  - **A ghost tab** (the snapshot painted before `tabs.list` answers) is replaced by a real tab with a new id and a fresh runtime, so anything the user can already type into it (the composer draft and pictures) is carried to the real tab's runtime when `takeGhost` adopts it (`carryGhostComposer` in stores/tabs.ts); new per-tab state the first frame lets the user edit needs the same carry plus a test in tabs.test.tsx.
  - **A new session is usable before its omp is ready** (`stores/tab-ready-gate.ts`, installed by `tabs.ts`): the tab appears at once (`openTab`; `status` `starting`), the composer takes the draft and the send (`use-composer-submit.ts` accepts `starting`), and `tab-runtime.ts`'s `command` holds every command of a starting tab until the tab list says it is no longer starting (≤ 120 s), then sends it. Nothing shows the wait (the sent bubble is the normal one); if the spawn fails the command fails as it always did and the composer restores the draft. A `restarting` tab is still refused.
  - **The first frame is the layout** (`scripts/boot-shell.ts` → `index.html`/`web.html`; `pre-paint.js` sets what it reads): a flat skeleton of the sidebar, the top bar, the transcript with the small π and the composer box, built from the app's own classes (`omp-session-sidebar`, `omp-topbar`, `omp-composer-box`, …) so the bundle's render-blocking CSS gives it the real geometry; React's first render replaces it with nothing moving (`bootShift` in the probe: ≤ 2 px; the composer box height is `COMPOSER_BOX_PX` in `src/shared/boot-shell-geometry.ts`, measured against the React box: change the composer's height and the e2e names the new number). The sidebar's width is mirrored to localStorage (`lib/sidebar-width.ts`, read by `pre-paint.js` into `--boot-sidebar-w`; the sidebar starts at it), the saved scheme is `pre-paint.js`'s as before, and in the desktop app it sets `data-platform` so the traffic-light room is there on frame one. The web variant has no sidebar below 1000 px and the phone's short bar and one-row composer, from media queries (`html[data-shell]` is not set yet).
  - **Tests**: `snapshot-cache.test.ts` (versioning, bounds, eviction, namespaces, privacy), `transcript-snapshot.test.ts`, `model-snapshot.test.ts`, `stores/session-list.test.ts`, `view-transition.test.ts`, `tab-snapshot.test.ts`, `sidebar-width.test.ts`, `lib/github.test.tsx`, `stores/pr-center.test.ts`, the ghost and ready-gate cases of `stores/tabs.test.tsx`; `e2e/no-loading.e2e.ts` asserts, frame by frame (`e2e/loading-probe.ts`), that a warm desktop start, a tab switch, a warm phone load (4× CPU, 150 ms link), the PR and GitHub centers, Changes, Commit history, Files, Terminal, Preview and Quick open (each opened twice) show no spinner, no blank window and no empty transcript, and move the layout ≤ 0.05 (CLS); that the boot skeleton's boxes equal the React layout's (≤ 2 px, desktop and phone), that the title and the selected row are on the first React frame, and that a new session's composer takes a message sent at once, which reaches the omp when it is ready (the session file holds it); with `OMP_GUI_LOADING_PERF=<dir>` (and `OMP_GUI_LOADING_SUBJECTS=before=…,after=…`) it also writes the numbers and the frame strips (`<dir>/strips/<subject>/<scenario>.png`). A new surface that fetches on open follows these rules, and a test that mounts a store holding module state calls its `reset…ForTest` (`resetSnapshotsForTest`, `resetSessionListFeeds`, `resetLastReadForTest`).
  - **Numbers** (production build, median of 3, fake omp booting in 1.5 s, `OMP_GUI_LOADING_PERF`; pass 1 = commit `7747cea0`, pass 2 = this work; strips in `/tmp/no-loading/strips/{before,after,pass1,pass2}`): first frame is the layout at 79 ms on the desktop and 166 ms (warm) / 324 ms (cold) on the phone, where it was the brand mark until the React layout at 158 / 417 / 1472 ms; the open session is marked in the sidebar on the first React frame (164 ms; 1758 ms before); a warm phone load's first transcript row 1150 → 416 ms and its composer answering 1255 → 586 ms (cold: row 1954 → 1491 ms, composer 2037 → 1615 ms); a warm desktop start's first row 183 → 164 ms; boot skeleton vs React layout ≤ 0.25 px (desktop), ≤ 1.14 px (phone); the PR and GitHub centers, Changes, history, Files, Terminal, Preview and Quick open, each opened twice: spinner time 3270 → 0 ms; a new session: composer usable in ~41 ms and a message sent at once shown as sent in 19 ms (it did not send before this work: the toast said connecting); CLS ≤ 0.001 everywhere; tab switch and reconnect: no spinner, blank or empty-transcript frame in either pass.
- **Bridge** (`resources/gui-bridge/`): an omp extension loaded with `-e`. Area modules export `methods` maps; `env.session()` is the live `AgentSession`, `env.pi.pi` the stock package index. Bare `@oh-my-pi/pi-coding-agent/...` subpath imports resolve through omp's own resolver. These are **internal omp APIs without semver** — keep methods small, feature-detect, and throw clear errors. A feature's pure rules (`<name>-core.ts`), runtime wiring (`<name>-engine.ts`), per-session memory (`<name>-state.ts`) and dependency-free contracts main and the renderer import (`<name>-contracts.ts`) sit in the folder of the module that registers the feature: `modes/` beside `modes/<name>.ts` (auto mode, auto title, delegation, prompt suggestions, output style, profile, goal time, visuals), `extensions/`, `stock-helper/`, or the root beside a root area module (`session-recap.ts`, `reset-passes.ts`, …; `timer-core.ts` serves two root modules).
- **Compatibility self-check**: every omp internal a bridge module uses is listed per GUI feature in `resources/gui-bridge/capabilities.ts` (`REQUIREMENTS`); add the new internal there (and its module to `LOADERS` in `health.ts`, literal specifier) whenever a bridge method starts using one. `index.ts` loads each area module separately, `guard(feature, methods)` refuses a feature's methods with `unsupported_omp` while anything it needs is missing, and the `capabilities` method reports them; `src/main/bridge-health.ts` probes once per omp version per launch and the renderer toasts the result. `OMP_GUI_BRIDGE_PROBE_HIDE=<label>,…` (labels as `capabilities` prints them, e.g. `AgentSession.setPlanModeState`) makes the probe treat those internals as missing, to rehearse a broken omp.
- Settings layout (`settings-catalog.ts` `SETTINGS_NAV`, one name per section in en/ko and the native menu): App — General (language, notifications, profile, window, Sidebar shortcuts, GitHub account; proxy under Advanced), Appearance & input, Keyboard shortcuts; Agent — Models & accounts, Agent behavior, Tools & extensions, Permissions & security, Projects & launch, Schedules; System — Devices & connections, Updates & about (versions plus This computer, the machine setup list), Other settings. Each section shows its curated rows first; rare or raw stock rows (non-curated schema rows, launch profile, reset passes, account pools, model overrides, the proxy) fold under one closed `SettingsAdvanced` disclosure at its end, and `SettingsPage` opens it before scrolling to a search hit or deep link inside — so every row stays reachable and searchable. Models & accounts reads top to bottom as connection, then models: Providers & accounts (accounts, live quota, rotation), Custom endpoints, then Model (the setup assistant, Model roles, Defaults, Compare models). "This device" / "Selected host" scope lines show only while the focused tab runs on a paired host. Sidebar shortcuts (prefs `sidebar.railPins`) are off by default (that includes the footer's Agents and Stats entries, reachable through the palette, the menu, ⌥A and Settings; Settings and the help menu always stay) except the usage block (on until a saved `railPins` array says otherwise; it lists one line per subscription account with usage windows and renders nothing for API-key-only users); Help › Customize sidebar… opens their switches. Older paths below ("Models & providers", "General › This computer", "Settings › SSH") mean these sections.
- Reply language: Settings › General › Conversation language stores prefs `replyLanguage` (`auto` default | a curated English name | `off`; `src/shared/reply-language.ts`). `auto` is the device language's English name (first usable `app.getPreferredSystemLanguages()` entry, then `app.getLocale()`; not the UI language). Each spawn passes the result as `OMP_GUI_REPLY_LANGUAGE`, and a pref change (or a bridge connect) pushes it to every live local sidecar and every tab this machine hosts through the bridge's `reply_language_set {language: string | null}`; a paired host's tabs follow the host's own pref. The bridge's `modes/reply-language.ts` keeps the value as module state and appends one instruction through `before_agent_start` from the next turn (nothing when off, unset or English, so the prompt stays stock); `currentReplyLanguage()` / `onReplyLanguageChange()` hand it to the session titles (see Automatic session titles). The same section has a second line for the labels a person sees, naming the same resolved language (never hardcoded): todo items and phase names, and a `Title: <short sentence>` first line in each `task` assignment. Stock's task tool has no `description` field and a subagent's stock title (`generateTaskLabel`, `task/label.ts`: tiny model, fixed English-style prompt, no hook, no extra call possible) is always English, so the GUI shows that `Title:` line instead (`assignmentTitle` in `modes/subagent-title-contracts.ts`, applied in `subagentPrimaryLabel` for the sidebar rows, the Subagent panel header and the work board, and in `stores/subagents.ts` / `work-run.ts` for the work map; no line → stock's label as before). Thinking, the rest of every assignment, tool arguments, code and file contents stay as the model writes them (Korean costs more tokens). Pinned by `bridge-reply-language.test.ts`, `subagent-graph.test.ts` and the opt-in `e2e/real-subagent-titles.e2e.ts` (Korean and Japanese). Never inject GUI text with `--append-system-prompt`: it collides with the launch profile's flag and turns off omp's fork prompt-cache key.
- Compact settings rows (`settings/SettingsLayout.tsx`): `SettingsRow`/`SettingsSwitchRow` are one dense line (`--sc-control-md` high) — optional `icon` (a lucide glyph, or `ProviderMark` for a provider, model or vendor CLI; decorative, `data-setting-icon`), label, a muted `description` and the control at the right. A string description up to `INLINE_DESCRIPTION_MAX` (56) characters reads inline; a longer one moves behind a `SettingInfo` glyph (tooltip on hover/focus, the text stays in the DOM as `sr-only`, `data-setting-info`); a node description (a cost, a warning) stays a visible small muted note. Group titles are small, the same split applies to a titled `SettingsSection` description, and collections (accounts, CLIs, devices, schedules, MCP servers, shortcuts) are dense list rows with their secondary actions behind an overflow `MenuButton`. New rows reuse these, never a boxed card or a full-width description paragraph. `e2e/settings-captures.e2e.ts` (`OMPDESK_SETTINGS_SHOTS=<dir>`) captures every section in light/dark × en/ko, wide and narrow.
- Compact rows outside Settings (the Mode popover, dialogs, run settings): `SettingInfo` and the inline/tooltip length rule (`INLINE_DESCRIPTION_MAX`, `splitDescription`) live in `common/SettingInfo.tsx`, shared by `SettingsRow` and `common/Switch.tsx`'s `SwitchField`, which is the same one dense line (label, a short clause inline or the (i) glyph, the switch at the right; the glyph sits outside the `<label>`, so the switch is named by the label alone; a node description stays a visible note). So a boolean row never stacks a paragraph under its label anywhere. The composer's Mode popover (`layout/composer/ComposerModes.tsx`, `modes/ModeConfig.tsx`, `modes/AutopilotConfig.tsx`) is built from `SettingsRow`s too (`compactControl`, a fixed-width `Select`/`Input`/segmented `Tabs` at the right, the explanation behind `SettingInfo` in the row's `badge`, `FocusChips compact` wrapping within two rows, a one-line `GrowArea` that opens on its first focus and never collapses on blur, the release-procedure check a glyph with the sentence as tooltip and name, one short warning line beside the Start button, no footer line) and scrolls inside `max-h-[min(36rem,calc(100dvh-6rem))]`. Nothing in the popover may change its layout on a blur: a press blurs the focused field at mousedown, so a reflow then moves the control from under the pointer and its click never fires (v0.35.0: typing in the guide or backlog box and clicking Start did nothing; `ComposerModes.autopilot.test.tsx`, the guide test of `e2e/autopilot-cycles.e2e.ts`). `e2e/mode-captures.e2e.ts` (`OMPDESK_MODE_SHOTS=<dir>`) captures its Plan, Goal and Autopilot tabs in light/dark × en/ko and in a short window; `OMPDESK_SETTINGS_SECTIONS`/`OMPDESK_SETTINGS_SIZES` narrow the Settings captures.
- Figure headers (`chat/FigureHeader.tsx`, `chat/Figure.tsx`): a box that has a title and actions (code block, mermaid/svg figure, a write's figure in its tool card, the Files viewer's mode switch) wears ONE header row on its top edge, `--sc-control-sm` high: the kind or file name on the left, lucide icon buttons on the right (`justify-between`; Preview/Code as two `aria-pressed` icon buttons through `FigureModeToggle`, then wrap, copy, download, enlarge), every icon with the tooltip + `aria-label` of `IconButton`. Never a separate text-tab row above such a box; `Figure` (`data-figure="mermaid|svg"`) owns the box and the Preview/Code state (controlled with `mode`/`onModeChange`), `CodeBlock` takes `title`, `headerActions`, `endActions`, `bare` so the code view wears the same header. Tests: `chat/Figure.test.tsx`, `e2e/figure-header.e2e.ts` (captures with `OMP_GUI_FIGURE_HEADER=<dir>`).
- Settings, centers and pickers on a phone (web build only; an Electron window keeps the desktop layout byte for byte): `common/MasterDetail.tsx` is the one list-beside-detail primitive (`MasterDetail`, plus `useMasterDetail` and `MasterDetailBack` for a page that lays out its own list). Phone (`useShellMode() === "phone"`): one pane at a time, the list, then the chosen row's detail under a back bar; the system back gesture takes a back layer while the detail is open and does what the bar does. Tablet and desktop keep two columns. `SettingsPage` uses the hook (`data-stacked` on `.settings-page`: the section list is its own page, search results replace it, a deep link opens the detail), and the GitHub center sections and the PR center use `MasterDetail` (the PR store's `deselect` is the back). On a phone `SettingsRow` stacks label and description above a full-width control (`[data-setting-desc]` wraps) unless it is `compactControl` (a switch row, or a row whose control is one button, such as the device's push "Turn on" and the install button: the control stays at the right of its label), switches and `SettingInfo` glyphs are small with a 44 px hit area, and `ListRow` carries `omp-list-row` (40 px on touch). `e2e/mobile-pages.e2e.ts` covers this on the dev harness in Chromium and WebKit (`OMP_GUI_MOBILE_PAGES=<dir>` writes captures; `harness.html?rail=all` pins Agents and Stats).
- Phone and tablet scale (`styles/mobile.css`; web build only): a tablet and plain touch do NOT resize controls or type (`--sc-control-*` 28/32/36, `--sc-icon-*` 14/16/20, `--sc-font-*` body 15, transcript 15, captions 12–13 stay the desktop's), but a **phone is denser than the desktop** (`html[data-shell="phone"]`, the "Phone density" block of `mobile.css`, the only place that overrides those tokens: `--phone-type-scale` 0.9333, which `applyFontSize` in `lib/theme.ts` multiplies into `--gui-font-size` and the body size so the type preference reads 14 px instead of 15 on a phone and still scales; `--sc-font-2xs` 12, `--sc-font-xs` 12, `-sm` 12.5, `--sc-control-sm/md/lg` 24/28/32, `--sc-hit-min` 24, `--sc-icon-lg` 18, `--sc-row-touch` 36). Targets measured at 390×844 on the harness: transcript and composer gutters 12 px (`--omp-column-pad`), header bar 40 px, dock cards one 32 px line, work chip 28 px, work-card head ≈ 32 px, composer an always-open box (a 16 px field row over one 32 px controls row, ≈ 77 px; see "Phone composer"), a turn boundary ≈ 24 px, side-panel title row 36 px and tab bar 40 px, so header + work chip + dock + composer take ≤ 19.4 % of the screen height while idle: measured 18.4 % (Chromium) / 18.5 % (WebKit) without suggestions and 22.6 % with them (the always-open composer, which the user prefers over a lower share). A new phone rule goes in that block, gated on `html[data-shell="phone"]`, and never changes a ≥ 1200 px or tablet layout. Fields stay 16 px (iOS zooms into anything smaller) and sheets' items `--sc-row-action` (44). The 44 px rule is a hit area only: under `html[data-input="touch"]` every `button`, `[role=button|switch|tab|checkbox]`, `summary`, `[data-setting-info]` and checkbox label gets an invisible `::after` reaching `--sc-touch-target` (a `@layer base` rule, so an element's own `::after` or a positioning utility still wins, and an element with `after:`/`before:` utilities is left alone). A hit area never covers a neighbour (the responsive audit's `stolen-tap`): a container of close controls sets `--hit-gap-x` / `--hit-gap-y` to the gap between them and the reach stops at half of it (title bar icons sit 14 px apart, ≈ 40 px hit; composer toolbar, dock header, work chip, sidebar and the panel top bar declare theirs); unset, the reach is the full 44 px. The bound counts from the control's own edge, so it must not be tighter than the room around the control: a compact settings row resets it (`--hit-gap-y: initial`; a control centred in its 36 px row between 4 px paddings reaches exactly the row's edge, where the stacked rows' 8 px bound left switches 32 px with 12 px between rows that no tap reached), and a scroller clips every reach to its padding box, so the terminal key bar's key scroller holds the bar's padding and its keys reach down to the panel tab bar's hit area. Never lift a switch, chip, ⋯ or icon button to 44 px visually; a field has no pseudo-element and is drawn 36 px. Pinned by `styles/touch-targets.test.ts` and, for the hit area, `hitSizes()` in `e2e/harness.ts`; the harness page takes `?stress=1` (long URL, wide table, long code line, diff, mermaid, long tool paths) for the responsive audit.
- Per-device appearance on a paired web page (`src/shared/device-prefs.ts`, routed by `src/renderer/web/shim.ts`; the desktop window is unchanged): the theme (`theme`, `themeName`, `followAgentTheme`, `colorBlindMode`), text and density (`fontSize`, `compactDensity`), how a conversation renders (`transcriptDetail`, `thinkingExpanded`, `codeLineNumbers`, `workBoard`, every `displayPreferences.*`) and the layout (`sidebarWidth`, `gui.panelWidth`, `panelSide`, `defaultPanelTab`) belong to the device (`DEVICE_LOCAL_PREF_KEYS`, `isDeviceLocalPref`), kept in its own `WebDevice.uiPrefs` record on the host (`web:device.uiPrefs.get/set`, `WEB_DEVICE_CHANNELS` in `shared/web-host.ts`, permission `device-self`, 64 KiB). Agent behaviour, models, accounts, notification policy, the keymap, the sidebar's grouping/pins/archive and every other key stay the host's. The shim overrides `PREFS_GET`/`PREFS_SET`: a whole-store read is the host's prefs with the device-local keys removed and the device's values overlaid (`overlayDevicePrefs`; dotted keys nest as the host's store nests them, a keyed read of a local key reads only the record), a write of a local key goes only to `uiPrefs.set` (serialized, each from the latest copy, a refused write rejects so `saveGuiPreference` toasts) and never to the host's prefs, so it cannot reach the desktop windows or `nativeTheme`, and `PREFS_CHANGED` for a local key is dropped on the page. A phone with nothing set therefore follows the system theme (`getPersistedThemeSelection` falls back to `system`) and the stock text size; phone sizes come from CSS (`mobile.css`), there is no phone-only size pref. A new appearance or layout pref that should differ per device is added to `DEVICE_LOCAL_PREF_KEYS` (and the list in `device-prefs.test.ts`); Settings › Appearance shows the "This device" note (`settings.appearance.thisDevice*`) on the web page only. Pinned by `shared/device-prefs.test.ts`, `web/device-prefs.test.ts` and `e2e/device-prefs.e2e.ts` (the phone sets dark, the computer stays light, across a reload and a relaunch).
- Sidebar memory (the left side keeps what was left open or closed across a relaunch, a reload and a reopened window; device-local prefs `sidebarVisible`, `sidebarFolds`, `sidebarFamilies` in `DEVICE_LOCAL_PREF_KEYS`, so a paired web page keeps them per device and the desktop per profile; code in `lib/sidebar-memory.ts`, `stores/sidebar-folds.ts`, `stores/sidebar-subagents.ts`, `stores/ui.ts`). Each pref has a localStorage mirror (`omp.sidebarVisible`, `omp.sidebarFolds`, `omp.sidebarFamilies`) read synchronously by the first render, like the width; the pref is read again after mount (`hydrate`, App's boot read for `sidebarVisible`) and wins when it differs, unless the user already edited. **Shown/hidden**: `useUiStore.sidebarVisible` starts from the mirror and every change is saved by a `useUiStore` subscription, except while the shell is a drawer (phone, portrait tablet: `AppShell` `useDrawerSidebarSync` calls `setSidebarDrawerMode`, the flag is then the drawer's open state, never saved, and the drawer always starts closed; the docked state returns, and is saved again, when the window docks). `pre-paint.js` sets `data-boot-sidebar="hidden"` on `<html>` from `omp.sidebarVisible`, and the boot shell (`scripts/boot-shell.ts`) drops its sidebar and clears the traffic lights, so the first frame has no open sidebar to flash. **Folds** (`sidebarFolds`: `groups` project / folder / worktree group `cwd` → folded, `devices` `local` and each paired host's deviceId, `deviceGroups` a host's folders as `deviceId\ncwd`, `lane` Code/Chat, `archivedOpen`, `starterOpen` Get started, `usageCollapsed` the footer usage block): "This device" (shown once a host is paired) and the usage block fold like the rest; the remembered lane beats the open session's lane at launch and the session wins from its next change on; a filter still shows every match. **Families** (`sidebarFamilies`: the session paths whose family rows are open or hidden): a remembered open family keeps its entry until children have been seen and gone (`FamilyGroup`'s `hadChildren`), never at launch while they have not reported yet;
