a live tab's `tab:` key is never saved. **Bounded**: each record keeps its newest `FOLD_LIMIT` (50) entries and keys ≤ 200 characters (a paired device's whole `uiPrefs` record is capped at 64 KiB: a write over it is refused for every pref in it), and the sidebar prunes entries of folders, hosts and sessions that no longer exist (`pruneGroups`, `pruneDevices`, `pruneFamilies`) only once a live read answered (`useSessionList().live`, never the last-known snapshot, which holds the newest 150 rows) and the host list has loaded. Tests: `sidebar-memory.test.ts`, `sidebar-folds.test.ts`, `ui-sidebar-visible.test.ts`, "Sidebar remembered folds" in `Sidebar.test.tsx`, `device-prefs.test.ts`; `e2e/sidebar-memory.e2e.ts` collapses the sidebar and folds two projects, relaunches and reads the boot shell's and the app's first frame. A test that mounts the sidebar resets `useSidebarFolds` in `afterEach`.
- Themes (18 Oct 2026; replaced eleven near-identical tints of one idea): ten distinct themes, five light and five dark. Ids, groups, the scheme of each and the retired-id table are the dependency-free `src/shared/theme-names.ts` (`LIGHT_THEME_NAMES`: `light` OMPdesk Light, `github-light`, `solarized-light`, `sepia`, `hc-light`; `DARK_THEME_NAMES`: `dark` OMPdesk Dark, `nord`, `tokyo-night`, `gruvbox-dark`, `hc-dark`; `LEGACY_THEME_NAMES` maps titanium/nord/latte/gruvbox/paper/solarized/dawn/frost/matcha to their nearest successor of the same scheme, by CIE Lab distance of page and accent). `light` and `dark` are written token by token in `lib/themes.ts` because `theme-light.css` / `theme-dark.css` must match them (`styles/tokens.test.ts`); every other theme is one compact `ThemePalette` in `lib/theme-palettes.ts` and `lib/theme-builder.ts` derives all 119 `--omp-*` tokens from it, plus the terminal's **ANSI 16**, six **chart series** and the **focus ring** (`ThemeDefinition.ansi/chart/focusRing`; `themeExtrasFor` writes `--omp-ansi-*`, `--omp-chart-1…6`, `--sc-focus-ring` inline next to the token map, `global.css` gives the first two a stylesheet default). Well-known palettes (Solarized, Nord, Tokyo Night, Gruvbox, GitHub Primer; all MIT, credited in `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.txt` section 2 through `scripts/generate-third-party-notices.ts`) start from their published colors; a color that fails AA as text is shaded toward the theme's text color until it passes, so edit a palette, then run `themes.contrast.test.ts`. Never add a theme that is a tint of an existing one: `themes.contrast.test.ts` fails a same-scheme pair whose page, accent and three loudest syntax colors are under ΔE 15 apart, or whose accents are under 15.
  - Contrast is a test, not a review: `lib/theme-contrast.ts` lists every pair (text, secondary, muted, dim, accent/link, success/warning/error/info on six surfaces and on their own washes, syntax and markdown code on the code surface, diff lines on their washes, button and badge labels, text over the selection, the ANSI 16 on the terminal surface) with its floor, 4.5:1 (7:1 for the High Contrast themes), and the 3:1 pairs (`border-strong`, input border, focus ring, accent and chart series as graphics on four surfaces); `themes.contrast.test.ts` fails any theme under a floor, `styles/colorblind.test.ts` holds the color-blind palette (per scheme, `--cb-*` in `global.css`) to 4.5:1 on every theme's surfaces. Run `bunx vitest run src/renderer/lib/themes.contrast.test.ts` after touching a palette.
  - **System** pairs one light and one dark theme (prefs `systemLightTheme` / `systemDarkTheme`, device-local like `themeName`; default `light` / `dark`; a name of the wrong scheme falls back to the default). `applyThemeByName("system")` resolves the OS scheme to the pair's theme and writes its tokens inline (System is no longer stylesheet-driven), `data-theme` carries the scheme and `data-theme-name` the theme shown (the Mermaid look, the e2e and the captures read it); `setSystemThemePair` re-applies when System is selected; `lib/theme.ts`'s watcher re-applies on an OS flip. The picker is `settings/ThemePicker.tsx` (System card with both halves previewed, a light group, a dark group, two selects for the pair; `ThemePreview` draws surface, text, accent and a line of code in the theme's own colors and is shared with `dialogs/ThemePickerDialog.tsx`).
  - Migration: `migrateThemeName` reads any stored value (a retired id gives its successor, an unknown value gives null so a caller falls back to System, never a blank theme). The renderer's `getPersistedThemeSelection` stores the successor the first time it reads a retired id (desktop prefs and a paired device's uiPrefs alike, since both go through `window.omp.prefs`), the main process rewrites `themeName` / `systemLightTheme` / `systemDarkTheme` once in `prefsStore()`, and `nativeThemeSource` (`main/window-chrome.ts`) takes the scheme from the theme id, so a saved window opens in the right scheme before any renderer runs. A host's `themeName` that a paired computer reports goes through the same migration (`use-host-appearance`).
  - Every surface follows the theme through tokens, never a literal: xterm (`TerminalPanel.terminalTheme` reads `--omp-ansi-*` and the selection), highlight.js (`--omp-syntax-*` in `components.css`), diffs (`--omp-diff-*`), Mermaid (`MermaidBlock` builds `themeVariables` for its `base` theme from `--sc-*` and re-renders on `data-theme` / `data-theme-name`), charts (`lib/chart.ts` `--omp-chart-*`), the Windows title-bar overlay and the phone page's `theme-color` metas (`lib/window-chrome.ts` `syncWindowControls` pushes `--sc-bg` / `--sc-text` on Windows and on the web page). Pre-paint: `applyThemeByName` stores `omp.themeScheme` (dark, light or system) and `omp.themePaint` (`{light?, dark?}` page backgrounds: System stores both, a fixed theme its own scheme) and `public/pre-paint.js` paints that background and sets the `theme-color` metas before any module runs; the static `manifest.webmanifest` colors stay the OMPdesk dark. `?theme=<any id>` works on the dev harness. Captures of all ten themes (desktop transcript with code and diff, phone) and a contact sheet were made on the harness under `bun run locked` (the script is throwaway, not kept).
- Profile: Settings › General › Profile stores prefs `userProfile` (`{name, experience}`; contracts and prompt text in dependency-free `resources/gui-bridge/modes/user-profile-contracts.ts`; levels beginner, vibe-coder, junior, expert). It rides the reply-language path: `OMP_GUI_USER_PROFILE` (JSON) at spawn, `user_profile_set` on change and on bridge connect to every live local sidecar and every hosted tab, and `modes/user-profile.ts` appends one `<user-profile>` section through `before_agent_start` (nothing for an empty profile). Stock has no such setting.
- Prompt suggestions (built in, Settings › Agent behavior, prefs `nextPromptSuggestions` default **on** and the sub-switch `nextPromptInline` default on; `resources/gui-bridge/modes/next-prompt-contracts.ts`, rules in `next-prompt-core.ts`, behaviour in `next-prompt-engine.ts` behind `modes/next-prompt.ts`): same plumbing as the reply language (`OMP_GUI_NEXT_PROMPT` JSON at spawn, `next_prompt_set` on change and on bridge connect to every live local sidecar and hosted tab; a paired host's tab follows the host's pref). Stock's `ComposerPredictionController` runs only in its TUI and `runEphemeralTurn` can only use the session model, so the engine calls stock `completeSimple` on the model stock's `smol` role resolves to (`resolveRoleChain` over `roleCandidatePool`, the session's credentials through `modelRegistry.resolver`; reasoning off, ≤ 512 output tokens, one retry without the cap when a transport refuses it) and never falls back to the main model: no model / no credentials / refused / offline become `unavailable` frames, shown as a muted `role=status` line under the composer plus a bridge debug log, never a toast. One request per finished turn (`agent_end`), regenerated (debounced 800 ms, gated by `gateSuggestion`: enabled, idle, main agent, empty composer, a reply exists, state key moved) after a compaction, a finished user `!` command (renderer `refresh_next_prompt`), a session switch and an emptied draft; typing (`set_next_prompt_draft`, reported by `use-idle-compaction-draft.ts`), a new run or the switch going off aborts the request, and an answer whose moment moved while the model answered (new messages, running work that ended or started) is dropped and asked for again. The prompt carries, first, `<user-style>` with one line only, an explicit `register: 반말 …` / `register: 존댓말 …` when most of the user's last 6 typed messages end in one Korean register (`userRegister` over the first lines of `sentTexts`, which leaves out stock's `synthetic` and `attribution: "agent"` user messages, slash commands and `<…>` blocks; `koreanRegister` majority, a tie names none; `koreanRegisterOf`: polite = ends in 요/니다/니까/죠 except nouns like 필요, casual = a listed 해체/해라체/음슴체 last syllable such as 줘, 해, 래, 지, 음, 임, anything else neutral). It carries no excerpt of the user's messages: given six excerpts the small model re-asked them as new requests (reported on v0.32.2: the placeholder "모드 추가할 건 없어? 모델풀은 지워야 할 것 같고" for the sent "모드를 추가할것은 없나? 모델풀은 삭제해야할꺼 같고"); replaying that moment on the real omp, 9 of 16 lines restated a recent message with six excerpts, 1 of 14 with three, 2 of 16 with only short ones, 0 of 16 with none, while the register held. Then the transcript tail (the reply being followed up clipped to 1800 characters, older ones to 700) plus a compact `<app-state>` (last failed tool/command and exit, files changed this turn, open plan/todos, a question answered this turn, the main agent's running subagents and background jobs as `running-now` — stock `getAsyncJobSnapshot`, a subagent with the intent of the `task` call that spawned it —, goal, whether tests ran and failed, the user's `!` command, compaction) read from the live session. Its rules take the voice and follow-up lines of stock's `prompts/system/composer-prediction-user.md` (write as the user, mirror how they phrase requests), ask for exactly 3 different next steps (asked for "up to 3", the cheap model often wrote one, so no chips showed), say each is a message the user sends to the assistant (never the assistant's voice, never addressed to the user, never offering or promising work), write in their voice as their own messages in the conversation show, never repeat, reword or re-ask what they already sent, use the register `<user-style>` names, keep each to 3–8 words within the limits below, and forbid anything already done, started, planned or handed to a subagent. Reported: a placeholder in the assistant's voice ("…알려 주시면 그대로 기록해 둘게요"), chips cut with "…" and everything 존댓말 for a user who writes "배포해줘"; after the voice rules shipped (v0.32.1) the small model still answered that user "롤백해도 되나요?", "알려주세요", "확인해줄래요?", so the register is enforced in code. The cleaner (`cleanCandidateLine`, `cleanCandidates`) enforces it whatever the model writes: a line in the assistant's voice (`isAssistantVoice`, conservative: ko `드릴게요/드릴까요/드리겠습니다`, `둘게요/두겠습니다`, `보신다면/하신다면/원하시면`, `알려 주시면`; en a leading `I'll`, `I will`, `Shall I`, `Would you like`, `Do you want me to`, `Let me` but not `Let me know`/`see`) is dropped whole; then `matchRegister` holds a Korean line to the user's register: for a casual user each sentence's polite end becomes its 반말 form by a fixed list (주세요 → 줘, 되나요 → 돼, 죠 → 지, and 요 after a 해체 syllable: 해요, 줄래요, 할까요, 있나요, 좋네요 …), while any other polite end (니다, 세요, 예요) or a remaining honorific/humble word (주시, 하세, 드리, 제가, 저희 …) drops the line; for a polite user only 반말 ends a noun never has gain their polite form (줘 → 주세요, 돼 → 돼요, ㄹ래/ㄹ까 → +요, 지? → 죠?) and nothing is dropped, so a polite user is never pushed to 반말; no register named leaves the line alone. A line over `MAX_CANDIDATE_CHARS_CJK` = 40 (any Hangul, Han or kana) or `MAX_CANDIDATE_CHARS` = 60 keeps its first sentence when that fits (`fitCandidateLength`) and is dropped otherwise: a closed sentence is a whole request, a cut at a comma or a word would send a different, unfinished one. Last, a line restating a message the user already sent is dropped (`SentMessages.isCopy` over every typed message of the session, `sentTexts`): similarity is the F1 of a weighted longest common subsequence of units (each Hangul/Han/kana character, each Latin/digit run; a text's first 400), each unit weighted by its rarity across the sent messages (inverse document frequency), so shared rare words (모델풀, 대기열) count, shared endings and everyday words (해줘, 끝나면) hardly do, and Korean rewording (particles and endings changed) still matches where n-grams miss it; `COPY_SIMILARITY` 0.6 against the latest message at any length, and against any sent message from `COPY_MIN_UNITS` 14 units on, because short generic requests recur and stay worth suggesting (that user sent "배포해줘" 6 times). On the reported session the two copies scored 0.66 and 0.79, long new requests at most 0.35. The debug log `next-prompt suggestion` carries the `register`. Running work does not suppress suggestions: a delegating session has jobs running at almost every turn end, so suppression would switch the feature off where it is used most; instead the model is told the work is underway and suggests waiting for it, checking or steering it, or something unrelated. The best of up to 3 ranked one-line candidates is the placeholder (Tab/→), the rest are chips (↓ from an empty composer enters them, ←/→, Enter/click fills; Alt+1..3 fills directly; one line each, up to `min(100%, 44em)` wide so a 40-character Korean candidate shows whole, wrapping to a second row rather than squeezing each other, the full text as the tooltip); the per-session memory in `stores/next-prompt.ts` is unchanged (submit or a run clears it, a deleted draft brings it back, so an accepted-then-sent suggestion is simply the submit). Inline completion (`complete_inline` → `next_prompt_inline`, same cheap model, ≤ 512 tokens, `InlineScheduler`): ghost text after the caret for a single-line draft of ≥ 8 characters with the caret at its end, 600 ms after the last keystroke, at most one request start per 2 s (so ≤ 30 a minute, answers cached per draft; `e2e/next-prompt.e2e.ts` scripts 10 typing bursts with pauses in a minute and sees 10 requests), aborted on every keystroke, never during IME composition, with a slash/`@` menu or paste choice open, for drafts starting `/`, `!`, `$`, `->`/`=>`, or a draft stock's `shouldSkipHistory` calls secret; Tab accepts, Esc dismisses. Its request carries the same register line (no excerpts) and its rules ask for the whole message, the draft restated character for character and then continued on the draft's own topic, one piece of the same sentence, never the assistant's words. `cleanInlineReply` takes what follows the restated draft (the draft window `inlineDraftWindow`; trailing spaces of a draft ending in one are not doubled); a reply that does not start with the draft as typed changed the user's text and shows nothing, so the user's text is never rewritten. The ghost must be at most `INLINE_MAX_CHARS_CJK` 20 / `INLINE_MAX_CHARS` 50 characters (longer shows nothing, never cut) with no role label, tag, fence or second sentence; then the assembled message, draft plus ghost, is judged, since a valid fragment ("버튼이" + " 안 바뀌어") is no request on its own: no assistant voice, no leaving the register (an opposite ending or, for a casual user, an honorific/humble word), no restatement of a sent message; each check counts only what the ghost brought, never what the user typed. Reported: "odd continuations". On six real drafts of that session (two runs each) all 12 old continuations were wrong (존댓말 "…확인이 필요해요", the assistant's "확인해 볼게요"/"정리해 둘게요", new sentences, other topics); with the register and checks but the continuation asked for alone, 9 of 12 glued onto the last word ("버튼이" + "이상하게", "모습이" + "가 맞는지"); with the whole message restated 11 of 18 were shown, all spaced, 반말, short and on topic ("…추가지시가 안 먹히는지 봐줄래?", "…모습이 어떤지 봐줘"), the rest no ghost. The debug log `next-prompt inline` records the model, time, whether a ghost was shown and the register, never the text. `next_prompt_state.requests` counts both kinds. The user's own `next-prompt` extension is found through stock's loaded-extension list (`legacyExtension`); while the built-in is on the renderer ignores its widget (its own request cannot be stopped from here), and with the built-in off or unsupported the widget path works as before. Pinned on the real omp by `src/main/bridge-next-prompt-stock.test.ts` (smol resolution, legacy detection, reply cleaning against stock's `parseComposerPrediction`) and, opt-in with `OMP_GUI_REAL_WORK=1`, by `e2e/real-next-prompt.e2e.ts`; the loaders are `config/model-roles`, `config/model-resolver`, `modes/controllers/input-controller` and `pi-ai` in `capabilities.ts` / `health.ts`.
  - Suggestions on touch (`useShellInput() === "touch"`; desktop byte for byte unchanged, the keys above stay): a phone or tablet has no Tab, →, ↓, Alt+1..3 or Esc, so `use-composer-suggestions.ts` builds the chips = the best candidate FIRST, then the runner-ups (at most `MAX_NEXT_PROMPT_CHIPS` 3), and `NextPromptChips` draws them as **one sideways-scrolling row** (`.omp-next-chips[data-touch]` in `mobile.css`: `nowrap`, `overflow-x: auto`, 32 px high, 28 px chips, scrollbar hidden, `--hit-gap-y: 4px` because an overflow container clips the global 44 px reach); on a phone and a touch tablet the row sits just above the composer box (never inside it). Nothing at all with no suggestion, hidden while typing, streaming or with a menu open, like the desktop row. The best candidate is **never also the placeholder** (`placeholderPrompt` is undefined on touch: the placeholder stays the idle hint, so one text is shown once), and there are no `Kbd` hints, `aria-keyshortcuts`, "to accept" chip or tooltip titles on touch. A **tap** fills the field with the text, caret at the end, NOT sent (the same `pickChip` → `placeDraft` as a click); a **long press** (the shell turns it into `contextmenu`) arms the chip and shows a "Send now" / "바로 보내기" button (`input.chips.sendNow`) right after it, which sends that text as it is through `send(text)`; any other pointer-down disarms. **Inline ghost completion is OFF on touch**: `useInlineCompletion` gets `enabled: … && !touchInput`, so no `complete_inline` request is ever made. Reasons: the phone keyboard has its own suggestion strip, IME composition (Korean above all) keeps the draft "composing" so the debounced request is rarely eligible and a ghost under a composing syllable would shift on every keystroke, and no clean accept gesture exists (Tab/→ are missing and a tap on the tail collides with caret placement); the Settings switch `nextPromptInline` is unchanged and still governs the desktop. Pinned by `InputArea.next-prompt-touch.test.tsx` (touch vs mouse: chip tap fills without sending, long press → Send now, first chip not the placeholder, no kbd/Tab hint, no `complete_inline`) and `e2e/mobile-suggestions.e2e.ts` (Pixel 8 emulation on the dev harness, which exposes `window.__stores.nextPrompt`; `OMP_GUI_SUGGEST_TOUCH=<dir>` writes the en/ko light captures).
  - Mojibake from the model. Reported on v0.33.0: the placeholder and a chip read "ì•Œê² ì–´ ê·¸ëŸ¼ ê³„ì† ì§„í–‰í•´" and "ì§€ê¸ˆ ì—ì´ì „íŠ¸ë“¤ …" ("알겠어 그럼 계속 진행해", "지금 에이전트들 …": UTF-8 read as Windows-1252). Nothing in OMPdesk decodes that text: the bridge socket, omp's stdout and IPC are UTF-8 end to end, and that one request (17 Oct 2026, claude-sonnet-5-5 as `smol`) answered 220 output tokens in 5.6 s with 2 candidates where the requests around it took ~65 tokens in ~2.5 s with 3 (the session's `model_usage` entries, omp's `next-prompt suggestion` debug log), so the model itself wrote those characters. `isUtf8Mojibake` (`resources/gui-bridge/mojibake.ts`, dependency-free) flags text with two or more whole UTF-8 sequences spelled in Windows-1252 characters (three- or four-byte ones, or Latin-1 letters led by Â/Ã); real text in a Latin script never forms them ("„Straße“", "café…", "ação" stay clean; `src/main/bridge-mojibake.test.ts`). `cleanCandidateLine` drops such a line, `cleanInlineReply` shows no such ghost, automatic titles keep the current name (`concise` in `auto-title-engine.ts`, the shorter retry too) and the idle recap is neither shown nor journaled (`RecapTimer` before `deliver`). It refuses rather than repairs: a model rarely spells every byte (Windows-1252 has no character for 0x81, 0x8D, 0x8F, 0x90, 0x9D; a no-break space 0xA0 becomes a plain space once whitespace is folded).
- Automatic session titles (built in, Settings › Agent behavior › Session titles, prefs `autoTitle` and the sub-switch `autoTitleEvolve` "keep the title up to date as work changes", both default **on**; dependency-free contracts in `resources/gui-bridge/modes/auto-title-contracts.ts`, rules in `auto-title-core.ts`, behaviour in `auto-title-engine.ts` behind `modes/auto-title.ts`, capability `autoTitle`): same plumbing as the prompt suggestions (`OMP_GUI_AUTO_TITLE` JSON at spawn, `auto_title_set` on change and on bridge connect to every live local sidecar and hosted tab; a paired host's tab follows the host's pref). **Stock machinery only, never the session model for a first title**: `AgentSession.generateTitle` (stock's `generateSessionTitle`, models from the `tiny`, then `commit`, then `smol` role chain; the session model only as stock's own retry fallback after those failed) names the operator's first message (`operatorTitleText`), `renameTitle(title)` adds the card per `title.icons` (OMPdesk holds `boring`, so none and no card request; see Held stock defaults), a message the title model declines (a greeting) is retried from the conversation after the turn with `renameTitle()` (stock's `/rename` without a title), the main agent only (subagents are excluded). **Origin is stock's own record**: `SessionManager.titleSource` and the journal's `title_change` entries (`source`, `trigger`); the one case stock cannot say, "a fresh automatic title over a typed one" (`setSessionName(…,"auto")` is refused over source `user`), is written as source `user` with `trigger: "ompdesk-auto"` (`TITLE_TRIGGER`) and read back by `titleIsAuto` (`auto-title-core.ts`), so nothing lives in a GUI-only file. **Title language, length and form**: stock's `title-system.md` has English-only rules and examples and asks for a 3-6 word imperative phrase, so the title model answered in English and long. The engine sets the session's TITLE_SYSTEM.md slot (`setTitleSystemPrompt`, read by `generateTitle`, `renameTitle` and stock's replan refresh; the card request passes its own prompt) to stock's own `title-system.md` (`ompPrompt` + `prompt.render`, never copied) plus one line (`titleLanguageLine`: the language the user writes in first, and for a non-English reply language (`currentReplyLanguage()`) that language when unclear; a plain noun-phrase summary of the task, 2-4 words (`TITLE_MAX_WORDS`), at most 12 characters in Korean/Chinese/Japanese (`TITLE_MAX_CJK_CHARS`) and 30 otherwise (`TITLE_MAX_CHARS`), spaces included, the subject and head noun kept and modifiers and generic words dropped, with two compressions as examples (`COMPRESSIONS`: "로그인 비밀번호 에러 메시지 버그 수정" → "로그인 오류 수정", "CSV export empty with filters" → "Empty CSV export"), no punctuation, quotes or emoji, identifiers and names as written) — at attach, on `reply_language_set` (`onReplyLanguageChange`) and again before each request (stock's headless `/move` re-resolves the slot). Off, unset or English get the same line without the language fallback (length and form only; the title stays in the user's language). **Over the limits** (`titleFits`), while the line is in effect: one retry, never more, never on the first pass, on a stronger model than the cheap title chain (the user's choice; reported: the cheap chain answered its own retry with the same 13–16-character titles): stock's `default` role (`roleCandidatePool` + `resolveRoleChain`, as the prompt suggestions resolve `smol`), else the session's model, the first with credentials (`modelRegistry.getApiKey`/`resolver`), through stock `completeSimple` with reasoning off, at most 256 output tokens, temperature 0 and no prompt cache (`cacheRetention: "none"`: Anthropic's 1 h cache write doubled the cost of a one-off request). Its system prompt is the slot's prompt plus `titleShorterLine` (the too-long answer named, ≤ 12/30 characters, subject and head noun kept, modifiers and generic tails such as 화면, 메시지, 로직, 기능, 관련, "with …" dropped, all four `COMPRESSIONS` as examples), its message the operator's first message, or the answer itself when stock built the context for `renameTitle()` (✨, evolving); the reply's `<title>` goes through stock `normalizeGeneratedTitle`; counted in `requests`; a failed retry keeps the first answer. The retry wins unless it is longer than the first answer (a tie goes to the retry), and is kept whole up to the hard cap (`TITLE_HARD_CJK_CHARS` 14, `TITLE_HARD_CHARS` 32, any word count); past it `conciseTitle` cuts whole words, never an ellipsis: in Korean/Chinese/Japanese the generic words go first (`CJK_FILLER`: 화면, 메시지, 로직, 기능, 관련, 부분), then the leading words, because the head noun comes last (a cut from the end turned "작업 현황판 화면 디자인" into "작업 현황판 화면"), otherwise the trailing ones, never ending on an English function word such as "with" or "the". Every generated title is logged (`auto-title: title {answer, retry, retryModel, retryUsage, title}` in omp's own log). Measured 16 Oct 2026 (opus-class `default`, haiku-class title chain): all six audit messages went over the limits on the first pass, the retry cost about $0.005 each (≈ 1.2 k input, 15–18 output tokens) and every title came back within 12/30 characters. Under `title.icons: boring` a fresh title drops a card (stock's retitle keeps the current title's card through `keepTitleCard`, so ✨ and evolving on an old carded title come back plain; `cfgTitleIcons`, stock `splitCardTitle`); under another value the card is kept (`formatCardTitle`). Stored titles are never rewritten on their own. A person's own TITLE_SYSTEM.md (any slot value the engine did not set) is their whole title prompt and stays as written, uncut and never retried (`planTitlePrompt`). Setting the slot turns off stock's `title.generator: fork` path, which a GUI session never reaches (`autoTitle` is TUI-only in `main.ts`). **Evolving** (sub-switch): after a terminal `agent_end` while the title is automatic, `EvolveGate` (cheap, no model call: at least 2 terminal turns and 60 s since the title was set or last considered, and the work moved — a file edited/written 1, the todo plan changed 2, the goal changed 3, threshold 3 — or 6 turns regardless) asks stock `renameTitle()` once per window; the result is applied only when it is not a reword of the current title (`isRewordedTitle`: normalized word overlap ≥ 0.6 or character-trigram overlap ≥ 0.55), and every write re-checks the session id, the name and `titleRevision` it started from, so a typed name wins over any request in flight and ends the evolving. Cost: 1 title-model request for the first title (plus 1 for its card when the user set `title.icons` to something other than `boring`, and 1 retry on the `default` model when the answer is over the limits), then at most 1 request per two finished turns and minute (plus its retry), only after files/plan/goal moved or six quiet turns (counted in `auto_title_set`'s `requests`). The editors' ✨ auto button (title bar, rename dialog, sidebar row; `hooks/use-auto-title.ts`, `common/AutoTitleButton.tsx`) sends `regenerate_session_title` → local handler → bridge `regenerate_title` (`renameTitle()` on the live session, saved by the bridge as an automatic title even over a typed name; failure is one inline `role="alert"` sentence, never a toast); a stored session no tab has open has no omp to ask, so its button is aria-disabled with a tooltip (the origin tracking lives in that omp, and a short-lived `omp` per click could neither keep it nor be cancelled); a paired host's tab answers on the host (the command is in `quota-commands.ts`' model list). A changed title eases in (`common/SwapText.tsx`, `.omp-title-swap`; the global reduced-motion rule shortens it). e2e: `e2e/auto-title.e2e.ts` on the fake omp (`OMP_GUI_TEST_AUTO_TITLE`); `e2e/real-auto-title.e2e.ts` (opt-in `OMP_GUI_REAL_WORK=1`, results in `/tmp/real-auto-title.json` with the model's raw answers, the retry's model and its usage read from the private profile's omp log; `realOmpProfile(…, { keepDefaultRole: true })` keeps the user's `default` role and the audit moves its session to `smol` with stock `set_model`, which does not persist) checks four Korean first messages (Korean reply language) and two English ones (reply language off) get short plain titles in their own language, with no card, on the real omp; four of them are the prompt's own compression examples, two are held out. Pinned against stock by `bridge-auto-title-stock.test.ts`.
- Automatic mode choice (Settings › Agent behavior › Smart automation › Auto mode and the "Auto mode" switch in the composer's Mode popover; pref `autoMode`, default **on**, only `false` turns it off; contracts in dependency-free `resources/gui-bridge/modes/auto-mode-contracts.ts`, rules in `auto-mode-core.ts`, per-session memory in `auto-mode-state.ts`, behaviour in `modes/auto-mode.ts`, capability `autoMode`). It decides **only Plan or Goal, or none**, and **uses that mode for the one request it classified, never as the session's mode** (the user's call after it put a busy work session into Plan): the other manual modes are never enabled by it. Stock has no prompt→mode classifier (`plan.defaultOnStartup` is unconditional; `auto-thinking/classifier.ts` picks an effort), so the bridge copies that classifier's approach with its own pieces: stock `judgment` (`resolveJudge` over the `judge` role chain, which starts at `@tiny`, then `@smol`, then `@default`; one choice question answered in a few tokens, `sharedJudgmentCache`, `journalJudgmentUsage` with purpose `auto-mode` so session totals count it) and `preprocessTinyMessage` for the input; the judge's state also carries the previous exchange (`previousExchange`: the last user message and reply, 400 characters each), and its instructions say none for anything that continues, answers or corrects that work. No env or sync plumbing: the composer reads the pref at send time (`lib/auto-mode-client.ts` `runAutoMode`, called from `planComposerSubmit`'s `beforePrompt` for a plain prompt only, never steer/follow-up/slash) and calls `auto_mode_decide` (a model command in `remote/quota-commands.ts`; a paired host's tab runs it on the host, with the host's quota). The bridge method decides and changes nothing; main (`local/auto-mode.ts`) uses it through stock's own commands: bridge `modes.set_plan_mode` with `auto: true` for Plan (`auto-mode-state.ts` `autoPlan` marks it), stock `goal create` with the prompt text as objective for Goal. On an idle session stock's `goal create` admits the goal's own turn at once (`rpc-goal.ts` `#scheduleContinuation`), ahead of the prompt, and stock refuses a plain prompt while a turn runs (`AgentBusyError`), so after a Goal decision `planComposerSubmit` sends the prompt with `streamingBehavior: "steer"`: it joins that turn, or starts one when none runs yet (sent plain it was refused and the composer put it back as "Message not sent"). **One request only**: an automatic plan ends when its plan is approved (stock's approval exits plan mode), rejected without feedback (`plan.ts` `approve` exits plan mode when `autoPlan` and answers `planModeEnabled: false`, which `PlanApprovalDialog` applies; feedback is a reply and re-plans), or the user sends a plain prompt while no proposal awaits approval (`runAutoMode` first sends `auto_mode_end`, which main turns into `modes.set_plan_mode {enabled: false}`); an automatic goal ends when it completes (`goal_updated`) or the user stops the turn (`abortActiveTurn` → `endAutoGoal` → `auto_mode_end` → stock `goal drop`); neither ending is an undo, so later prompts are still classified. **The Mode picker never shows it** (`ComposerModes` leaves `autoMode`'s plan/goal out of its label, badges and Plan switch); only the "This request: Plan/Goal" chip (`AutoModeChip`, store field `autoMode`, which clears itself when the mode ends) does, with the undo. Turning Plan on in the picker over an automatic plan makes it the user's (`set_plan_mode` without `auto` clears `autoPlan`; the store's `autoMode` goes). **No hijacking busy sessions**: `autoModeSkip` answers `busy` without a request when the session ran anything within `AUTO_MODE_IDLE_MS` (10 min; the last message's timestamp), has open todos (pending or in progress) or running subagents/background jobs (`getAsyncJobSnapshot`), so Plan only happens on a session's first message or after 10 idle minutes; Plan also needs a stated probability ≥ `MIN_PLAN_PROBABILITY` (0.75), Goal ≥ 0.6. A decision is bounded by `AUTO_MODE_TIMEOUT_MS` (2000 ms in the bridge, 3000 ms in the renderer); a timeout, failure, low probability or any unknown answer is none, and a message under 12 characters is none without a request. Other guards: pref off, a turn running, `/` and `!` messages, any active mode the user picked (plan or goal: a manual mode always wins), and per session (`auto-mode-state.ts`, keyed by transcript id) a rejected plan (no plan again, goal still allowed) and an undone decision (`auto_mode_undo`, a free command: leaves the mode, stops all auto decisions for that session). It is logged at debug level (`gui-bridge: auto mode`). Cost, measured 16 Oct 2026 on the real omp (`smol`/`tiny` = claude-haiku-5-5): one judge request per eligible prompt, ≈ 430 input and 4 output tokens, about $0.00005, answered in 0.7–1.4 s; a cold first request or a loaded machine often needs more than the 1500 ms deadline and then decides none (3 of 6 prompts in a loaded run), so the deadline was raised from 1500 to 2000 ms (the one knob, `AUTO_MODE_TIMEOUT_MS`). Pinned by `bridge-auto-mode-core.test.ts` (guards incl. busy, plan threshold, memory), `bridge-auto-mode-stock.test.ts`, `auto-mode-client.test.ts` (plan ended by an unrelated message, kept for a reply, goal ended by Stop, manual plan untouched), `ComposerModes.auto-mode.test.tsx` (picker unchanged, manual takeover), `e2e/auto-mode.e2e.ts` (fake omp: `OMP_GUI_TEST_AUTO_MODE_IDLE_MS` sets its busy window, `OMP_GUI_TEST_AUTO_MODE_DELAY_MS` holds each decision like the real judge, `OMP_GUI_TEST_GOAL_TURN` `{holdMs, complete}` starts stock's goal turn on `goal create`, refuses a plain prompt during it and can complete the goal; plan ended by approval, rejection and an unrelated message, goal ended by completion and Stop, a busy session left alone, a Mode popover with only the Plan and Goal tabs) and the opt-in real-model audit `bridge-auto-mode-real.test.ts` (`OMP_GUI_REAL_WORK=1`).
- Composer run controls (`lib/messages.ts` `abortActiveTurn` / `stopFromEscape` / `restoreAbortedQueue` / `resumeRestoredQueue`, `stores/composer.ts`; stock's semantics, nothing new on the wire; the views are in "Composer modules" above): **one Stop per turn**: `composer.stopping` is set before the `abort_and_restore_queue` goes out and held until the turn ends (the session's `isStreaming` goes false, 10 s cap), so a double click, a double Esc or Stop + Esc send one command. **Esc while a turn runs and the field holds text or a picture only arms** (`composer.stopArmedAt`, `DOUBLE_ESC_MS` 1.5 s, an edit of the draft disarms; the view says "Press Esc again to stop", `input.escAgainToStop`); a second Esc inside the window, or an empty field, stops; popovers/menus/dialogs that consume the Esc never abort (the `defaultPrevented`/`[role=dialog]` guards in `App.tsx`), and the Stop button never asks twice. **Stop never releases the queue**: stock withdraws it, and `restoreAbortedQueue` keeps each message its own `composer.restoredQueue` item (steers, then follow-ups, oldest first) while a view that shows them is mounted (`ComposerRestoredQueue` registers `restoredTrayMounted`; Resume sends the first as a `followUp`-behaviour prompt and the rest as follow-ups, "Put in the field" edits one, nothing is sent by itself), and without one merges them ahead of the draft with a blank line between and one toast. **Withdrawing a prompt that waits on the automatic mode choice returns at once** (`runAutoMode` `signal`, `composer.decidingMode` for the "deciding" cue); a decision the host makes afterwards is ended when it lands (`onLateDecision`). **⇧Tab is never ours** (keymap chords need ⌃/⌥/⌘, so Shift+Tab is focus navigation in the composer too); the thinking level cycles on `thinking.cycle` (⌥⇧T on both platforms, remappable, in Settings › Keyboard and the palette). **Pictures sent as steers** are remembered by queue chip id in the queue store (`expectImages`, matched by text when the chip appears, dropped when an edit or a steer→follow-up move re-queues text only), so the queue pill and popover count them although stock's lanes are text only; a picture-only chip's "[Image]" is the UI language's word (`lib/queue-chip.ts`); `queue.moved` says where an edited message landed (the end of its lane). `lib/composer-blocked.ts` `composerBlockedBy` names what the cards above wait for (`ask` | `approval` | `plan`) for the placeholder. **A session activation hands the next key to the composer** (`lib/tab-focus.ts`, installed in `App.tsx`): after a tab or session change, focus on `<body>`, a sidebar button or a pane that just left moves to the active composer; a dialog, a text field, the terminal, the files editor and touch input are left alone. Pinned by `messages.test.ts`, `auto-mode-client.test.ts`, `queue.test.ts`, `tab-focus.test.ts`, `ComposerRestoredQueue.test.tsx` and `e2e/composer-flows.e2e.ts` / `e2e/steer-send.e2e.ts`.
- Autopilot (UI name **Autopilot**, ko "오토파일럿"; every code id `autopilot`; Mode popover tab beside Plan and Goal): an endless development run in its own new git worktree tab on branch `autopilot/<date>`, built on **stock Goal continuation** (`goal.continuationModes ∋ rpc`), no scheduler of its own. Contracts (journal entries, states, settings, the `AutopilotStatus` that `get_autopilot` and the `autopilot_update` frame carry, `set_autopilot`'s `AutopilotAction`, the bridge protocol `AUTOPILOT_BRIDGE_METHODS`) are the dependency-free `resources/gui-bridge/modes/autopilot-contracts.ts`; the bridge (`modes/autopilot*.ts`) keeps the cycle ledger in the session journal, registers the `autopilot` tool and a static system section and installs a `tool_call` guard (destructive git always blocked, no push/tag/publish for the agent, path checks, `eval`/`debug`/`ask` off the active tools); main (`src/main/autopilot*.ts`) coordinates (start, end/abort, children, approver, post-cycle git audit as the second defence, `ShipRunner` for push/PR/merge/revert, release detection, restart resume). State machine `running | stopping | paused(reason) | waiting_quota | ended`: "End" is stock `goal pause` (the turn in progress finishes) then drop, "Stop now" is `abort`. Renderer: per-tab runtime store `stores/autopilot.ts` (`get_autopilot` hydration + `autopilot_update`), the screen `components/panels/autopilot/` (side panel view `autopilot`, a `PanelView` outside `PANEL_TABS` like `subagent`, listed in View options only for an Autopilot tab and opened once by `lib/panel-auto-open.ts` when a run starts), the work board card (first section), the composer chip "Autopilot · cycle N · area" with "open screen", `GoalDockBar` showing the run's strip, pure rules in `autopilot-view.ts`, log Markdown in `src/shared/autopilot-log.ts` (screen copy and the draft PR body). An Autopilot tab's approval card is held back 3 s (main's approver answers it); a rate-limit stop books `useQuotaResumeStore.reserve` itself (`lib/autopilot-quota.ts`, 15 min when no report names a reset). Prefs `autopilotShip` (default preset local/push/release), `autopilotNotify` (stops | cycles | off), `autopilotLimits` (all off), Settings › Agent behavior › Smart automation. Tests: unit (`src/main/bridge-autopilot-*.test.ts`, `src/main/autopilot*.test.ts`, the renderer's `autopilot*` tests); e2e on the fake omp in `e2e/autopilot-{cycles,rules,ship,restart}.e2e.ts` sharing `e2e/autopilot-harness.ts` (cycles 1-2-3 with en/ko × light/dark captures, Stop versus ■, the stop rules and budgets, shipping against a temp repo with a local bare remote and a fake `gh`, approvals, the quota wait, SIGKILL relaunch and normal quit in the pattern of `interrupted-resume.e2e.ts`); opt-in on the real omp `e2e/real-autopilot.e2e.ts` (`OMP_GUI_REAL_WORK=1`: two cycles in a new worktree, the guard blocking a banned command the agent is asked to run, Stop, the remote unchanged while local only, then one push-level cycle). Fake omp: `e2e/fixture/autopilot.ts` (hooked into `rpc-ui.ts`; env `OMP_GUI_TEST_AUTOPILOT`, its JSON keys are documented at the top of the file) emulates the `modes.autopilot_*` methods and the four frames by **importing** the bridge's own pure rules (`autopilot-core.ts`, `autopilot-guard-core.ts`, `restart-core.ts` `goalActiveAtExit`) over the fixture's journal, which is written to the fake session file so a restart restores it, and drives stock's Goal continuation (`goal` create/pause/resume/drop journaled as stock `mode_change` entries, abort pauses the goal, `retry` re-arms it, stdin EOF journals the turn in flight aborted, a load pauses an active goal). Only the agent is scripted: a cycle's turn runs scripted tool calls through the real guard, commits for real in the worktree, opens children through the real host tools and reports with `end`. The yolo override is only recorded (`fixture_autopilot_session`), not enforced; the active tools are kept as stock and the bridge would (arm/disarm, stock's goal tool memory, `ensureTools` at turn start) and reported in `get_state.dumpTools` of an Autopilot session. Stock's behaviour for both is pinned by `bridge-autopilot-stock.test.ts` and the real-omp audit.
  - Branch choice (the Branch row of the start sheet under Shipping; the headline's `autopilot/<date>` is only the default): a run commits on, and Push pushes to, the branch the user picks. `AutopilotStartOptions.branch` is `new` (default, `autopilot/<date>` made free of clashes, the name editable and sent only when the user changed it; the real `worktree_create` takes `branch: {name, mode: new|local|remote}` and never renames it) or `existing` (a local branch, or one only the ship remote has, which is checked out tracking `<remote>/<name>`). The picker's list is `AutopilotStatus.branches` (idle status, `src/main/autopilot-branches.ts` reading git; the sheet reads it again each time it opens): the repository's default branch first (origin/HEAD, else main/master), then the folder's own branch, then the rest newest first, local and remote merged into one row. **A branch another worktree has checked out is never refused or greyed**: it is the run's target (next bullet), the row reads "via a work branch" and the user's own folder is left alone. **The default branch is allowed only when picked explicitly**, with a one-line warning under the row ("Commits go straight to main; Push goes to main."/"커밋이 main에 바로 쌓이고, 푸시는 main에 해요"); a run that commits on it is its own base (`baseBranch` = the branch, so a release's `HEAD:<default>` stays the one push the guard allows) and Merge/PR refuse (`autopilot_on_base`). Rules are pure in `src/shared/autopilot-branch.ts` (list, `branchProblem`, default warning, suggestion, run id: the part after `autopilot/`, else the date), shared by the sheet and main. Unchanged on any branch: the agent's own pushes stay blocked (main's `ShipRunner` pushes `<sha>:refs/heads/<branch>` to the same name upstream, never with `+`), destructive git (force push, `reset`, branch delete or move, rewriting) stays banned in the guard, and a push that is not a fast-forward is refused by git, never forced, and reported to the agent as a note (`<remote>/<branch> has commits this run lacks (not a fast-forward); Autopilot never forces a push.`), not a stop. The post-cycle audit judges the chosen branch (`AuditScope.branch`; the `autopilot/` prefix earns nothing). The choice persists in the journal as the arm's `branch`, so a restart resumes on the same branch (`#adopt` reads `status.branch`). The `autopilotShip` default does not hold it; the last choice per repository is the pref `autopilotBranch` (`{[repo top level]: choice}`, written by the sheet after a start, validated by `validAutopilotPref`) and only prefills the next sheet while it is still usable. Pinned by `src/shared/autopilot-branch.test.ts`, `src/main/autopilot-branches.test.ts`, `autopilot.test.ts` ("the branch a run is on", restart), `autopilot-audit.test.ts`, `autopilot-ship.test.ts`, `bridge-autopilot-guard.test.ts`, `local/git/worktree.test.ts`, the Branch row tests in `ComposerModes.autopilot.test.tsx` and, against a local bare remote, `e2e/autopilot-ship.e2e.ts` (an existing branch moves on the remote and main does not; the sheet offers main, warns, and Push pushes to it; captures with `OMP_GUI_AUTOPILOT_CAPTURES`).
  - Target branch and no soft stops. **A chosen branch is the run's TARGET**: when another worktree holds it (usually `main` in the user's own folder) the run works on its own branch `autopilot/<date>` cut from the target (`worktree_create` `branch: {name, mode: "new", from}`, `viaWorkBranch` in `src/shared/autopilot-branch.ts`), journaled as `target` (arm params, state entry, `AutopilotStatus.target`, so a restart keeps it), and the ShipRunner pushes `<sha>:refs/heads/<target>` (`ShipTarget.pushTo`), fast-forward only; the sheet labels such a row "via a work branch" and shows "작업 브랜치 → 푸시 대상" under it, the user's folder and its working tree are never touched. **A PR follows the push**: it is opened from the branch Push moves, so with a target `ShipRunner.#pr` pushes the sha to the target (the only branch Autopilot ever pushes; the work branch never reaches the remote) and opens the PR from it; when that head is the base branch there is nothing to compare, so the screen's Create PR is disabled with the reason (`autopilot.ship.prOnBase`) and `set_autopilot ship pr` refuses with `autopilot_on_base` (Merge refuses only when the run's own branch is the base). Pinned by `autopilot-ship.test.ts` ("pr"), `autopilot.test.ts` and `AutopilotPanel.test.tsx`. A rejected push is a note to the agent (`#tellAgent` → bridge `note`, handed over by the next `begin`: fetch, merge, never force), not a stop. **The run does not stop for soft reasons**: red cycles, a failed check, a missing report, compaction churn, cycles without a commit, a rejected or unreachable push, a failed release run, plan mode, all todos blocked, a quiet turn, a model that keeps failing after TurnResume's retries are repaired by the next cycle; `decideAfterCycle` only reports ceilings the user set, `classifyStall` returns `nudge` (frame `autopilot_nudge`: main leaves plan mode and sends one follow-up "call `autopilot begin`", at most every 20 s, 60 s after a failed turn), and `AutopilotStatus.redStreak` is the counter: from two red cycles in a row the timeline shows one muted line "연속 빨강 N"/"N red in a row" (`data-autopilot-red-streak`, `AutopilotTimeline.tsx`). Pauses left: the user's Pause/Stop/End and ■, `ban_violation` (a destructive git action, always), the user's own ceilings (`budget`/`time`/`cycles`/`cost`, off by default), `app_closed` (Continue interrupted work off), `repo_gone` (the worktree or its git directory vanished), `agent_done` ends it; a quota wait resumes itself at the reset. Smart defaults: parallel sessions = a cap of `MAX_PARALLEL_CHILDREN` (4) the agent may use, never forced (the protocol says optional, `begin` hints say fan out only for multi-minute items), release cadence = the agent decides, notifications = stops, focus = all, remote = origin or the only remote.
  - The start sheet is four things and a disclosure (`modes/AutopilotConfig.tsx`): the guide/direction box (`GrowArea`, the repo's `AUTOPILOT.md` or a draft), 내보내기/Shipping (Commit / Push / Release), 브랜치/Branch (with the "작업 브랜치 → 푸시 대상" line under it when the target is held elsewhere) and Start. Everything else sits in ONE closed `SettingsAdvanced` ("고급"/"Advanced"): focus areas, parallel sessions (the 4 option reads "Agent decides (up to 4)" and is the default), the release cadence (Release only), the remote (Push and Release only; empty = detected, the placeholder names it, and `ship.remote` is sent only when typed), notifications (stops), the backlog and the ceilings (all empty). So a Start on the closed sheet sends: focus all, parallel 4, cadence agent, notify stops, remote auto, no ceilings. Nothing in the popover changes layout on blur, Advanced included. Settings › Agent behavior › Smart automation keeps only the 내보내기 default and the notifications visible (`AutopilotSettingsRows`); the ceilings (`autopilotLimits`) are under that section's own Advanced. Pinned by `ComposerModes.autopilot.test.tsx` ("only the guide, Shipping, the Branch row and Start", "changes no layout on blur") and `AutopilotRows.test.tsx`; captures of the sheet with `OMP_GUI_AUTOPILOT_CAPTURES` (`/tmp/autopilot-simple/`, before/after).
  - Permissions are held only while the run lives: arm takes `ask`/`eval`/`debug` off and adds `autopilot`, holds `tools.approvalMode = yolo` as a runtime override on the session's settings (never written to config.yml) and journals the tools it took off plus the ones that were `xd://` devices (`excludedTools`, `mountedTools`); disarm clears the override and restores the set with stock `setActiveToolPresentation(names, mounted)`, because `getEnabledToolNames` also lists the devices and `setActiveToolsByName` would bring a device taken off at arm (`debug`) back top level. Pinned by `src/main/bridge-autopilot-stock.test.ts` (tool presentation before arm = after disarm) and, opt-in on the real omp, by the second test of `e2e/real-autopilot.e2e.ts` (the run tab's approval mode, settings layers and active tools read while armed and after Stop, against a fresh session in the same folder; once more across a SIGKILL relaunch, where the run must continue by itself, armed again before it runs).
  - A restart continues the run (the user's rule: always continue after any restart, a crash, a SIGKILL, a normal quit, an omp restart; only Settings › General › Continue interrupted work off leaves it paused `app_closed` with Resume). `#onReady` sees the journal say `running` and asks `TurnResume.relaunchContinuation`: with a launch marker the run waits for TurnResume's restore of the cut-off turn (`resumeGoal`; a `goalSettled` of `none` continues it all the same, `cancelled`, the resuming row's Stop, pauses it), without one (a kill between turns) it continues at once; a run `#start` is still arming is no restart. The order is stock `goal pause`, arm with `resume`, `goal resume` (`#continueAfterRestart`): stock's startup reconcile reattaches the goal paused and remembers the tool set of that moment (ask, eval, debug, no `autopilot`), its `goal resume` puts that set back, and its `goal pause` restores and forgets it (`RpcGoalController` `#previousTools`), so without the pause the resumed run lost its tool set until the next turn's `ensureTools` and every later pause gave the startup set back. Measured on omp 18.11.3: a killed or quitting OMPdesk closes omp's stdin, and stock journals the cut turn aborted, sometimes with a `session_exit` (the real SIGKILL run) and sometimes without (the stock test), so `goalPausedByExit` must not need one (see Interrupted turns). Pinned by `autopilot.test.ts` (restart), `bridge-autopilot-stock.test.ts` (the third test: stdin EOF mid-turn on the real omp, then the restore and the tool set), `e2e/autopilot-restart.e2e.ts` (the fake omp journals the cut turn as stock does, pauses at startup and remembers its tool set: kill mid-cycle, normal quit, kill between turns, setting off) and the second test of `e2e/real-autopilot.e2e.ts`.
  - Project guide (`AUTOPILOT.md` at a repo's root; constants `AUTOPILOT_GUIDE_*` and `AutopilotGuideInfo` in `autopilot-contracts.ts`, rules in `modes/autopilot-guide.ts` + `boundGuide` in `autopilot-core.ts`): project-wide direction shared across sessions and computers (goals/roadmap, area priorities, no-touch paths, done criteria, taste, release cadence, long-lived ideas). It is **not** the run's backlog or notes: those stay in the session journal and come back through `begin`, and nothing ever writes app backlog items into the file (one source of truth). The bridge reads it at every `autopilot begin` from the run's worktree root (`state.root`) and returns it as `begin.guide` (≤ 8 KB in all, cut on a character boundary with a marker; an absent file or blank text leaves the field out), so an edit applies from the next cycle; the static system section stays byte-identical (it only says the guide exists, ranks below the repo's rules files and never lifts the HARD LIMITS). A repo without one gets a draft in the start sheet (`AutopilotConfig.tsx`, once per sheet): action `guide_draft` → bridge `modes.autopilot_guide {root, draft}` → ONE `completeOnSmol` request (`next-prompt-engine.ts`: stock's `smol` role through `resolveRoleChain`, never the main model, usage in stock's ledger as `autopilot-guide-draft`) over a bounded prompt (rules-file excerpts, README, recent commit subjects, test/build commands), and the deterministic `templateGuide` of the same facts on any failure. A guide that exists is shown for editing instead (`get_autopilot` carries `guide` for a session that is no run). Starting sends the text as `AutopilotStartOptions.guide`; `#commitGuide` writes it into the new worktree and commits it (`--no-verify`, no signing) as the autopilot branch's first commit when the worktree's file differs, so it never touches the user's current branch and reaches main only when the user merges. Precedence: the rules files (AGENTS.md etc.) win over the guide, and the hard limits can never be lifted by it. Pinned by `src/main/bridge-autopilot-guide.test.ts`, the sheet tests in `ComposerModes.autopilot.test.tsx` and the last test of `e2e/autopilot-cycles.e2e.ts`; OMPdesk's own guide is `AUTOPILOT.md` at this repo's root.
  - A cycle's report is checked, not trusted (measured on the real omp, small model, a scratch TS repo: 11 of 11 cycles told the truth about their check, but the agent left the commit hash out in 5 of 7 reports and a second `end` overwrote the first). Rules are pure in `modes/autopilot-verify.ts` (pinned by `src/main/bridge-autopilot-verify.test.ts`), wiring in `autopilot.ts`: **the first `end` of a turn stands** (a repeat only adds follow-ups, children, ship, commit; `shouldSteerStop` sends no stop steer once the cycle has reported); `observeReport` takes the commit from git (a claimed hash that is not in the branch's history is dropped; no repo, the claim stands) and measures `change` (`AutopilotChangeStat`: files, +/−, commits, `docOnly`); a **green claim needs the turn's own tool calls to show it** (ledger of `tool_execution_*` events: a check-like `bash` command, or the one named in `end.check`, that did not fail and ran after the last `edit`/`write`/`ast_edit`): otherwise `verify` becomes `skipped` or `red` with a structured `verifyNote` (`no_check` | `check_failed` | `edited_after`, shown in the timeline in en/ko and in the Markdown log), the `end` result tells the agent and it may run the check and `end` again (a refused report is the only one a second `end` replaces); a cycle with a commit and no green counts red, so it is neither pushed nor ignored. Release cycles are exempt. A new `pick` op names the item after the audit (`now.title` in the status, the panel's live row and "Now" line; before it `now.area` is unset and the screen says "Picking an item…", never the rotation's guess) and refuses a repeat of a recent title before the work, not after. `begin` carries `pick` (what to do next) and `hints` (a follow-up carried ≥ 3 cycles asks for a decision, a docs-only last cycle, the cost of fan-out): all dynamic guidance rides in `begin`, the static system section is unchanged. Fan-out measured: two 25–30 s items took 62 s and cost about twice as much in two children as one after the other (both merged cleanly), so `hints` says to fan out only for items of several minutes. The fake omp mirrors `pick` and the first-end-stands rule (`endTwice`); verify enforcement is unit-tested and measured on the real omp.
- Delegation strength: the Run chip's Delegation section (`layout/composer/DelegationField.tsx`, mounted by `RunChip.tsx`; the phone's Run sheet shows it too, and it stays hidden when the omp cannot report a strength) sets Your settings / Solo / Assisted / Collaborative / Swarm per tab through `get_delegation`/`set_delegation` → bridge `modes/delegation.ts` (rules `delegation-core.ts`, presets `delegation-contracts.ts`). Each strength is stock `task.eager`, `task.maxConcurrency` and (Solo) `task.maxRecursionDepth = 0` held as runtime overrides, never written to config.yml; stock's own listeners rebuild the prompt and drop or restore the task tool. The last pick is prefs `delegationMode`, passed as `OMP_GUI_DELEGATION` on every spawn and applied inside the extension factory like the thinking default; a Settings write of one of those settings releases the strength.
- Desktop slot (one session at a time drives the desktop; contract `resources/gui-bridge/desktop-slot-contracts.ts`, bridge `desktop-slot.ts` + `desktop-slot-core.ts`, main `src/main/desktop-slot.ts` + `desktop-slot-host.ts` + `local/desktop-slot.ts`, renderer `stores/desktop-slot.ts` + `DesktopSlotIndicator.tsx` + the waiting line in `ToolCard.tsx`, capability `desktopSlot`). **What stock lacks** (checked in stock `tools/computer*`, `computer/supervisor.ts` and the native addon): each omp process has its own `ComputerSupervisor`; the native layer only refuses an input while another desktop operation owns input/focus control ("no input was sent"), it neither queues nor spans a session's observe → act → verify sequence, and nothing crosses processes at the session level. So OMPdesk owns one slot per computer in **main**, and the bridge asks it by frames: it patches `ComputerSupervisor.prototype.run` once (feature-detected; without `run`/`revokeControl` the slot is `unavailable` in capabilities/health and stock behaviour stays). A run with `snapshot.readOnly` (screenshots, `capabilities`) never waits; the first acting run takes the slot (`desktop_slot_acquire` → main grants through the `desktop_slot_bridge` command), and the session keeps it across its whole observe → act → verify span until its turn ends (`agent_end`), `DESKTOP_SLOT_IDLE_MS` (20 s) pass without a computer run, Stop, abort, tab close or crash, or the lease (`DESKTOP_SLOT_LEASE_MS`, 120 s without `desktop_slot_touch`) runs out. Waiters queue first come, first served; an acting run that had to wait does **not** run once granted: it returns `DESKTOP_SLOT_STALE_NOTICE` so the agent takes a fresh screenshot and repeats. **Handoff order:** abort the holder's in-flight input run, await stock `ComputerController.revokeControl()` on every controller the bridge saw, and only then grant the next session. **The slot is never handed on unconfirmed:** a `revokeControl()` that rejects (the bridge's `revokeControllers` fails if any controller does, and the holder's acting runs are refused with "input is blocked" meanwhile) or does not answer within `DESKTOP_SLOT_REVOKE_TIMEOUT_MS` (10 s) leaves the holder in a `revoke-failed` state (`DesktopSlotState.holder.revokeFailed`): nobody is granted, main retries the revoke every `DESKTOP_SLOT_REVOKE_RETRY_MS` (5 s; an answer that arrives late still counts), and the indicator offers "다시 시도" (`desktop_slot_retry`) and "그 세션 중지" (`desktop_slot_kill`: abort plus `restart()` of its omp, SIGTERM only). The slot is freed only by a confirmed revoke, a confirmed exit of the holder's process (the host re-reads `whenExited()` while the sidecar is ready and acts on a non-ready status only after that exit; a failed grant proves nothing and is not treated as a crash), or a kill whose process exited within 5 s (`KILL_CONFIRM_MS`); a bridge `desktop_slot_release` follows only a revoke it confirmed. Pinned by fail-first tests in `desktop-slot.test.ts`, `desktop-slot-host.test.ts` and `bridge-desktop-slot.test.ts` (rejecting revoke, revoke that never answers, holder death after a failed revoke, kill). The UI is an app-wide indicator "화면 사용 중: <title> · 대기 N" with Stop (`desktop_slot_stop`: aborts the holder's turn and hands off), and a waiting `eval` card says "화면 차례를 기다리는 중 (N번째)". Hosted tabs this computer runs for a paired device join this slot; tabs on a remote host use that host's own slot (main skips `sidecar.device` tabs). Autopilot never takes it (its `eval` is off). Not built: a "run marked done" release, because stock has no such marker; idle and turn end cover it. Pinned by `desktop-slot.test.ts`, `desktop-slot-host.test.ts`, `bridge-desktop-slot.test.ts` and `e2e/desktop-slot.e2e.ts`.
- Account rotation: Settings › Models & providers stores prefs `accountRotation` (`quota` default = stock omp's usage ranking | `even`; `src/shared/account-rotation.ts`, control `providers/RotationModeControl.tsx`). Stock has no switch for it, so each spawn passes `OMP_GUI_ACCOUNT_ROTATION` plus the shared ledger `<userData>/account-rotation.json` (`OMP_GUI_ACCOUNT_ROTATION_STATE`), and a change (or a bridge connect) pushes `account_rotation_set {mode}` to every live local sidecar and every tab this machine hosts (`LocalMachine.setAccountRotation`, `src/main/account-rotation.ts`). In even mode the bridge's `account-rotation.ts` (`before_agent_start`) gives a session without an account the unblocked one handed out least recently across all local omps, as an automatic warm pin (`authStorage.sessions.pin(…, {restoredAtMs})`); a session keeps its account until it is blocked (an idle pin is renewed, not re-ranked), and stock's 429 blocking/failover is untouched. A paired host's tabs follow the host's pref, which Settings reads and writes through `host.accountRotation.get|set`.
  - Quota mode re-ranks at turn start. Stock re-ranks an automatic pin only when it is cold (Claude: 60 min idle), blocked or spent (`select.ts` `rankDespitePin`, `automaticPinWouldBeEvicted`), and `affinity.record` refreshes the last-use time on every request, so a session used without a 60-minute gap stayed on its first account for days while a sibling stock itself ranked first sat idle, and its subagents (`sessions.inherit` copies the parent's pin) with it. The same `before_agent_start` hook therefore runs `rerankBeforePrompt` (`account-rotation-core.ts`, wiring in `account-rotation.ts`) for the main agent only (`ctx.agent.kind === "main"`; a subagent's own turn never re-ranks, it keeps what stock copied at spawn) and never mid-turn. It asks stock, not a policy of ours: the account stock's selector gives a session without a pin right now is `authStorage.keys.getWithCredential(provider, <stand-in id>, {…, recordAffinity: false})` with the options model-registry's `getApiKey` passes. It moves the session there (`sessions.pin(…, {restoredAtMs: now})`, an automatic warm pin, so subagents spawned afterwards inherit it) only when every one of these holds, else stock decides as before: more than one account; a pin exists and is warm (a cold one is stock's to re-rank); it is automatic — stock keeps `explicit` off the public account list, so the bridge reads it through the `include(provider, explicit)` callback of `sessions.inherit` with a stand-in target and a `false` return (nothing is copied), and a pin with no provenance reported is left alone; the session is unrestricted (`sessions.permits` for every account) and no account has a stock account policy (`oauth.policy`): pools and policies are the user's choice; the pin is not blocked; and stock's own comparator still ranks the other account first once the pin's required drain is multiplied by `RERANK_DRAIN_MARGIN` (`orderUsageRankedCandidates` over `RankedOAuthCandidate` rows built from the two accounts' `usage.report`s with stock's `windowRequiredDrain`/`normalizeUsageFraction`/`remainingUsageFraction`/`planPriority`, mirroring `#rankOAuthSelections`; both accounts must be measured). The margin (2) is the one thing OMPdesk adds, because stock compares drain at 1e-6 tolerance and every account change rewrites the provider's per-account prompt cache: only the plain drain order gets it, every stock tier (blocked, spent allowance, the 85% 5h demotion, unmeasured usage) moves a pin at once, and a round trip needs a 4x swing. Pinned on the real omp by `src/main/bridge-account-rotation-stock.test.ts` (2.7x moves, 1.9x never, 2.1x does, no way back at 1.9x, hot 5h tier, explicit/cold/subagent, inheritance) with the rules in `bridge-account-rotation.test.ts`; every internal is in `capabilities.ts` (`accountRotation`) and `health.ts` (`auth/rank`). The proper fix belongs in stock (re-rank warm automatic pins at a turn boundary behind a margin, where `explicit` and the ranked candidates are already at hand).
- Provider sign-in (Settings › Models, first run; `providers/ProviderLogin.tsx`) runs stock `login` on the tab's omp. Its `open_url`, `input` and info `notify` requests that arrive while a sign-in is in flight belong to the panel (`loginRequestIds` in `stores/extension-ui.ts`; the kinds are `isSignInRequest` in `shared/rpc-types.ts`) and never render as chat cards, even after the panel unmounts, the page reloads or a paired host's link comes back: main counts the `login` commands in flight per tab with their providers (`SidecarPool.noteSignIn`; for a paired host's tab also what the host reports through its `signIn` tab event — sent as `login` starts and ends and on every `tab.open`, a new tab's included (`{active: []}`) and ahead of `openAsks` on a re-attach — because the host's login runs on when this window's `login` call fails with the link; a status the host reports (not the link) that leaves `ready` drops the host's list, since its logins died with that omp) and marks every request `isSignInRequest` accepts while one runs (`IpcSignIn`: `active`, provider, the browser page it opened) on the live `EXTENSION_UI` frame, in `EXTENSION_UI_OPEN` and in a host's re-sent `openAsks`. All of them reach the page through `pushTabExtensionUiRequest`, so one rule applies: a marked request is never a card. While `login` runs (`active`) it goes into `loginRequestIds` and the page adopts the sign-in (`adoptedLogin`, also counted as in flight), so the panel shows it again when opened ("Sign-in in progress…", the browser link, the paste fallback, Cancel) until main sends `SIGN_IN_ENDED` (`endTabSignIn`: answers the leftover, the panel reloads the providers or shows the failure; the outcome is unseen when a host's login ended while the link was down or its omp went away); once `login` has answered it is a leftover and is answered `cancelled` at once. A browser sign-in finishes through stock's loopback callback; stock also races a code paste against it ("Paste the authorization code…") and never closes it, so the panel shows the paste as a collapsed fallback (open on a paired host's tab) and answers the leftover request with `cancelled` once `login` returns — an empty answer before that only re-asks. Stock's RPC `login` takes no cancel and holds omp's serial command queue (up to its 5-minute callback timeout), so Cancel sends `login_cancel` (`src/main/local/settings.ts`), which ends it through the callback's own denial (`error=access_denied` with the flow's `state`, what a provider's Deny button sends). A relaunched client has lost the browser page of a host's login (the Cancel of its adopted panel then answers the paste instead).
- Featured providers (the one picker both sign-in surfaces share: first run's "which provider do you use" step and Settings › Models & accounts › Add a provider, `providers/ProviderPicker.tsx`; list `FEATURED_PROVIDERS` in `providers/provider-catalog.ts`): the popular ones lead as their own group ("Popular providers"/"주요 프로바이더", cards `[data-featured-provider]`, `ProviderMark` + name + one line + the action, `min-h-11` = 44 px, `auto-fill minmax(15rem)` so a phone gets one column) and everything else stays below under "Other providers"/"다른 프로바이더", searchable as before. Ids and login kinds are stock's (pi-catalog `auth/*.kdl`, `/login` roster), never invented: ChatGPT (Plus/Pro) = `openai-codex` OAuth, Claude (Pro/Max) = `anthropic` OAuth, OpenRouter = `openrouter` (browser sign-in or paste yields a durable API key), Google (Gemini) = `google-gemini-cli` (stock's plain `google` has no login), Grok (SuperGrok) = `xai-oauth` (SuperGrok / X Premium+ device-code sign-in, primary) with `xai` (xAI API key) as the card's quieter second action (`apiKeyId`; a provider with both logins gets subscription first, API key second; the key action is a quiet row INSIDE the same card, under a hairline, never a loose row under the grid). The card names are fixed brand labels (stock's names are long); a card whose logins stock's catalog does not list is skipped. First run (`showConnectedFeatured`) shows a connected featured provider as "Connected" in place; Settings already lists connected ones above and leaves them out. A search is the flat list of every match, no group. `STARTER_PROVIDERS` holds the featured ids too, so the cards show before omp answers. Tests: `ProviderPicker.test.tsx` (order, actions, connected, search), `e2e/first-run-no-omp.e2e.ts` (fake omp `OMP_GUI_TEST_FEATURED=1`, en/ko × light/dark captures), `e2e/real-core.e2e.ts` (stock's real catalog lists all five in order), `e2e/mobile-pages.e2e.ts` (`?firstrun=1` on the dev harness: one column, 44 px, no overflow).
- Held stock defaults: sessions OMPdesk spawns (local tabs and the tabs a host runs) differ from stock in the settings of `GUI_THINKING_DEFAULTS` (`resources/gui-bridge/thinking-default-core.ts`, wired by `thinking-default.ts`) unless some layer (config.yml, a project config, `--config` overlay, env) sets them. Each is held as a runtime override applied inside the extension factory (stock loads `-e` extensions after `Settings.init` and before createAgentSession builds the tools and the advisor and picks the first thinking level); never written to config.yml (the omp CLI keeps stock), reported by Settings with `runtime` provenance, released by a Settings write first (so the user's value persists and wins), taken up again by a reset, and inherited by subagents through stock's `createSubagentSettings` snapshot. Pinned on the real omp by `src/main/bridge-omp-defaults-stock.test.ts` and `bridge-goal-continuation-stock.test.ts`, rules in `bridge-thinking-default.test.ts`. The list:
  - `defaultThinkingLevel` = `auto` (stock `high`) and `providers.autoThinkingMaxEffort` = `max` (stock `xhigh`), so `auto` may resolve every effort the model supports up to its top tier. The advisor role is written with `:auto` when picked without a level (`resources/gui-bridge/role-values.ts`), because stock runs an unsuffixed advisor at a fixed `medium`; pickers show it without the suffix.
  - `goal.continuationModes` = `["interactive", "rpc"]` (stock continues a goal between turns over RPC only with `"rpc"` listed); the loader is `goals/settings` in `capabilities.ts` / `health.ts`.
  - `display.showTokenUsage` on (the GUI's usage row follows it unless a GUI display preference overrides), `lsp.diagnosticsOnEdit` on (a language-server pass per edit), `lsp.formatOnWrite` on (rewrites a written code file with its formatter).
  - `astGrep.enabled` on (`astEdit.enabled` is on in stock already). Both tools are the `@oh-my-pi/pi-natives` addon every omp ships, not a separate binary; `applyGuiThinkingDefault` feature-detects the export (`NATIVE_DEFAULTS`), and an omp without it keeps stock's value with a Settings note (`SettingEntry.note`, `settings.note.native`).
  - `computer.enabled` on (stock default off): the `computer` eval prelude (screenshots, input, accessibility of the host desktop; `/computer on|off|status` still toggles it per session, and a Settings write releases the default). An omp without the setting keeps stock's behaviour (`lookup` finds no handle, so it is skipped). **Cost and risk:** the prelude adds its prompt section and tool text to every session's context, and it rides on `eval`, so a session without `eval` has no computer use: **Autopilot removes `eval` (`EXCLUDED_TOOLS` in `modes/autopilot.ts`), so computer use stays off there by design**. Stock's `computerApproval` gives `capabilities` and `read_only` runs `read` approval and every input action `exec`; stock's `tools.approvalMode` default is `yolo` and OMPdesk does not change it, so **by default (yolo) a session can click and type on the user's desktop with no approval card**: only `always-ask` (or a rule) puts a card in front of input. The prelude runs inside the omp process (a worker), so on macOS Screen Recording and Accessibility are granted to the app that launched it (OMPdesk, or the terminal for a CLI omp); without them stock's native layer fails the call with "macOS Screen Recording permission is not granted for this process" / "macOS Accessibility permission is required for native input" and `computer.capabilities()` reports `capturePermission`/`inputPermission`/`axPermission`. macOS never prompts from stock's preflight, and OMPdesk adds no hint of its own: the error reaches the model and the tool card as stock words it. Windows: the native `DesktopCapabilities` API is backend-agnostic (`backend`, `displayServer`), but only the macOS backend was inspected here; Windows behaviour is stock's and untested by OMPdesk. Pinned by `bridge-omp-defaults-stock.test.ts` (the setting resolves `true` as `runtime`, `eval` is enabled and the computer prelude is active, which is what `/computer status` reports as "enabled · prelude: active") and by the opt-in `e2e/real-computer-use.e2e.ts` (read-only turn shows no card; an input turn under `always-ask` shows a card and is denied; nothing is ever approved).
  - `advisor.enabled` on: a second model (the `advisor` role; unset it follows `slow`, then stock's slow chain) reviews every turn, which costs another model call per turn. Stock pins it off for RPC hosts (`protocolDefault: ["rpc", "acp"]`, `applyProtocolDefaults` in `main.ts` before the extensions load) as a soft runtime override that reads as configured; `PROTOCOL_PINNED` lets the override replace such a pin while nothing but the pin sets the value (provenance `runtime`, value equal to the schema default, no persisted layer). The advisor's other pinned tuning (`advisor.syncBacklog`, …) keeps stock's defaults. OMPdesk already renders advisor cards.
  - `title.icons` = `boring` (stock `nf+emoji`): session titles are plain text, with no icon and code card ahead of them and no extra title-model request for the card (`renameTitle` in stock `agent-session.ts`). Only new titles change; a stored carded title stays until ✨ or evolving makes a fresh one (see Automatic session titles).
  - OTLP telemetry export off (the user's decision; not a setting): `telemetry.otlpExportEnabled` is read by `initTelemetryExport` in `main.ts` before the extensions load, so no runtime override reaches it; instead every omp OMPdesk runs a session in gets `OTEL_SDK_DISABLED=true` (`OMP_TELEMETRY_OFF` in `omp-runtime.ts`, spread last over the user's environment: local and hosted tabs through `SidecarManager`, scheduled runs, benchmarks, model setup and `omp` CLI calls through `runtime.run`), on which stock's only exporter (`telemetry-export.ts`) returns at once, whatever `OTEL_*` endpoints the user's environment names. The stats server and the stock helper never call it; `omp --version`/`update` exit before it. Pinned by `shell-env.test.ts`.
  - Not held, on evidence from stock 18.11.1: `display.showTurnTime` and `read.toolResultPreview` are read only by the TUI renderer (`modes/utils/ui-helpers.ts`) and the GUI has no consumer for them (it hides `display.showTurnTime`); `retry.usageAwareFallback` and `retry.waitForUsageReset` stay stock's, since account rotation and the interrupted-turn resume cover them.
  - Automatic session titles on (not a stock setting: stock's `autoTitle` is a session option that `main.ts` sets for the TUI only, and `main.ts` sets `PI_NO_TITLE` for `--mode rpc-ui`, so a GUI session stayed "New session"; prefs `autoTitle` and the sub-switch `autoTitleEvolve`, both default on, Settings › Agent behavior › Session titles). Not a runtime override of a setting: the bridge (`modes/auto-title.ts`, behaviour in `auto-title-engine.ts`) runs stock's own title machinery from the live session's events, so `PI_NO_TITLE` cannot reach it. The user's own `PI_NO_TITLE` is still respected: main reads it from the environment it hands omp (`titleSuppressedByEnv`) and passes `suppressed` in `OMP_GUI_AUTO_TITLE`/`auto_title_set`. Pinned by `bridge-auto-title*.test.ts` and `shell-env.test.ts`.
- Model roles (Settings › Models & accounts › Model roles; `ModelRolesSection.tsx`, `ModelRoleRow.tsx`; contracts in dependency-free `resources/gui-bridge/model-role-contracts.ts`): stock owns the roles (ids, names, `accepts`, empty-role fallbacks, `@role` resolution in `config/model-roles.ts` / `config/model-resolver.ts`); OMPdesk only groups them by purpose — Conversation (default, slow, smol, plan), Agent work (task, advisor, commit, tiny), Input & output (vision, image, web, speech, dictation, judge), Custom roles (the stock `memory` role is in `HIDDEN_ROLE_IDS`: it only served stock's memory backends, which OMPdesk no longer offers) — and writes a localized one-line "when it is used" (`modelRoles.use.<id>`, grounded in the stock call site, e.g. `commit/model-selection.ts`, `utils/image-question.ts`, `auto-thinking/classifier.ts`) and the empty-role fallback text (`modelRoles.fallback.<id>`) per role. `get_model_roles` adds `effective` to every role (`role-effective.ts`: the model it really runs on, resolved through stock `resolveModelOverride` / `resolveRoleSelection` over the role's own `roleCandidatePool`, with `source` configured / fallback / session / none and `via` the role that answered; the per-role selector lists copy the stock call sites and are pinned by `src/main/bridge-model-roles-stock.test.ts` against `resolveRoleChain` and `resolveAdvisorRoleSelection` on the real omp). A row also shows the provider's account state and the tightest live quota (`useUsageReports` from `hooks/use-usage-reports.ts`, the tab's one usage poll; `inUseReport`, `tightestLimit` from `lib/usage-reports.ts`), the thinking level the resolved selector names (else "follows session"), and a picker over stock's candidate pool. The model setup assistant proposes per group (its prompt and review table use the same groups) but assigns only stock's built-in roles; a custom role keeps the user's model.
- Custom roles (`modelRoles.<id>`, selected as `@id`): stock treats `@id` as a role only while `modelRoles.<id>` holds a value, and otherwise leaves it in an agent's `model:` list as a pattern that matches nothing, so resolution moves to the next entry (`getModelRoleAlias`, `resolveConfiguredRolePattern`, `resolveModelOverride`). An agent therefore lists the role first and the built-ins it follows while empty (`["@review", "@slow", "@task"]`) and needs nothing assigned.
