Consumers stock reads: an agent definition's `model:` (`resolveAgentModelSelection` in `task/executor.ts`, `structured-subagent.ts`), `task.agentModelOverrides`, `/model` and `--model` (`resolveCliModel`, only configured roles), and `cycleOrder` (a role needs a value to join it). OMPdesk ships six preset roles in `PRESET_ROLES` — explore, review, debug, docs, test, design — and never a role nothing consumes: `AGENT_TEMPLATES` (`src/shared/agent-templates.ts`) carry `role` and a `model` of `roleSelectors(role)` (code-explorer, code-reviewer, debugger, docs-writer, test-writer, ui-polisher), and `agent-templates.test.ts` fails on a preset without a template or a template naming a role outside the preset. The bridge lists presets while empty; users add, rename (the display name, stored in stock `modelTags.<id>`; the id never changes because agents select it) and remove their own (`define_model_role` / `forget_model_role` → `model_role_define` / `model_role_forget`: `modelTags` with OMPdesk's `description`, `modelRoles`, `cycleOrder`). Each custom row lists the agents using it (`get_agent_definitions` → `modelRoleRefs`) and the template agents not installed yet. `e2e/model-roles-real.e2e.ts` (`OMP_GUI_ROLES_REAL=1`, private copy of the user's config through `e2e/real-omp.ts`, no model call) resolves every role and every template agent's model through stock; `OMP_GUI_ROLES_CAPTURES=<dir>` adds the light/dark × en/ko, wide and narrow captures.
- Idle compaction: stock's `compaction.idleEnabled` / `idleThresholdTokens` / `idleTimeoutSeconds` (Settings › Agent › Context, `/autoclear on [delay] | off`) only had a timer in the TUI, so `resources/gui-bridge/idle-compaction.ts` (rules in `idle-compaction-core.ts`, a copy of the TUI's `#scheduleIdleCompaction`) arms it on the live session after a terminal `agent_end` and calls stock `runIdleCompaction()`. It and the idle recap share one idle-window state machine (`timer-core.ts` `IdleWindowTimer`: window, superseded arms, the GUI draft); each keeps its own settings, delay bounds and fire. The renderer (`hooks/use-idle-compaction-draft.ts`) reports each tab's draft empty ⇄ non-empty through `set_idle_compaction_draft`, because stock skips a session whose input holds text.
- Idle recap: stock's `recap.enabled` (default on) / `recap.idleSeconds` (240) only had a timer in the TUI (`#scheduleIdleRecap` in `event-controller.ts`), so `resources/gui-bridge/session-recap.ts` (rules in `session-recap-core.ts`, a copy of that timer) arms it on the live session after a terminal `agent_end` and makes the recap the way `#runIdleRecap` does: stock's own `prompts/system/recap-user.md` rendered with the goal (or title) and next todo task, one `runEphemeralTurn`, `previewLine(…, TRUNCATE_LENGTHS.RECAP)`, the reply journaled with `sessionManager.recordRecap`. It is skipped while the composer holds a draft (the same `set_idle_compaction_draft` report, which `local/settings.ts` also forwards as `recap_draft`), cancelled by any new turn, compaction or typing, re-checked for idleness after the reply, and follows `recap.*` writes (`recapSettingChanged`). Delivery: stock's TUI notice is transient, so the GUI gets a displayed `recap` custom message (a `custom_message` journal entry the transcript replays; `RecapCard` in `CustomMessageCard.tsx` draws one quiet muted line, en/ko `chat.custom.recap`) and a `context` hook drops it from every model request. Pinned on the real omp by `src/main/bridge-session-recap-stock.test.ts` and `bridge-session-recap.test.ts`; the fake omp serves `recap_draft` and `OMP_GUI_TEST_RECAP` (`e2e/omp-defaults.e2e.ts`); `sessionRecap` in `capabilities.ts` / `health.ts`.
- Context inspector (Insights › This session › Inspect, `components/insights/ContextInspector.tsx`): the bridge's `context.inspect` (`resources/gui-bridge/context-inspect.ts`, rules in `context-inspect-core.ts`) keeps stock `getContextBreakdown()`'s anchored totals, splits the tool schemas by source (built-in / MCP / extension, counted per tool with stock `estimateToolSchemaTokens`) and reads the context files back out of the rendered `<repo-rules>` block; `context.entry` returns one entry's provider text (tool schema via stock `toolWireSchema`, messages via `convertToLlm`), capped at 32k characters.
- Per-turn prompt sections: stock keeps what `before_agent_start` appended only while a turn runs (the per-turn override is dropped when it ends and a rebuild of the base prompt replaces it), so `session.systemPrompt` lacks OMPdesk's own sections (reply language, profile, reply style, visuals) before the first turn and after a rebuild. Each of those modes registers its section through `resources/gui-bridge/prompt-sections.ts` (`registerPromptSection`: one "append if missing" step per id, hooked into `before_agent_start`), and the context inspector applies the registered sections in install order (`withTurnSections`) to list and preview the prompt the next turn sends. A new mode that appends to the system prompt registers there, never with a bare `pi.on("before_agent_start")`.
- Prompt origins (A85, `resources/gui-bridge/prompt-origin.ts`, contracts `prompt-origin-contracts.ts`): a host passes the device a tab runs for (and its own name) as `OMP_GUI_PROMPT_ORIGIN` at spawn; that omp journals one `ompdesk.prompt-origin` custom entry per user message (public `message_end` hook, keyed by the message timestamp), and `sessions.transcript` sets `promptFrom` on the prompts typed on another device than the viewing one once a session holds prompts from two devices (MessageBubble shows "from {device}"). Each device's tab is its own omp, so no prompt or approval is ever cancelled or answered from another device.
- `src/main/omp-runtime.ts` finds omp (`OMP_GUI_OMP` override → login-shell `PATH` → `~/.bun/bin/omp`, `/opt/homebrew/bin/omp`, `/usr/local/bin/omp`; Windows `%USERPROFILE%\.bun\bin\omp.exe`, `omp.cmd`/`omp.exe` on `PATH`) and enforces `MIN_OMP_VERSION` (18.11.0, kept: the floor was set for the removed Memory panel, nothing else documents a need for it, so it only moves with a newer requirement or an audit). Every bridge feature in `capabilities.ts` is required at that version; when a feature needs a newer omp, raise `MIN_OMP_VERSION` instead of carrying an older-omp fallback. The fake omp (`e2e/sidecar-fixture.ts`) reports the minimum.
- Machine setup (`src/main/setup/`, contract `src/shared/setup.ts`): at launch main detects Bun, omp (through `resolveOmp`, so `MIN_OMP_VERSION` gates it), Git, GitHub CLI and Node.js (through the REA probe) and installs what is missing **on its own** — no install button, no consent click. Required, in order: Bun (`curl -fsSL https://bun.sh/install | bash` / `irm bun.sh/install.ps1 | iex`, user-level), on Windows Git for Windows (an existing install with `bin\bash.exe` is reused; otherwise the PortableGit pinned in `plans.ts` with its published SHA-256, extracted without admin into `%LOCALAPPDATA%\ompdesk\tools\git`; never WSL), then omp (`<abs bun> install -g @oh-my-pi/pi-coding-agent`, or `omp update` below the minimum). Optional, in the background once omp works: Git on macOS (`xcode-select --install`, Apple's dialog), gh and Node (Homebrew on macOS, else the official archive checked against its published SHA-256 into the managed tools dir `<userData>/tools`; `winget … --accept-source-agreements --accept-package-agreements` on Windows, else `unavailable`). Main downloads the PortableGit and archive files itself (`download.ts`: streamed to `<tools>/.downloads`, SHA-256 checked, removed after the install) before their script re-checks and unpacks them, so a running item's `progress` carries real bytes against the announced size; every running item also carries its place in its run (`step`/`steps`) and `startedAt`, and the view shows a determinate bar only for those bytes, an indeterminate one otherwise, plus "Step n of m" and the elapsed time. `paths.ts` appends the managed bin dirs to `process.env.PATH` and sets `GIT_INSTALL_ROOT` (which stock `resolveWindowsShell` checks after the Git for Windows roots) at startup and after each install, so every later spawn inherits them; after an install it re-reads the login-shell PATH (macOS) or the registry PATH (Windows), re-detects, and when omp became usable restarts every local tab in `error`/`exited` and the stats server — no app restart. Each item is tried automatically once per launch; after three automatic misses only once a day (`<userData>/setup-state.json`); the user's Retry always runs. Every install runs under `supervisor.ts` (the app binary as Node via `ELECTRON_RUN_AS_NODE`), which kills the installer's process tree when its stdin closes: Cancel (which aborts main's download too), quit (`SetupService.dispose`) or a crash. IPC (`setup:get-state`, `setup:retry`, `setup:cancel`, push `setup:state`) takes item ids only and refuses anything else; no handler accepts a command. Automatic installs run only in a packaged app outside `OMP_GUI_HEADLESS=1`, or with `OMP_GUI_TEST_INSTALLER` (plus `OMP_GUI_TEST_DOWNLOAD`/`_SHA256`, a file every test plan downloads first); dev and test launches only detect. While a local tab's omp is unusable and a required item is missing or installing, the renderer shows a full-window setup screen (`components/onboarding/SetupTakeover.tsx`, shown by `App.tsx` from `useMachineSetupNeeded()`): one modal dialog over the whole window, sidebar and title bar included, with the window drag strip and the OS window controls kept live; everything beneath it is `inert`, and menu actions (except the language switch; the tray's "new session" included), omp:// links, shortcuts and drops are no-ops through `useMachineSetupStore.takeover`, Tab is trapped and chords never reach the app. When required setup finishes it fades out (reduced motion skips the fade) into the normal first-run flow (`FirstRunGate` → provider step; the inline step list shows "Install omp" as done). Optional items never show it, remote tabs never show it, and every item stays listed in Settings › General › This computer. Claude Code CLI and Codex CLI are never installed: omp signs in to Anthropic and OpenAI itself. `.github/workflows/setup-assets-health.yml` downloads every one of those files weekly through `download.ts` (`scripts/setup-assets-health.ts`, `gh workflow run setup-assets-health.yml` to run it now) and keeps one issue open while any fails.
- Connect installs its tool (`SetupService.installForConnection`): a connection the user starts that needs a missing optional item installs it right then, as the user's own action — past the daily back-off and the once-per-launch rule, queued behind any install that runs, only in a launch that installs on its own (otherwise it only detects) — and the connection carries on when the tool lands. Connect GitHub (Settings › General, the first run's GitHub step, the PR center's signed-out card; `onboarding/GithubSignIn.tsx`) calls `GithubAuthService.startLogin`, which with gh missing sets the flow to `installing`, awaits setup's gh item (`installGh`), then starts `gh auth login`; an install that ended without gh is `gh_install_failed`, nothing able to install it here is `gh_missing`, and Cancel during the install stops it (`cancelGhInstall`). Turning the built-in REA server on while Node.js is missing or too old (`LocalMachine.setBuiltinRea`) starts setup's node item. The connect UI shows that item inline (`onboarding/SetupInstallInline.tsx`: "Installing GitHub CLI…" with step, bytes and clock; the reason plus Retry after a failure; the download page when nothing can install it), and setup's own background install of gh shows there too. A paired host's tab: the host's own UI and setup install on the host. git/gh lookups (`local/git/exec.ts` `resolveBin`, `execBin`) read the spawn PATH on every call (`spawnPath` shares the cached login-shell probe, which `refreshPath` drops after an install), so a gh that setup just put into the managed tools dir or the registry PATH is found without a relaunch; a copy of PATH kept from the first git call used to hide it. No stock omp provider login shells out to a CLI (github-copilot, gemini-cli, google-antigravity, cursor, anthropic and openai-codex are OAuth or device-code flows in pi-ai's `registry/oauth/`; perplexity only reads its macOS app's token), so no provider sign-in installs anything. `e2e/github-cli-install.e2e.ts` covers it with the fake installer: gh off PATH and in its back-off, the click downloads (held at half), fails offline with Retry, installs into the managed tools dir and reaches the device code and the signed-in account (en/ko captures).
- omp updates itself (`src/main/omp-updater.ts`, contract `src/shared/omp-update.ts`; OMPdesk's own update is `updater.ts`, installed by hand unless the opt-in `appAutoUpdate` is on): while the app runs, main asks stock `omp update --check` (`SetupService.latestOmp`) for the newest omp at launch (after machine setup's first pass) and every 4 h, doubling the wait per failure in a row up to 24 h; while omp is missing nothing is checked. When that is newer than the installed omp (`compareVersions`) it runs stock `omp update` through `SetupService.updateOmp` — the omp item's `omp-update` plan under the setup supervisor, in the background, no click, queued behind any install, never waiting for a turn to end (the user's rule: a running omp keeps the code it loaded). The check and the update run on the detected omp in one environment (`ompUpdateEnv` in `omp-runtime.ts`): that omp's folder first on PATH, because stock updates the first `omp` on PATH (`resolveOmpPath` = `$which("omp")`), and for a `bun install -g` launcher `BUN_INSTALL=<its Bun root>`, because stock calls it a Bun install only inside `bun pm bin -g`; so they follow the user's registry (.npmrc, bunfig, `npm_config_registry`), channel and install method (Bun, npm, Homebrew, mise, standalone binary), agree with each other, and land in the omp OMPdesk runs, never in another `omp` earlier on PATH. Settings' `get_omp_update` (a paired host's omp check, `local/misc.ts`) uses the same environment. Why that is safe: `bun install -g` replaces package files by new inodes (verified: the inode of an installed file changes across `bun install -g pkg@old` → `@new`), and the bridge's literal `@oh-my-pi/*` imports resolve to the running omp's bundled singletons (`health.ts`); the processes that read omp's files from disk (stats server, stock helper — `import(variable)`) are stopped before the installer and started again after it. Known limit: the old process's lazy hashed assets in `dist/` (HTML export template, highlight/marked chunks) vanish, so those features can fail in a tab until it restarts. Windows locks files a running omp holds: stock's own EBUSY/launcher repair applies, and an install that fails is only a failure here, kept in `OmpUpdateState.failure` and retried at the next check. After a successful update main re-detects omp (`refreshSystemOmp`), starts the stats server on it, restarts every idle local tab and every idle tab it hosts for paired devices on their own session file, and restarts a busy tab one tick after its turn ends (`src/main/omp-restart.ts`; `SidecarPool.restartOnNewOmp`, `LocalHostServices.restartOnNewOmp`; an `asleep` tab needs nothing); a composer draft lives in the window and survives. Those restarts make each tab's bridge answer `capabilities` again for the new version (`BridgeHealth` is keyed by omp version), so a feature the new omp lacks shows the existing `unsupported_omp` warning toast; success shows one quiet "omp updated to X" toast in one window. Prefs `ompAutoUpdate` (default on; a stored `false` turns it off; `OMP_AUTO_UPDATE_PREF`) and the manual "Update now" live in Settings › Updates & about › This computer (`OmpUpdateRows.tsx`); IPC (`omp-update:get-state`, `omp-update:now`, push `omp-update:state`, `omp-update:updated`) carries no version, command or path. Each computer updates its own omp under its own pref and always through its own `OmpUpdater`: on a paired host's tab, Settings › Updates & about shows the host's row (`HostOmpUpdateRow`: the host's `get_omp_update` check plus its `OmpUpdateState`), and its button only asks the host to run its updater (`host.ompUpdate.now` over the link, `host-omp-update:now` in the window; the state is `host.ompUpdate.get` / `host-omp-update:get`, and every change is pushed by the host as the `host.ompUpdate.changed` event and forwarded to the windows as `host-omp-update:state`), so the host's helpers are released, its supervisor and re-detection run, its tabs restart on the new omp and it never overlaps its own scheduled update; there is no `omp_update` RPC and no second `omp update` path. Scheduled checks run only in a packaged app outside `OMP_GUI_HEADLESS=1`, or in a test launch with both `OMP_GUI_TEST_INSTALLER` and `OMP_GUI_TEST_OMP_UPDATES=1` (the installer is then called `<installer> omp omp-update`); dev and test launches otherwise never check. `e2e/omp-auto-update.e2e.ts` covers it with the fixture's `OMP_GUI_TEST_LATEST_OMP` (what its `update --check` reports), `OMP_GUI_TEST_UPDATE_CHECK_LOG` (each check's first PATH entry), `OMP_GUI_TEST_VERSION_FILE`, `OMP_GUI_TEST_BOOT_LOG` and `OMP_GUI_TEST_UNAVAILABLE_AFTER_UPDATE`; `src/main/setup/omp-update-install.test.ts` runs the real `bun install -g` against a fake registry (`src/main/test-omp-registry.ts`) in a temp HOME and BUN_INSTALL, with a second omp first on PATH that must stay untouched.
  - The omp, tool CLI and extension updaters run on one loop, `UpdateScheduler` (`src/main/update-scheduler.ts`): it ticks at start in a scheduled launch and re-arms its timer from the end of each tick (a rejected tick does not end the schedule), queues passes one at a time (`enqueue`, dropped once disposed) and owns the back-off (`nextCheckDelay`, per item `settleBackoff`). omp backs off its timer by its own failure count and joins a running check instead of queueing a second one; the CLI and extension updaters tick every 4 h and back off per tool or row (a cancel is no failure). `updater.ts` (OMPdesk's own update, with its own 5-minute check) is not on it.
- Tool CLIs of connected accounts update themselves (`src/main/cli-tools/`, contract `src/shared/cli-update.ts`, pref `cliAutoUpdate` default on, its own switch in Settings › Updates & about › This computer because a user may pin a vendor CLI and OMPdesk needs no minimum version of one): at launch (after machine setup's first pass) and every 4 h, with omp's per-tool back-off (`CliUpdater`), main reads the providers with a stored credential from stock's own store (stock-helper `accounts.providers` = `discoverAuthStorage().credentials.list()`, ids only; `sdk` is listed under `providers` in `capabilities.ts`) and maps them to CLIs (`catalog.ts`: anthropic → `claude`, openai-codex/openai → `codex`, google-gemini-cli/google → `gemini`, cursor → `cursor-agent`, github-copilot → `copilot`, `gh` and its `gh-copilot` extension), plus what machine setup installs (gh, and Node in the managed tools dir only). It **never installs** a vendor CLI (Claude Code and Codex stay uninstalled by OMPdesk): only an installed one is listed and updated, by the owner found from its resolved executable path (`detect.ts classifyInstall`, the `ompUpdateEnv` idea): Homebrew formula/cask (`brew upgrade [--cask] <name>`), a global npm (`npm install -g --prefix <that prefix>`) or Bun package (`bun install -g` under that `BUN_INSTALL`), winget (`winget upgrade --id`), the vendor's own updater (`claude update`, which also owns a Claude Code in npm/Bun; `cursor-agent update`; `gh extension upgrade gh-copilot`), or machine setup's verified archive for the managed gh/Node. System (`/usr/bin`, Xcode CLT, `C:\Windows`), app-bundled (the Codex in ChatGPT.app) and admin-only installs are listed with their reason and never touched; no sudo. The check asks the owner first (`npm view`, `bun pm view`, `brew outdated --json=v2`, `winget list --upgrade-available`, the release feed), updates only when newer (`compareVersions`), runs it as `SetupService.runToolUpdate` under the setup supervisor (queued behind installs, never touching the setup items' state) and detects again (`updated`, or `not-updated`). Running-process rule, measured with a fake registry: npm and Bun replace the whole package dir (new directory inode; a running process keeps its open files, but a later `require` reads the new version's file and a file only the old version had is gone), and Homebrew removes the old keg, so on macOS an npm/Bun/Homebrew update of a Node CLI waits while a process of it runs (`ps`, `plans.ts waitsForIdle`/`runningIn`; result `deferred`, no back-off, retried at the next check); a native binary (gh), the vendors' own updaters and managed copies update at once; on Windows a file a running tool holds just fails the update, which is recorded and retried. A failure backs off like omp's. IPC `cliUpdate.getState/now(id?)/onState` carries tool ids only (an unknown id throws; no command, path or version crosses it); opening Settings rescans for tools installed since. Each computer updates its own tools and a paired host does its own. Test launches check on their own only with `OMP_GUI_TEST_INSTALLER` plus `OMP_GUI_TEST_CLI_UPDATES=1` (checks and updates run `<installer> cli-<tool> <method> check|update`; `e2e/cli-update.e2e.ts`); `OMP_GUI_TEST_BREW_DIRS` (PATH-style list, empty = none) replaces Homebrew's prefixes so a test is never answered by the developer's brew. Those prefixes (`/opt/homebrew/bin`, `/usr/local/bin`) are also looked in when the login-shell PATH omits them (`resolveBin` for gh/git, machine setup's gh detection, `cli-tools/detect.ts`), and the PR center and the GitHub center show the connect card (install, then sign in) instead of a download link for a missing gh on this computer; a paired host's tab keeps the link, because that gh is the host's.
  - Windows: a tool is found only as `<bin>.exe` or `<bin>.cmd` (npm's bare `<bin>` and `npm` beside them are POSIX shell scripts Windows cannot start), a `.cmd` runs through cmd.exe with the omp shim's quoting (`src/main/spawn.ts`: `launcherNames`, `findOnPath`, `spawnCommand`/`spawnArgs`/`needsShell`, the one PATH lookup and Windows spawn rule every external tool goes through), and since npm writes a global bin there as a cmd-shim instead of a link, `detect.ts cmdShimTarget` reads the package file the shim runs (`"%dp0%\node_modules\<package>\<bin>"`) so its npm prefix owns it. A Bun global's `.exe` + `.bunx` shim is not read and lists as `unknown-install`.
- Composer history uses stock `HistoryStorage` through guarded `history_recent`, `history_search`, and `history_record` bridge methods. Local tabs share history with this machine's terminal omp; remote tabs use the paired host's history and never import client prefs. Legacy `inputHistory` prefs migrate oldest-first on the first successful local bridge load and are then deleted. Stock `shouldSkipHistory` keeps secrets out of SQLite; renderer-only transient recall and Up/Down draft restore remain local. Compaction-limit commit/prefill uses stock `config/compaction-threshold` helpers through `compaction_limits`; typing never waits for bridge validation.
- Stats: `resources/gui-bridge/stats-host.ts` runs the user's omp-stats server (stock `omp stats` always opens a browser) and adds the paginated `/api/stats/requests` route stock lacks.
- Main-side stock operations without a tab run in three lazy Bun workers (`src/main/stock-helper.ts`, `resources/gui-bridge/stock-helper/host.ts`, request routing in `dispatch.ts`): `terminal.*`/`pty.*` go to the terminals worker (`host.ts terminals`, every request concurrent), `extensions.*` to the extensions worker (`host.ts extensions`, its own serial lane: a plugin upgrade runs `bun install` for minutes and must neither block session scans nor be recycled), everything else to the stock worker's serial lane (`host.ts stock`), so a hung stock call can never take a shell or a running upgrade with it. Area handlers register request names; resolve stock modules from `OMP_PACKAGE_ROOT`, never boot an agent or call a model. The session index uses read-only `listAllSessions`/`listSessionsReadOnly` and preserves only GUI chat labels, scope filtering, duplicate-id suppression and change notifications, which come from one recursive `fs.watch` of the sessions tree (`SessionIndex.start`; never a watch per directory, chokidar's way: on macOS each new one recreates libuv's single FSEvents stream and drops the session files written meanwhile). Deleting a session never starts another: the tab holding it closes as closing a tab does, and only then does the stock worker's `FileSessionStorage.deleteSessionWithArtifacts` remove the file (a file already gone counts as deleted, as in stock's own `dropSession`). The renderer closes its own window's tab first (`closeDeletedSession` in `stores/tabs.ts`: the window's last tab gives way to the empty start tab, `placeholder`, which has no sidebar row and no session file until its first run, and the closed-tab history forgets the session); `deleteSession` (`session-mutations.ts`) then calls `SidecarPool.closeSessionTab`, which closes a tab another window holds or a paired device's request reaches (its window hears `TAB_LIST_CHANGED` with `closed` and drops it the same way), refuses while that tab runs a turn or compacts, and waits until the omp that had the file open has exited (`#exiting`; `LocalHostServices` waits the same way for a device tab closed just before). There is no `drop_session`: stock `new_session` writes the new session's header at once (≥ 18.11), so the old leave-for-a-fresh-session delete left a "New session" row (and a header-only file) after every delete. The session picker and the sidebar list no session without content (`sessionHasContent`, stock's `filterSessionsForPicker` rule). stdin EOF and app quit stop all workers. Each request has its own 30 s timeout by default (`stockHelperRequest(method, params, { timeoutMs })` overrides it; armed before the worker starts) that fails only that request and sends `{cancel: id}`, so the worker skips it if it has not started (a mutation never runs after its caller was told it failed) and answers `withdrawn` in its turn. When a timed-out stock request still has no answer 30 s later, the lane is stuck: main logs it, fails what waits behind it, kills the stock worker and starts a fresh one on the next request. The terminals and extensions workers are never replaced for a slow request.
- A deleted session never comes back (`src/main/deleted-sessions.ts`): a successful `deleteSession` (`session-mutations.ts`, the one path for the sidebar, `/delete`, `ompdesk_session_stop {delete}` and a paired device's `sessions.delete` on its host) records a tombstone (session path, plus the host's `device` for a remote one) in `<userData>/deleted-sessions.json` (0600, newest 2000), and every path that could bring the session back asks it: `SidecarPool.acquire` returns null (a reopen from another window's closed-tab history, a child or any restore), `sanitizePersistedTabLayout` drops the saved tab outright (desktop `tabLayouts` and web `webTabLayouts`; a missing file **without** a tombstone still becomes a fresh tab in its folder), `TurnResume.launch` drops and purges its marker, a host's `tab.open` refuses it `session_deleted` for a device whose layout still names it (that device's `RemoteSidecar` emits `sessionDeleted`, and its pool closes the tab and records the tombstone under the host's id instead of showing an error tab), and `SessionIndex.list` never lists it even if a stray writer recreated the file. A never-written session (a first turn cut off before stock wrote its file, marker `prompt`, `#unwrittenSessionTab`) has no tombstone and still continues. In the renderer `sessionListFeed(...).deleteSession` drops the row from both scopes' feeds and their snapshot copies (`lib/snapshot-cache.ts`), and every global list read prunes the local list's snapshot of rows it lacks, so a session deleted on another device never paints again after a reload; ghost tabs (`lib/tab-snapshot.ts`) become real only when the live tab list names their session. Pinned by the tombstone cases in `session-mutations.test.ts`, `tab-layout.test.ts`, `turn-resume.test.ts`, `sidecar-pool.test.ts`, `remote/host-services.test.ts`, `remote/remote-sidecar.test.ts`, `session-index.test.ts`, `stores/session-list.test.ts`, and the relaunch over a stale layout and marker in `e2e/session-delete.e2e.ts`.
- Extensions of connected omp update themselves (`src/main/ext-update/`, contract `src/shared/extension-update.ts`, helper contract `resources/gui-bridge/stock-helper/extensions-contracts.ts`, pref `extensionAutoUpdate` default on, its own switch in Settings › Updates & about › This computer › Extensions): marketplaces, plugins, skills and MCP servers, on `CliUpdater`'s schedule (after machine setup's first pass, every 4 h, per-row back-off `nextCheckDelay`, failures recorded, deferred rows retried, `omp` being installed defers without back-off). `ExtensionUpdater` reads the inventory in the extensions worker (local files, no network) and works marketplaces → plugins → skills → MCP, one row at a time. Marketplaces: stock `updateMarketplace` (what `marketplace_action update` runs). Plugins: marketplace installs by stock `checkForUpdates`/`upgradePlugin`, npm and git plugins by `PluginManager.upgrade`; stock has no user pin for a marketplace plugin (the catalog's `ref`/`sha`/`version` is the catalog author's, upgrade follows it), and `upgrade(name)` reinstalls an npm plugin at the registry's latest whatever its recorded spec says, so OMPdesk reads `plugins/package.json` and calls upgrade only when the spec is what a bare install writes (measured, bun 1.4.2: `pkg`/`pkg@latest` → `^x.y.z`, `pkg@beta` → `^<tagged>`, exact and range specs verbatim; `^<installed>`, `latest`, `*` follow latest) and latest is strictly newer; an exact or ranged npm spec and a git commit/version-tag ref are pinned: checked, shown as "update available", never moved, not even by Update now; linked plugins are skipped; npm/git/linked plugins are user scope only (stock's `PluginManager` has no project scope), marketplace plugins also per project registry of the open local tabs' folders. Skills: stock records a source only for skills-registry installs (`skills.json`/`skills.lock.json`, `skillshare/manifest.ts`; `omp skill install|update|uninstall` take `@scope/name` registry specs and there is no git-source skill), so those update by `updateSkillPackages` (exact range = pinned; a version that ships scripts is never approved for the user: `needs-approval`), plugin-provided skills follow their plugin (one grouped row), plain local files are one "local, not updated" row. MCP servers (the user's config is never written): the built-in REA is listed as pinned to the OMPdesk release; URL servers are skipped (`remote`); an unpinned `npx`/`bunx`/`uvx` package is "follows latest at start" and its cache is not touched (measured with npm 11.17 against a fake registry: a bare `npx -y pkg` run after a new publish started the new version, so there is nothing to refresh, and the only way to warm a cache is to run the package, which would execute the server); a pinned `pkg@1.2.3` is checked against the registry and shown as an available update, the config untouched; a global binary is updated by its owner found with `cli-tools/detect.ts classifyInstall` plus pipx (`/pipx/venvs/<pkg>/`), uv tool (`/uv/tools/<pkg>/`) and pip (shebang python's `importlib.metadata`), through `SetupService.runToolUpdate` under the supervisor (system, bundled and admin-only installs listed and skipped, no sudo); running rule: a script (shebang) whose owner replaces package files in place (npm/Bun replace the package dir, Homebrew removes the keg, pipx/uv/pip rewrite site-packages) waits while a `ps`-visible process of it runs (`deferred running`, retried), a native binary updates at once; Windows has no scan, a held file fails and is retried. Applying to live tabs (`src/main/extension-reload.ts`, `SidecarPool.applyExtensionUpdate`, `LocalHostServices.applyExtensionUpdate`): one `ExtensionApplyPlan` per pass; an idle ready tab reloads now (bridge `extension_update.reload` = stock `reloadPluginState`, what `/reload-plugins` does), a plugin that ships extensions/tools/hooks (`activation: restart-required`, `extensions/activation-core.ts`) restarts the tab on its session file (`omp-restart.ts` pattern), an updated MCP package is reconnected (`extension_update.reconnect` = stock `MCPManager.reconnectServer`), a busy tab waits and applies one tick after its turn ends, `asleep` tabs need nothing. Every stock internal the worker uses is in `capabilities.ts` and `health.ts` `LOADERS`. IPC `extensionUpdate.getState/now(key?)/onState` carries row keys only. Each computer updates its own; a paired host's OMPdesk does its own. Test launches check only with `OMP_GUI_TEST_INSTALLER` plus `OMP_GUI_TEST_EXT_UPDATES=1` (MCP binary checks and updates run `<installer> cli-mcp-<name> <method> check|update`; `e2e/extension-update.e2e.ts`).
- Scheduled runs (Settings › Schedules; contract `src/shared/schedules.ts`, service `src/main/schedules.ts`): main checks every 30 s and starts stock `omp -p [--model] [--yolo]` in the schedule's folder, only while OMPdesk is open; times missed while it was closed are skipped, a start due while the previous run still goes is recorded as skipped. Stock omp has no scheduler. Each run goes through the setup supervisor (`spawnSupervised`, the prompt file as omp's stdin), so a Stop, a quit or a crash kills omp's whole tree. Schedules and the last 200 runs live in `<userData>/schedules.json`, run output in `<userData>/schedule-runs/<runId>.log`.
- Right panel (`components/layout/PanelContainer.tsx`): header = view title, Terminal/Changes/Preview/Files icons, ⋮ View options (Background tasks, Plan, Logs, Queue, Tree, Memory), close; a view's own actions sit in its toolbar row, never in the header. The terminal reuses stock `@oh-my-pi/pi-natives` `PtySession` inside the stock helper's terminals worker (`resources/gui-bridge/stock-helper/terminals.ts`, `src/main/terminal/`): no second native addon, one `TerminalService` per worker (overlapping first requests share its creation), stdin EOF kills every PTY; a paired host's tab gets the host's terminals (`terminal.request`/`terminal.shells` in `remote/host-services.ts`, disposed with the host tab). The agent reads a tab's terminals through the read-only `gui_terminal_read` host tool, registered with stock `set_host_tools` on every ready sidecar and answered in ipc.ts (`src/main/terminal/agent-tool.ts`). The preview is a main-managed `WebContentsView` (`src/main/preview/`) on a non-persistent partition with sandbox/contextIsolation, no preload, http(s) only, permissions/downloads/new windows refused, and remote-tab loopback URLs blocked. Background tasks read stock `inspectAsyncJob`/`cancelAsyncJob` through the bridge.
- Side panel Memory view (`panels/AiMemoryPanel.tsx`, `stores/ai-memory.ts`, `/memory`; the view id stays `memory`, so saved default-view prefs keep working): a glance, not a second browser: status and counts, an **Open Memory** button (Settings › Memory), "Open web console" (the local server's page), the recent pages (a row calls `useMemoryPageStore.openPage` and opens Settings › Memory › Pages) and the open handoffs (a row opens Handoffs). The palette's `memory` command (`cmd.memory`, "Memory") opens the page too. Everything else is the Memory page, next bullet. Stock's `MemoryBackend` (`memory.backend` and its mnemopi, hindsight, sharpshooter, local and autolearn rows, the `memory` model role row via `HIDDEN_ROLE_IDS`, the bridge's `get_memory_report`/`memory_*` methods) is gone from OMPdesk; stock keeps `memory.backend` `off` for RPC hosts on its own (`protocolDefault: ["rpc", "acp"]`), so nothing is held as a runtime default.
- Memory is ai-memory (src/main/ai-memory/, contract `src/shared/ai-memory.ts`, pref `aiMemory` default **on**, only a stored `false` turns it off; Settings › Memory, `settings/memory/MemorySection.tsx`, is the ONLY memory setting; the name in every UI string is just "Memory"/"메모리" — never "ai-memory", no link to the project's repo or docs, no credits in the UI: the licence notice lives in `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.txt` and `LICENSES/` only, and Settings › Updates & about › This computer lists it as one of OMPdesk's own components (`settings/memory/MemoryComponentRow.tsx`: version, status, "updated with OMPdesk", no update control). [ai-memory](https://github.com/akitaonrails/ai-memory) (MIT) is **built in**, not downloaded: `resources/omp-builtin/ai-memory/ai-memory.json` is the single version source (version and per-platform archive and binary SHA-256); `bun run sync:ai-memory` (packaging time; CI and release run it before packaging) downloads the pinned macOS arm64 and Windows x64 assets, checks the published `.sha256`, the pin and the extracted binary, and writes them to the gitignored `resources/ai-memory-bin/<platform>-<arch>/`, which each builder config ships as `<resources>/ai-memory-bin/` outside the asar (`scripts/after-pack.cjs` `checkAiMemory` and `packaging-config.test.ts` fail a package without the binary or with a different hash; `scripts/mac-sign.cjs` signs the nested binary with the app's certificate and the release's `Verify bundle` step verifies and runs it). `bun run bump:ai-memory [version|latest]` moves the pin and syncs; then run `bun run gen:licenses` and `bun scripts/gen-ai-memory-notices.ts` (`LICENSES/ai-memory-LICENSE`, `LICENSES/ai-memory-dependencies.txt`: its MIT text and every Rust crate it links, `licenses:check` fails when they lag the pin). The bundled copy updates only with OMPdesk (no CLI-tools updater entry).
  - The Memory page (Settings › Memory, `settings/memory/`; Settings sub-target `memory:<tab>`, the tab is the route): `MemorySection.tsx` is the shell (tabs Overview, Search, Pages, Handoffs, Sessions, Profile, Maintenance; the project selector, `resolveProject`: the user's pick, else the project whose name ends with the active session's folder name, else the latest; Memory off/starting/failed shows only Overview), each tab one component. **Overview**: switch, status, project, the project's counts, last capture (7-day activity and health in its tooltip), disk size, data folder, "Open web console" (`웹 화면 열기`, only while running; opens the local server's page) and its password, terminal omp, one privacy line with the full text as a tooltip. **Search**: full text in the project or across all projects (a toggle); a hit shows title, path, kind, tier, age, snippet and opens in Pages (hits carry no tier or time on the wire: main fills them from the projects' page lists). **Pages**: by kind (rules, decisions, gotchas, procedures, sessions folded, notes), a markdown reader, Edit (saved through the server's write path as a new version, keeping kind, tier, tags and pin), Pin, Helpful/Not helpful, Delete (confirm) and a **Deleted** group whose Restore reads the page back from the checkpoint the delete returned (`AiMemoryDeleted.rev`, kept in `useMemoryPageStore.deleted` and the snapshot cache); session pages are read-only. **Handoffs**: open first, the detail reads one without claiming it, Cancel (confirm) discards an open one. **Sessions**: recent captured sessions with observation counts; a session opens its raw observations read-only, 50 a page. **Profile**: the cross-project profile every session starts with, read-only; Edit opens the entry's page in Pages. **Maintenance**: the rule-based lint (writes no report), the retention sweep (Preview is a dry run; Run sweep is enabled only after a preview that found something, then confirms) and Delete all memory (typed word confirmation). Lists/detail are `MasterDetail` (list, then detail on the phone); the page's rows use `SettingsRow` `tipOnly` (every description is a tooltip, never an inline sentence; `data-setting-desc` must not appear), its row actions are quiet secondary buttons (danger only in the confirm dialog), and its close rows declare `--hit-gap-x/-y` (the tab bar 4 px, the reader's action row 4/16 px) so no button's touch reach covers a neighbour, and Korean calls a handoff "인계". The responsive audit covers every tab and each list → detail state (`settings-memory-<tab>`, `settings-memory-<tab>-detail` in `e2e/responsive-surfaces.ts`, answered by `dev-harness/fixture-memory.ts`); run it at 390 with `OMP_GUI_RESPONSIVE_ONLY=settings-memory`. Reads go through `useMemoryRead(key, read)` (`use-memory-read.ts`: the last known value at the first frame via `lib/last-read.ts`, read again in place, re-read when `useMemoryPageStore.version` bumps; never a spinner); every write goes through `useMemoryAction` (`memory-ui.tsx`: toast, then `bump()`). Strings: `locales/memory/<section>.ts` (en and ko side by side, aggregated by `locales/memory/index.ts`). Tests: one `*.test.tsx` per tab and the shell/component row/panel on `memory-test-harness.tsx` (a fake `window.omp.aiMemory`), `e2e/memory-page.e2e.ts` on the stateful fake server (palette → Overview → search → open → edit and save → delete → restore → read and cancel a handoff → session paging → the side panel), the rig shared with `ai-memory.e2e.ts` is `e2e/fixture/ai-memory-rig.ts`.
  - The Memory client (`src/main/ai-memory/client.ts`, one `AiMemoryClient`, token read in main, never in the renderer): reads are the `/api/v1` REST routes plus `/admin/status`, `/admin/checkpoints`, `/admin/profile/*`; writes go through the server's own write path, never its files: `/admin/write-page`, `/admin/delete-page` (returns `pre_checkpoint`; the client falls back to the head checkpoint read before the call), `/admin/restore-page`, `/admin/lint` (always `dry_run` + `no_llm`), `/admin/forget-sweep`, and the two actions the REST surface lacks, `memory_feedback` and `memory_handoff_cancel`, as MCP `tools/call` on `/mcp` (stateless JSON, `Accept: application/json, text/event-stream`). Twelve IPC channels were added for the page (`AI_MEMORY_OVERVIEW|PAGES|SESSIONS|OBSERVATIONS|PROFILE|LINT|SWEEP|WRITE_PAGE|DELETE_PAGE|RESTORE_PAGE|FEEDBACK|CANCEL_HANDOFF`): reads are web-`workspace`, writes `host-admin` in `IPC_POLICY`; for the agent (`app-channels.ts`) deleting a page (`memory.delete_page`) and the sweep (`memory.sweep`) are `sensitive` catalog actions beside `memory.purge`, everything else is `never` (the agent has its own memory tools). `ipc.ts` validates every argument (a page path is never absolute or `..`).
  - Which binary (`binary.ts`): the shipped file is never run in place (its path ends up in a launchd unit, a Windows logon entry and omp's MCP entry, and a Windows update replaces the install folder under a running server): `stageBinary` verifies it and copies it once to `<tools>/ai-memory/<version>/` (atomic rename; old versions pruned after wiring). The shipped file is accepted when its bytes are the pin's `binarySha256` (Windows ships it unsigned; a dev build's synced copy) or, on macOS only, when its signature satisfies `AI_MEMORY_REQUIREMENT` = `identifier "ai-memory" and certificate root = H"<scripts/mac-signing.json certificateSha1>"` (`codesign --verify --strict -R`, `codeRequirementProblem`): packaging re-signs the nested binary with OMPdesk's certificate (`mac-sign.cjs`) after `after-pack.cjs` checked the pin, so a release's bytes never equal the pin, and any other valid signature (a system binary, an ad-hoc re-signed copy) fails the requirement. A package signed ad-hoc (no certificate) therefore has no memory, by design. The staged copy is trusted only by bytes, never by its signature: reused when it is the pin's, or byte-identical to the shipped file verified at that launch; otherwise replaced. release.yml's `Verify bundle` checks the nested binary against the same requirement on main. (0.38.0/0.38.1 compared the signed bytes with the pin, failed every launch and logged nothing, so memory never started on a Mac; pinned by `binary.test.ts`, `service.test.ts` and `e2e/packaged-ai-memory.e2e.ts`.) A new app version stages a new directory, so ai-memory's own `setup` sees a changed unit and restarts the service on the new binary. A user's own ai-memory (PATH, `~/.local/bin`, Homebrew, the Windows script's folders) is used only when it is NEWER than the shipped one (the data's schema migrates forward only); equal or older ones are left alone.
  - Runtime log: `AiMemoryService` writes `<userData>/logs/gui-runtime.jsonl` lines with source `ai-memory` (its injected `log`): `launch` {enabled, automatic, platform, arch, bundledDir, override} once per start, `binary` {source, version, bin}, `phase` {phase, step, detail, from, fromStep} on every change of phase, step or detail, and `failed` {step, detail, stack} for each failure (a poll's too). `start()` never rejects: anything it throws becomes the `failed` phase, which Settings shows with its reason and Try again.
  - Lifecycle (`service.ts`, `AiMemoryService`; started after the first tab; only a packaged app or `OMP_GUI_AI_MEMORY_BIN=<binary>` changes the machine, a dev build only reports; `OMP_GUI_AI_MEMORY_NO_SERVICE=1` passes `--no-service` for tests; `OMP_GUI_AI_MEMORY_UNIT_PIDFILE=<file>` stands in for the OS service in the fake-binary e2e): the server is **ai-memory's own per-user service** (launchd LaunchAgent / Windows logon entry, installed by `ai-memory setup`), not an OMPdesk child, because that is its documented local single-user setup. OMPdesk owns only its own wiring and **never writes ambient configuration**: nothing goes to `~/.omp/agent`, no other agent's config is touched, `uninstall --apply` is never run (it removes every agent's wiring ai-memory finds). On: `setup --plan --json --agent omp` (data dir, `fresh`), on a fresh data dir `init` plus the one config change below, then `setup --yes --agent omp` with `PI_CODING_AGENT_DIR` pointed at a scratch folder (`<userData>/ai-memory/omp-ext.setup`, deleted afterwards): `setup` cannot install the service without wiring an agent, so its omp wiring lands in the scratch folder, and the shared skills it also writes to `~/.agents/skills` are taken back when this run created them (`terminal.ts removeNewSharedSkills`; ones that were there stay). A lone `problem: self-check` is not fatal, the `/healthz` poll decides. Every `setup --yes` runs **isolated** (`SpawnSpec.isolated`, `setup/supervisor.ts isolatedLaunch`), still under the setup supervisor (it dies with the app): the service host it starts outlives OMPdesk by design, and on Windows a child inherits every inheritable handle of its parent (libuv's spawn and Rust's `Command` both pass `bInheritHandles`, and Electron keeps the main process's stdio inheritable), so a plain spawn handed the host the app's pipes to the supervisor and the pipes of whatever launched the app, open for as long as the service runs (v0.38.0: the packaged first-launch e2e's worker teardown timed out; Sysinternals Handle showed `service-host --supervise` and `serve` holding `\Device\NamedPipe\uv\*-<main pid>` and `…-<Playwright worker pid>`, ci.yml run 39123278044). Those launcher pipes reach the supervisor as stray handles no Node or Electron API can name, so clearing the supervisor's own std handles would not do. Isolated, Bun starts the command (`bun -e` with `bun:ffi` calling kernel32's `CreateProcessW`: no PowerShell, nothing compiled at run time) with an explicit handle list (`PROC_THREAD_ATTRIBUTE_HANDLE_LIST`: NUL as stdin, a temp log file as stdout/stderr, `CREATE_NO_WINDOW`), and the supervisor passes the log on as its own output once the command exited. Bun is a machine-setup item a fresh Windows machine may still be installing, so the wiring depends on it (dep `bunPresent`, `findBun`): a pass without Bun runs no command and fails with "Waiting for Bun…", and the setup's state reporting Bun present (`index.ts` calls `AiMemoryService.bunInstalled()` on every such state) runs that pass again, once — memory sets itself up on the first launch with no relaunch or Try again. On macOS launchd starts the server (`launchctl bootstrap`), so it inherits nothing from `setup`; there the log file only stands in for the supervisor's pipes. Then ai-memory's OWN generators fill the **app-owned extension root** `<userData>/ai-memory/omp-ext/` (`root.ts`): `install-hooks --agent omp --apply --config-file <root>/index.ts` (the omp extension), `install-mcp --client omp --apply --config-file <root>/.mcp.json` and `install-skills --target-dir <root>/skills`, produced next to the root and swapped in only when the bytes differ (nothing is hand-written; no `.bak` clutter, nothing touched on an unchanged launch). omp reads a package root's `index.ts`, `.mcp.json` and `skills/` (stock `omp-extension-roots.ts`), exactly like `resources/omp-builtin/rea`. The service ownership (`unit.ts`: launchd plist `com.github.akitaonrails.ai-memory` / HKCU `Run\ai-memory` runs a binary under `<tools>/ai-memory` or the binary in use) is stored as pref `aiMemoryServiceOwned`; a service OMPdesk did not start (someone else's unit, or a server already answering) gets `setup --no-service` and is never stopped. Off: the pref is stored first (`extensionArgs()` returns `[]` at once), the root is deleted, and the server stops only when OMPdesk started it and no other client uses it: `uninstall` WITHOUT `--apply` is a read-only listing, any line that is not a managed skill means another agent (or the terminal omp) is wired, an unreadable listing counts as in use; stopping is `launchctl bootout` + deleting the plist on macOS, deleting the `Run` value and `taskkill` of the PIDs in `<data>/service-host.json` on Windows (never by image name: every live omp has an `ai-memory mcp-bridge`). The data stays. Delete all memory: the same stop (owned units only), then the `wiki/ db/ raw/` folders are deleted after `/healthz` stops answering (`uninstall --purge-data` refuses while any `ai-memory` process runs), then on again (or, with memory off and other clients using the server, `setup` brings it back). A poll every 60 s (`/healthz`, `/admin/status` for the counts, the root's files) heals by re-running setup and the generators (twice down in a row, or the root gone; once per 10 minutes). Defaults stay ai-memory's zero-LLM path (no provider, no dream) and its single-user cross-project profile; the one change: a **fresh** config gets `embedding_provider = "none"` (`config.ts`), since the default local embeddings fetch an 87 MB model from huggingface.co and hold ~355 MB resident (measured 3.1.1: 39 MB without); search stays full-text, entity and graph, and removing that line turns hybrid search on. An existing config is never touched.
  - Coverage: every omp OMPdesk spawns gets `-e <root>` while memory is on and the root is complete (`AiMemoryService.extensionArgs()`, read at each spawn: `sidecar-factory.ts` `aiMemoryExtensionArgs` for local tabs and the tabs a host runs for a paired device, `schedules.ts` `ompRunLauncher` `extensionArgs` for scheduled `omp -p` runs; subagents are children of those omps). The terminal `omp` CLI is separate and unaffected unless the user asks. The short-lived internal omps that pass `--no-extensions` (rename, model setup) capture nothing, on purpose. The brief is the open handoff of the folder, injected as a hidden `ai-memory-handoff` custom message at a session's first prompt. A paired host's own OMPdesk wires its own.
  - Terminal omp (Settings › Memory › "Also use in the terminal omp", `AiMemoryService.setTerminal`, pref `aiMemoryTerminal`): runs ai-memory's own `setup --yes --agent omp` against omp's real agent dir (MCP entry `mcp.json`, extension `extensions/ai-memory-omp.ts`; its skills go to the shared `~/.agents/skills`), and each later launch re-runs it so the entry follows an app update's new staged binary. ai-memory has no per-agent uninstall (`uninstall` has `--only hooks|mcp|instructions|skills`, never an agent), so the reverse (`terminal.ts removeTerminalWiring`) removes exactly what `setup` added and nothing else: the extension only when it carries ai-memory's generated header, the `mcpServers["ai-memory"]` entry only when it runs an `ai-memory` binary or the `/mcp` URL (other servers and every other key stay; the file goes only when nothing is left). The shared skills stay (other agents read them). While omp's agent dir holds the generated extension, `extensionArgs()` leaves the root out so a session never captures twice. Needs memory on.
  - Migration (once, in the first `enable` pass with pref `aiMemoryTerminal` unset): an earlier build wrote ai-memory's extension and MCP entry globally into `~/.omp/agent` with the staged binary. When the `ai-memory` MCP entry runs a binary under `<tools>/ai-memory` or the binary in use (`legacyOwned`), exactly those two entries are removed (`removeTerminalWiring`) and the pref is stored `false`; wiring that runs someone else's ai-memory is the user's own and stays (it then counts as the terminal's). The shared skills the old `setup` left in `~/.agents/skills` stay: they cannot be told from another agent's.
  - Measured (3.1.1, omp 18.12.1, claude-sonnet-5-5, `omp -p`): the main request carries +3.5k tokens of MCP tool schemas and +0.36k of skills in every session (11.85k tokens baseline; prompt-cached after the first request) and, only when an open handoff exists, +0.4k at the first prompt; the extension adds none. Idle server: 0.0% CPU, 39 MB resident without embeddings; disk 5.7 MB after three sessions (the database is 5.2 MB of that, the wiki 0.2 MB). Prompts and tool results are scrubbed of vendor keys (`sk-`, `ghp_`), bearer tokens, private keys, `*_KEY=…` env shapes and URL passwords before storage (tested); free text such as "my password is …" is stored as typed, the assistant's replies are not recorded, and `[capture] ignore_paths` in a `.ai-memory.toml` covers file and shell tools only (prompts are always recorded unless capture runs in allowlist mode).
  - Tests: `src/main/ai-memory/*.test.ts` (staging, selection, client, service with a fake runner and a fake unit — off never lists or applies an uninstall-all, the root is loaded only while on, ownership, other clients, purge, the terminal button, the migration — plus `terminal`, `unit`, `root`, IPC; on macOS the real codesign refuses `/usr/bin/true`, an ad-hoc copy named `ai-memory` and an ad-hoc re-signed copy of the synced release), `src/main/setup/runner.test.ts` (an isolated command's detached descendant holds none of the supervisor's pipes, argv quoting), `src/main/sidecar-factory.test.ts` and `schedules.test.ts` (the `-e` seams), `e2e/ai-memory.e2e.ts` on the fake binary `e2e/fixture/fake-ai-memory.ts` (a stateful fake server since the Memory page) (a planted Claude Code wiring survives on/off, the root, the terminal button, delete-all, migration, Settings, the panel; CI: `gh workflow run ci.yml -f win_e2e=true -f win_e2e_grep=ai-memory`, and `-f installer_check=true` for the packaged setup), `e2e/first-launch-window.e2e.ts` (`@smoke`, Windows: the packaged app sets the real service up, then ⌘Q's quit and an update's quit must close the app's pipes within 30 s and leave no supervisor, `ai-memory` command or omp of OMPdesk's running, while the service may stay; ci.yml's win-e2e packages `dist/win-unpacked` for it and, with Sysinternals Handle, lists which process holds which libuv pipe; `-f win_e2e_grep=first-launch`), `e2e/packaged-ai-memory.e2e.ts` (`@smoke`, macOS: the packaged `dist/mac-arm64` app, or `OMP_GUI_E2E_APP`, in a private HOME and userData stages the shipped binary re-signed with OMPdesk's certificate, reaches `ready` with its real launchd service, creates the extension root and logs `launch`/`binary`/`phase: ready`; skipped for an ad-hoc package and while the user's own label or port 49374 is in use, ends with `uninstall --apply --yes` in the private HOME), the opt-in `e2e/real-ai-memory.e2e.ts` (`OMP_GUI_REAL_WORK=1`: the real release in a temp HOME with its real service, the real omp reads the root's MCP entry, two sessions in one folder, the second gets the first's handoff, off removes the launchd service).
- Side panel Subagent view (`panels/SubagentPanel.tsx`): a sidebar subagent row dragged onto a session pane (`omp:subagent-drag`, taken by `SplitWorkspace`) or its menu's "Open in side panel" sets `useUiStore.panelTab = "subagent"` + `panelSubagent {tabId, agentId}`; the view reads the transcript and steers through the owning tab's runtime. It is not in `PANEL_TABS` (default-view setting, `panel.*` shortcuts); panes stay main-owned omp tabs. The transcript (`SubagentTranscript.tsx`, shared with `SubagentTranscriptSheet`) is the main transcript's own components, never a second renderer: `buildHistoryRows`/`reuseTranscriptRows` rows drawn by `MessageBubble`, `ProcessGroup` (the compact work card, or per-message tool cards by `transcriptDetail`) and `ReadGroupCard` under `.omp-transcript-editorial.omp-subagent-conversation` (`styles/subagent-view.css`: the editorial insets follow the panel's width by container query; code and tables scroll inside their own block). They run in a derived `SessionRuntime` of the owning tab with private tools/messages/session stores (`hydrateMessages` from the loaded pages, `turnIsLive` while the agent runs), so the owner's streams and goal never reach the cards. `use-subagent-transcript.ts` reads by byte offset and follows a running agent (a progress frame or a 4 s poll; a page with nothing new keeps the same array), `use-stick-to-bottom.ts` is ChatStream's gesture-vetoed follow plus "Jump to latest". The header is one line (provider mark + agent, status, tokens, clock, open full / stop / copy); the steer box reuses `.omp-composer-box` and `.omp-composer-action`. Tests: `SubagentPanel.test.tsx`, `SidebarSubagents.test.tsx`; `e2e/subagent-view.e2e.ts` (`fixture:subagents:transcript` / `:append`, light/dark × en/ko × 380/520 px, 320 px overflow check; `OMP_GUI_SUBAGENT_SHOTS=<dir>` writes captures).
- Sidebar families (`layout/sidebar/SidebarSubagents.tsx` the group and chip, `SidebarFamilyRow.tsx` the one-line row, `sidebar-family.ts` the pure rules, `stores/sidebar-subagents.ts` the open state): everything derived from a session shows together under its row as one family, one indent level in — its subagents (the tab runtime's roster) and the sessions its agent opened with `ompdesk_session_open`, in a worktree or not, whatever their folder. Provenance: `AgentControlRuntime.#spawn` calls `SidecarPool.setTabOpener(tabId, callerTabId)` (not for an Autopilot child, which keeps its run's row); the tab entry carries `openedBy` (the opener's tab id) and `openedBySession` (its session file) on `IpcTabInfo`, and the saved tab layout keeps the file as `PersistedTabDescriptor.openedBy` (a restored tab's id is new, the file is what finds the opener again; the opener's tab being closed leaves the file in place). The renderer matches by tab id first, then by file (`openerOfSessions`): an opened session whose opener is listed leaves its folder's group (so the "Parallel work" group lists only sessions without a live opener), one opened by an opened session goes under the first of them, and a cycle nests nothing. A child is ONE line (`.omp-family-row`, 28 px; `--sc-row-touch`, 36 px on a phone): a 16 px kind glyph (subagent `Bot`, worktree session `GitBranch`, plain session `MessageSquare`), the title (a subagent's: the `Title:` line of its ORIGINAL assignment, else stock's label, else its role, never a later message and never the `Title:` label — see "Subagent titles" below; an opened session's: `agentTaskTitle`/`sessionDisplayTitle`, the `Title:` line else its first line, via `assignmentHeadline`/`stripTitlePrefix` in `subagent-title-contracts.ts`), one status token (running pulse, waiting ⚠, done ✓, failed ✕, paused) and the age; the status in words, folder, model and progress are the tooltip. The parent row's chip reads "🤖5 · ⑂4" with the running count as a filled pill. **A family is folded by default and NEVER opens or folds by itself** (v0.38.1 on a phone: in a long orchestration session every follow-up message to a subagent is a new run, so the old open-on-start edge, `noteFamilyActive`, re-opened it all the time over the person's fold; removed together with the fold-on-finish): running, finishing or starting children change only the chip, whose running count is the filled pill and which takes a warning tint (`data-family-waiting`, `sidebar.family.waiting` in its summary) while a child waits for the person. The only thing a folded family shows unasked is a child that waits for an answer or approval (state `waiting`: a stale registration, or an opened session `awaitingInput`): that one row shows alone under the folded parent (`data-sidebar-surfaced`, flat, no connector for the others) and leaves again when it is answered; the rest stays folded and the store's `expanded` is untouched. The person's own toggle (chip, session row click, ←/→, "expand/collapse all", the dock's reveal) is the only writer of `expanded`, remembered per session in memory; with no child at all the entry is dropped. **Finished children are never listed** (the user's rule: a finished child is meaningless in the sidebar): a family shows only children that are live or need a look — running, waiting (ask/approval), failed or stale, or an opened session with work left to review (`problem`: failed ending, unmerged commits, uncommitted work) — and a finished subagent (completed/aborted/cancelled, `isFoldedSubagent`) or a finished opened session that landed clean (`status` `done`, `isShownOpened`) has NO row and NO "n done" fold (no `sidebar.family.done` string, no `data-family-done`). The chip counts only shown children (`familyCounts`) and is absent when none show; the remembered fold state applies to what is left, and a family left with nothing is dropped from the store. Nothing is deleted: a finished subagent stays in its session's own Subagent panel and the work board, a finished opened session stays in the session picker/search. "Hide this session's subagents" hides only the subagents. **Opened sessions are children: same lifecycle and visibility as subagents.** One derived status per opened session (`openedChildStatus` in `sidebar-family.ts`, a rule table over `ChildFacts`: waiting, running, problem = finished with an error/abort ending or unmerged commits/uncommitted work, done = finished; `CHILD_TOKEN` maps it to the row token, a problem is the ✕) is what the row token, the chip's counts and the fold read. A `done` opened session is not listed (`FamilyOpened.status`); running, waiting, problem and not-yet-run ones stay rows. A session main closed after its work landed has no tab, so provenance is the pref `agentClosedChildren` (`ClosedChild`: file, opener's file, title, folder; read by `stores/closed-children.ts`, `openerOfSessions(…, closedOpeners)`): it is not listed (done); the record still finds its opener, so a reopened one keeps nesting there and a stored one opens from the session picker (a pick; in `cwd` = the checkout it came from when its worktree was removed). A session no tab holds is never a row of its own or a "Parallel work" group: its opener is found, in order, by the tab's `openedBySession` file (the opener needs no tab: a stored opener row carries the family too), the closed record, then the agent brief its indexed first message holds (`agentMessageSender` reads the `from` of `<ompdesk-agent-message from="…">`; `briefOpenerOf`: the newest session with that title created before the child; an Autopilot child keeps its run's row whatever its brief says). One without an opener (the record or brief names none that is listed, or only its `omp/agent/…` worktree says it was opened by an agent) is listed only while it needs a look (`SidebarLooseChildren`, `data-sidebar-loose-children`: plain family-style rows closing its project's main-checkout group, the recent list has the same at its end; a project with nothing else to show has no group); a finished one is not listed; a live tab keeps its row. The row title is `sessionDisplayTitle` else the tab's `task` (`IpcTabInfo.task`, the task's `Title:` line) else the closed record's title, so an opened session never reads "(untitled)" while its index entry has no first message yet. The same children are listed on the work board ("Opened sessions" section, `opened-children.ts` + `hooks/use-opened-children.ts`: title, status token, current step and clock from the review item main pushes, branch and merge state; waiting first with the warning tint), counted by the phone strip (⑂ count and its running/waiting in the label; the expanded panel is the board), the composer's background cue (running ones), the dock island ("Sessions" segment) and the turn-resume log (`opened`). "Needs review" (`needsReview` in `work-board.ts`) lists only finished exceptions: conflict, failed ending, unmerged commits, uncommitted work; a running session is never there. A phone bounds the hit areas with `--hit-gap` (`mobile.css`). Tests: `SidebarSubagents.test.tsx` (no auto-open, the waiting child alone, the remembered toggle, no done row), `sidebar-family.test.ts`, `Sidebar.test.tsx` (nesting by opener, folded by default, finished children not listed), `sidecar-pool.test.ts` and `tab-layout.test.ts` (provenance persistence), `touch-targets.test.ts` (row sizes); `e2e/family-fold.e2e.ts` on the dev harness (phone and desktop, `OMP_GUI_FAMILY_FOLD=<dir>` writes folded/open captures, light/dark ko).
- Subagent titles (`subagentPrimaryLabel` in `panels/subagent-graph.ts`, store rules in `stores/subagents.ts`, `unwrapStockPrompt` in `subagent-title-contracts.ts`): a subagent's row, panel header and board card read the `Title:` line of its ORIGINAL assignment, else stock's own label (`description`), else the first line of that assignment, else the role name; never a later message. Where stock's frames differ per run (`task/executor.ts`, `modes/rpc/rpc-subagents.ts`): the first run's progress carries `task` = the assignment wrapped by `prompts/system/subagent-user-prompt.md` ("Complete assignment thoroughly:" + blank line) and `assignment` = the bare text; every later run of the same subagent — **(a)** a follow-up message to an idle subagent (`attachIrcWakeTurnMonitor`) and **(b)** the revival after a relaunch (`restoreSession` → `runSubagentFollowUpTurn`, message = `session_init.task`) — reports its own message as `task`, NO `assignment`, no label (`handleProgress` in stock's RPC registry then overwrites the roster's `assignment` with undefined too). (a)'s `task` is the follow-up's plain text ("Your mac check in 7e3ec34a…" in v0.38.1), (b)'s is the wrapped original ("Complete assignment thorou…"). So: `assignment` is sticky in the store (a frame without one keeps the row's), `unwrapStockPrompt(task)` recovers it from (b)'s wrapped task (store and label both), a plain `task` is never a label, and a roster snapshot taken after a follow-up (no assignment, no label) reads the first user message of the subagent's transcript once (`recoverAssignment`, `get_subagent_messages` then `get_subagent_transcript`). Pinned by `stores/subagent-titles.test.ts` with stock-shaped frames.
- Work board (`layout/work-board/WorkBoard.tsx`, rules in `layout/work-board/work-board.ts`, look in `styles/work-board.css`, design record in `docs/work-board-design.md`; mounted per session pane by `SplitWorkspace`): a pane with at least 300 px (`WORK_BOARD_MIN_SPARE`) beside its transcript column (`workBoardFits`, measured on the pane by ResizeObserver, constants pinned to components.css by `work-board.test.ts`) docks this session's live state in that margin — one status line with the todo progress bar, then waiting questions/approvals first (warning wash; the ⌥B `board.focus` key jumps to them; the only live region), the turn's last steps (with the turn meter's tok/s beside its elapsed time), running subagents (model mark, step, tokens) and background jobs, todos (finished ones folded), goal meters, and the last turn's duration and +/− once idle; section folds persist (pref `workBoardCollapsed`); the head also holds `AccountLimit.tsx` (the subscription account in use and its tightest usage window from `get_usage` through `useUsageReports`, warning from 80 %, danger at the limit, hidden without usage windows) and rows leave through `usePresence`. While it has a section the pane carries `data-work-board`, so the transcript scroller and composer shift left by `--omp-board-reserve`, and `data-work-board-running` / `-todo` hide the dock strips (`[data-workspace-island]`, `[data-dock-card="todo"]`) that say the same. It reads the existing per-tab stores and `get_jobs`/`get_goal`, derives nothing, never shows beside the side panel, and is off with the pref `workBoard` (default on; Settings › Appearance, command palette, its own ✕). Cost rules (measured with `e2e/flow-performance.e2e.ts`'s `daily` flow): its only running animation is the head pulse (rows fade in and out once and otherwise stay still: each animated row cost the GPU process about 2 % of a core while the transcript streamed, so a busy board doubled it against v0.31.0), `useNowTick` stops while the window is hidden, and `get_usage` and `get_jobs` run one poll per tab through `hooks/use-tab-poll.ts` (each joining surface reads once on mount): `use-usage-reports.ts` serves the board's account row, the sidebar usage lines, Stats › Usage, the Settings model tables and accounts and the usage-limit notice; `use-background-jobs.ts` serves the board, the dock island and the Background tasks view. Tests: `WorkBoard.test.tsx`, `work-board.test.ts`, `use-usage-reports.test.tsx`, `now-tick.test.tsx`, e2e `work-board.e2e.ts` (the fake omp's `fixture:board`). **Where it cannot dock (a phone, a portrait tablet) the status strip under the header (`WorkBoardChip`) expands in place**: a tap grows an inline panel (`data-work-strip-panel`, same wrapper `.omp-work-strip` as the strip, absolutely placed right under it) over the top of the transcript, which is neither pushed nor dimmed — no Sheet, no scrim or backdrop element, so the transcript stays visible and takes taps below it. The panel holds the board itself (waiting ask with its jump to the card first, steps, running subagents, jobs, todos with finished folded, goal), capped at `50dvh` and scrolling inside (`.omp-work-strip-body`), inset like the strip so safe areas and the scrollbar gutter hold. It collapses on a second tap of the strip, a swipe up (≥ 32 px) on the strip or the 16 px grip at the panel's foot, the back gesture (`useBackLayer`), a pointerdown anywhere outside (a document listener, so the tap still reaches what it hit), or a tap on a board row (`button.omp-work-board-row`; its own action runs first: open the subagent, focus the card); section fold toggles and review's approve/discard keep it open. The strip stays and its chevron flips (`aria-expanded`, `aria-controls`). Nothing is remembered: every mount starts collapsed. Motion is height (`omp-strip-in`/`-out`, the exit keeps the panel mounted 160 ms) and the chevron's transform only; reduced motion and `html[data-gpu="software"]` make both instant. Pinned by `WorkBoardChip.test.tsx` and the `mobile-shell` e2e (panel under the header, ≤ 50 % high, hit test below it lands on the transcript, second tap and outside tap collapse). The desktop board and tablets that dock it are unchanged.
- Work card head, findings and steps (`chat/ExecutionGroup.tsx`, pure rules in `chat/work/work-checks.ts`, `work-findings.ts`, `work-steps.ts`; design record and the A/B/C comparison in `docs/work-card-design.md`): a finished card's fact line names what the run's shell commands checked instead of counting them — `checkKind` reads the command's own words (test runners, `tsc`/`check:types`, biome/eslint/ruff, `build`), the last run of each kind decides (`summarizeChecks`: "128 tests passed", "types OK", "build failed"; a non-zero `details.exitCode` fails even without stock's error flag; interrupted and unanswered runs are skipped and still count as commands), the test count is the runner's last printed summary (`testCounts`: vitest, jest, bun, pytest, cargo, go), and every other command keeps "N commands". Under the head (LiveStepPeek's `findings` slot, finished cards only, so streaming never reflows; a running card has no row) one row of labeled chips (class `omp-work-finding`; `omp-work-chip` is the composer's work chip), derived by `deriveFindings` from the ledger alone, no model call: **Error** (`data-work-chip="error"`, danger, the failing check's cause — `firstErrorLine` over stock's bash result text, `WorkCommandOutcome.text`, the last 8,000 characters kept only for checks and failed commands, ANSI and runner chrome skipped — else a failed non-check command's cause (`firstFailure`), else the word "Error" for a failed call of any other tool), **Tests ✓ N** (`tests`, the last test run passed, N the runner's count), **Open Changes** (`changes`, `setPanelTab("diff")`), **Preview** (`preview`, a bash result `devServerUrl` (`lib/panel-auto-open.ts`) read an address from, kept as `WorkCommandOutcome.previewUrl`; `openPreview`) and a copy icon (`CopyButton`, `workSummaryText`: head facts and time, cause, changed files with +/-, checks, preview address). Error and Tests open the steps and show their call: `show(toolCallId)` sets a `StepReveal` the body reads through `StepRevealContext`, `revealStep` (`work/reveal-step.ts`) finds the card by `data-tool-call-id` (`ToolCard`'s root; the ledger carries each main-agent command's entry key as `WorkCommandOutcome.toolCallId`, a subagent's none), opens it and scrolls it to the middle, retrying for 2 s because a big card mounts in slices. The opened finished card (`StepGroups.tsx`, `groupSteps`; a running card keeps `ProcessMessages`, one bubble per message, so the live list never rearranges; `transcriptDetail` full mode has no work card and is untouched) lines consecutive calls of one stock intent (`ToolCallContent.intent`, then the arguments' `intent`/`i`; a generic "Running requested command" names nothing) under one closed row ("Checking the types · 3 steps"), folds consecutive collapsible reads into `ReadGroupCard`, and every call keeps its own `ToolCard`; a group (or read group, `forceOpen`) holding a failed call opens by itself and again when a finding reveals a call inside it, only thinking may sit between two calls of one intent. Pinned by `work-checks.test.ts`, `work-findings.test.ts`, `work-steps.test.ts`, `work-replay.test.ts` (the recorded `real-advisor-frames.json` and stock's bash envelope through the ledger and rules), `ExecutionGroup.checks.test.tsx`, `ExecutionGroup.chips.test.tsx`, `StepGroups.test.tsx`, `e2e/event-replay.e2e.ts`; captures with `OMP_GUI_WORK_CARD_SHOTS=<dir>` (`e2e/work-card-captures.e2e.ts`: pass and `OMP_GUI_TEST_WORK_TYPES_FAIL=1`, en/ko × light/dark, wide and narrow window, plus the card opened; `e2e/work-card-phone.e2e.ts`: the paired phone page at 390, ko light/dark, collapsed and opened; both run the fake omp's `OMP_GUI_TEST_WORK_RICH=1` variant, which gives the search steps one intent, turns the eval step into a test run and the last step into a dev server; `OMP_GUI_WORK_CARD_OUT`/`_LABEL` capture another build as the before).
- Split view and picking a session (`stores/tabs.ts`, rules in `shared/split-layout.ts`; main owns tabs and the pool, the renderer owns what the window shows): a window shows one session alone or one split of 2–4 panes (`split`), and remembers the other splits it has shown (`rememberedSplits`: layouts only, no focus). A session is in at most one split, on screen or remembered. Three rules, all in `viewAfterPick`, and every way of picking a session goes through `switchTab`: ① a pane of the split on screen only takes focus, the layout stays; ② a session outside the split leaves it and shows alone, full width, and the split on screen is remembered — it never replaces or opens into a pane; ③ a member of a remembered split brings that split back exactly as it was (sessions, positions, sizes) with the picked session focused, and the split that was on screen is remembered in turn. Sidebar rows, ⌘1–⌘9, ⇧⌘[ ⇧⌘] / ⌘PageUp/PageDown when no split is on screen (with one they cycle its panes), the tab overview, the ⌃⇥ switcher and the notification/prompt-card focus are all picks. The session picker (⇧⌘F) and the sidebar's stored-session rows open a session no tab holds as a new tab, which is a pick of an outside session (`switchSessionNow` never re-roots a pane's tab while a split shows; `activeTabUntouched` is false then). Dropping a session on a pane edge (`splitTab`, drag-to-split, the row menu's Split right/bottom) and the menu's Exit split view are the only edits of the layout: a dropped session leaves the split it was remembered in, at four panes the dropped session replaces the pane under the pointer and that one leaves the split, Exit split view forgets the split. Closing a tab (`closeTab`, which a deleted session's tab goes through) and removing a pane drop the session from its split; a split left with one session dissolves. Sidebar: every row whose tab is a pane of the split on screen has `data-active` (the selected style), the focused pane's row adds `data-pane-focus` (a quiet colorless edge, only while a split shows). Persistence: the renderer sends `rememberedSplits` with every `tabs.setView` (the `tabs.setActive` shortcut is used only while neither side remembers any), main keeps them per window (`#rememberedByWindow`, pruned on release and never overlapping what is on screen) and saves them as `splits` next to `split` in the tab layout (`PersistedTabLayout`, leaves are tab indices); `GET_TABS` returns each remembered split once, on its first tab (`rememberedSplit`). Tests: `shared/split-layout.test.ts`, `tabs.test.tsx` (remembered splits), `Sidebar.test.tsx`, `tab-layout.test.ts`, `sidecar-pool.test.ts`; e2e `split-select.e2e.ts` (3 panes: all selected, outside pick solo, member restores, relaunch), `split-drop`, `desktop-tabs`, `workbench-shortcuts`.
- Agent browsing in the preview (Preview toolbar robot button, local tabs only, off until the user clicks it): the tab's bridge (`resources/gui-bridge/browser-relay.ts`) starts stock's own `omp browser-relay` server (`tools/browser/relay/server.ts`) on a free 127.0.0.1 port with a random extension token and holds `browser.relay`/`browser.relayUrl` as session runtime overrides, so stock's browser tool runs in relay mode. Main joins as the relay's "Chrome extension" (`src/main/preview/agent-relay.ts`, stock's `relay/protocol.ts`) over the preview page's `webContents.debugger`, offering that one page — never the app window, which keeps no remote-debugging port. Electron's per-page debugger is not confined to its page (observed on Electron 36: `Target.getTargets` lists the app window and `Target.attachToTarget` onto it answers `Runtime.evaluate` there; only `attachToBrowserTarget` is "Not allowed"), so the relay forwards an **allowlist** only: the page domains stock's puppeteer uses (`Page`, `Runtime`, `DOM`, `Input`, `Network`, `Emulation`, … — never `Browser`, `SystemInfo`, `Tethering`, `Tracing`, `Memory`), and from `Target` just `setAutoAttach` and `detachFromTarget`. `Tracing` is browser-wide (a trace records the app's other windows), so stock's `tab.traceStart()` fails by design, and the relay's reply says so.
