It also refuses `Page.navigate` off http(s)/about:blank, `Page.close`/`crash`, downloads, `DOM.setFileInputFiles`, `Page.setInterceptFileChooserDialog`, `Input.setInterceptDrags`, an `Input.dispatchDragEvent` whose `data` carries `files` or a `file:` URL (Electron 36's debugger turns `files` into a real dropped File read from the local path; `e2e/preview-agent.e2e.ts` pins that finding), a `Network.loadNetworkResource` whose `url` is not http(s) (Electron 36's debugger already answers a `file:` URL with "Unsupported URL scheme", pinned by the same spec; the rule keeps local files out whatever a later Electron does) and `Security.setIgnoreCertificateErrors`/`setOverrideCertificateErrors`/`handleCertificateError`. `Page.navigateToHistoryEntry` is allowed only after the relay reads `Page.getNavigationHistory` and the target entry's URL passes the same http(s)/about:blank rule, so stock's `tab.back()`/`tab.forward()` (puppeteer `goBack`/`goForward`) work. The relay addresses only sessions this page's own debugger announced with `Target.attachedToTarget` (its frames and workers, never a `page`/`tab`/`browser` target), so no new, closed or other target is reachable; the page keeps its partition, sandbox and no-preload rules. A banner shows the agent's last CDP method; Take over (or a tab switch, closing the panel, the session ending) detaches the debugger, closes the socket and stops the relay. `e2e/preview-agent.e2e.ts` drives it with stock's relay server and puppeteer-core from the installed omp and checks back/forward, and that `Target.getTargets`/`attachToTarget`, `Tracing.start`, a file drag and a `file:` resource load are refused.
- Changes polling (`panels/RepositoryChanges.tsx`, `get_git_fingerprint` in `local/git/changes.ts`): the open list asks git every 3 s only for a fingerprint (one `status --porcelain=v2 --branch -z --untracked-files=all`, whose `branch.oid` is the HEAD oid, and an in-process `lstat` of each listed path so a second edit of an already modified file moves it; the panel passes the worktree `root` its last full read reported, so `rev-parse --show-toplevel` runs only before the first read or when that root does not hold the tab's folder): 1 git process per unchanged tick, against 9 for a full `get_git_changes` (counted by git's trace2 log in `changes.test.ts`; on a 500-file change set 20 git processes and about 0.35 s of git CPU per minute while the panel shows, measured through `/usr/bin/git`). GUI git calls run the developer dir's git directly on macOS (`gitBin` in `exec.ts`), never xcrun's `/usr/bin/git` trampoline, which costs about 3.4 ms of CPU per call. Only a changed fingerprint reloads the list, and the open diff stays on screen until its replacement arrives; a hidden window clears the timer, and the panel (unmounted when hidden) polls nothing. Every `git`/`gh` child gets `GIT_OPTIONAL_LOCKS=0` (`execBin`), so a status refresh never takes `.git/index.lock` from the agent's own git. A failed reload keeps its fingerprint as the baseline and waits for the checkout to change again. Known gap: a stash drop or an operation with no status change alone does not move the fingerprint (the refresh button reads it).
- Unsaved Files edits belong to their tab: `closeTab` on the tab named by `fileEditTabId` while `fileEditDirty` does not close it but raises the editor's own discard confirm (`holdTabClose` → `pendingFileSwitch`); "Discard" sets `discardedForTabClose` and the side panel then closes the tab, Cancel keeps both.
- Agent visuals (`src/main/preview/visuals.ts`, `visual-navigation.ts`, `page-scripts.ts`; contract in `src/shared/preview.ts`): the `gui_visualize` host tool (registered with `gui_terminal_read`, local tabs only) shows a project `.html`/`.svg`/image/`.txt` file, inline HTML the agent wrote or an http(s) page as a Preview tab of the calling tab, keeps a version per change (`<userData>/visuals/<id>/v<N>.<ext>`, 20 per visual, 100 visuals in `history.json`, Page options › All visuals…). Each window's preview has two in-memory sessions, reused by every preview host of that window, their storage cleared when a host is disposed: `web` (`preview-<win>`) for every page the user, a popup, the agent's browser or a web visual browses, and `visual` (`preview-visual-<win>`) for file and inline visuals alone. Those load from `omp-visual://v<N>-<id>/…`, registered on the visual session only: the entry from its snapshot, other files from the entry file's own folder (the project folder when the entry sits at its root) by known asset type, never hidden paths or anything outside it. A page stays in the session it was made in (`PreviewPageState.session`): a visual opens in a visual-session page (an empty web tab is replaced, a web tab is never reused), an address typed on a visual's tab opens a new web tab, and the agent's browser never takes a visual's page. Every response (404 included) carries a Content-Security-Policy (`VISUAL_CSP`, `default-src 'none'` with each allowed kind named, `connect-src 'self' data: blob:`, `form-action 'none'`, no frames or objects) so the page cannot send what it reads off its own origin by `fetch`, `sendBeacon`, `<a ping>`, WebSocket, image, CSS, prefetch, form or frame. What CSP does not cover, the host refuses (Security #3): whatever a visual's page starts itself (a link, `location`, a form, a meta refresh, a frame's load, a redirect) goes only to another version of the same visual or to `about:blank`/`about:srcdoc` (`visualMayNavigate`); its popups never open; its downloads are dropped without a notice; WebRTC runs with `disable_non_proxied_udp` (no STUN or TURN over UDP) and the visual session's proxy is a dead `http://127.0.0.1:1` with `<-loopback>`, so TURN over TCP and any other socket go nowhere. DNS prefetch, which no CSP or proxy stops (it asks the system resolver for any name the page spells), is off app-wide: `index.ts` adds `dnsPrefetchingEnabled=false` to `--blink-settings` (merged with any given at launch) before ready, which Blink applies after each page's preferences in every frame (srcdoc and about:blank ones too) and which `x-dns-prefetch-control: on` cannot undo; other pages only lose a hint. A web address refused right after a real click or key on the page (the C143 gesture window) shows in full in a notice whose "Open in new tab" loads it in a web tab, only when the user picks it. `e2e/preview-visual-egress.e2e.ts` pins all of it against a 127.0.0.1 sentinel (TCP and UDP) that must hear nothing and the visual session's net-log, which must hold no host resolution the page asked for. `gui_visualize open` with an existing `visual` id moves that record to the new target. The page keeps the Preview's sandbox (no preload, permission-less sessions). Comment on an element / a region, pins, parent element and CSS variable tweaks run in an isolated world (`PAGE_WORLD`) on trusted events and go to the composer as one message; `choose` returns a `data-choice` click only when it was trusted, a real mouse/key release on that page came within 1 s and neither the agent's relay nor any debugger holds the page.
- SSH tools on this computer (`src/main/ssh/`, contract `src/shared/ssh-tools.ts`, Settings › SSH): port forwards for a saved host (`ssh -N -L 127.0.0.1:<local>:127.0.0.1:<remote>`, saved in `<userData>/ssh-forwards.json` and started again at launch), the server's listening ports every 5 s, ssh-agent/key state, and a reconnect with growing delay until the chosen grace time (1 min–7 days). Every such ssh runs under the setup supervisor with `-F <userData>/ssh/config`, whose first `Host *` block pins OMPdesk's own `known_hosts` with `StrictHostKeyChecking ask` and no connection sharing and then includes `~/.ssh/config`; OpenSSH hands `-F` to every ProxyJump hop, so each hop's key is checked too. Host-key, password, passphrase and code questions come through `SSH_ASKPASS` (`askpass.ts`: a Node helper run by the app binary, a per-process token over a local socket) into `SshPromptDialog`; answers are never stored and expire after 2 min. A changed host key stops the forward until the user forgets the saved key. The agent's own ssh tool stays stock's. SSH server windows (a headless OMPdesk on the server) are out of scope: OMPdesk ships for macOS and Windows only, and remote devices cover running work on another of the user's computers.
- Built-in MCP: `resources/omp-builtin/rea/.mcp.json` starts the dependency-free Node `rea-relay.mjs` via stock's `${OMP_PLUGIN_ROOT}` substitution, outside asar, as a directory `-e` on every eligible spawn (the REA root is `resources/omp-builtin/rea/`; `resources/omp-builtin/` itself is OMPdesk's own root, see the control tools below). The relay runs pinned REA (`rea/rea-config.json` is the sole version pin), exposes 15 unchanged entry-tool schemas plus `rea_catalog` / `rea_call`, and keeps the full catalog available on demand instead of adding 207 xd:// mount-notice lines to each session. The same root carries REA's own `reverse-engineer-anything` skill in `rea/skills/` (stock loads an extension root's `skills/`), so the skill comes and goes with the server; it is vendored from the pinned release plus an OMPdesk section (mounted vs `rea_call` tools, no `rea setup`) by `bun run sync:rea-skill`, which every version bump runs (`packaging-config.test.ts` fails on a stale copy). Node/npx must satisfy REA's engines; prefs `builtinMcp.rea` (default on) governs tabs this machine spawns or hosts. stdin EOF kills the child process tree; never write the user's MCP config.
- Built-in pstack engineering mode (Settings › Agent behavior › Smart automation, pref `pstack`, default **on**; contract `src/shared/pstack.ts`, row `settings/PstackRow.tsx`): `resources/omp-builtin/pstack/` is a translated copy of [pstack](https://github.com/cursor/plugins/tree/main/pstack) (MIT, Lauren Tan; pin `pstack-config.json`: commit `0b8e952…`, 0.15.26) loaded as a directory `-e` beside the REA root only while the switch is on (`sidecar-factory.ts` `builtinMcp`, from the next spawn). It holds `skills/` (principles, the playbooks under `poteto-mode/playbooks/`, workflow skills; every one `disable-model-invocation`, so hidden from the model's skill list and read as `skill://<name>`), `agents/` (`pstack-agent`, the wrapper every delegate of a playbook uses, and `comment-sicko`), the hand-written `adapter.md` (Cursor/Claude names → omp `task`/`ask`/`todo`, `ompdesk_session_open`/`compare`/`merge`, model roles `@task`/`@slow`/`@smol`, `~/.omp/agent/sessions` transcripts, the routing gate) and `pstack-manifest.json`. **Never add a `plugin.json` there**: omp would class the folder an Agent Plugins root and drop 54 of the skills for their frontmatter. `tdd`/`teach` are `pstack-tdd`/`pstack-teach`, so no stock or user skill is shadowed. Left out as Cursor-only: `setup-pstack`, `poteto-help`, `make-bot-ui`, Bugbot triage, the `orchestrate` playbook and the `watch-pr`/`orch` tools (they need Cursor cloud agents or a custom CLI). Everything except `pstack-config.json`, `adapter.md`, `agents/pstack-agent.md` and `README.md` is generated by `bun run sync:pstack` (`scripts/sync-pstack.ts`: clone at the pin or `PSTACK_SRC=<clone>`, copy, a rule table in which every rule must match at least once, so a reworded upstream line fails the sync; `-- --check` lists newer upstream commits) and pinned by `src/main/pstack/pack-drift.test.ts` (sha256 manifest, no `plugin.json`, names and links, no leftover Cursor term, section size cap, tool names). The always-on part is one `registerPromptSection` section (`resources/gui-bridge/modes/pstack.ts` + `pstack-core.ts`; the texts are generated into `pstack-text.ts` from `adapter.md` and the translated `poteto-mode` skill: about 20 KB, 7.3k tokens by the context inspector on 18 Oct 2026): static text for the prompt cache, main agent only, opening with the precedence repo rules > user settings > a skill the user invoked > the pack, then a routing gate (a trivial request gets a plain answer; anything else reads the matching playbook first and copies its steps into `todo`). It is off for subagents (they get `pstack-agent`), Plan mode, a chat session without a project (`OMP_GUI_PSTACK=0` at spawn) and the switch off; an Autopilot run gets the 2 KB `PSTACK_AUTOPILOT_SECTION` instead (its protocol and HARD LIMITS win; bug-fix, hillclimb, autonomous-run, session-pickup, pause-safely and prove-it-works, no shipping or PR playbooks); Solo delegation swaps the delegation paragraph. Tests: `bridge-pstack.test.ts` (the gating table and `install` on a fake `pi`), `e2e/pstack.e2e.ts` (fake omp, whose `get_skills` and context inspector read the `-e` roots and the real gating under `OMP_GUI_TEST_PSTACK=1`), and opt-in `e2e/real-pstack.e2e.ts` (`OMP_GUI_REAL_WORK=1`, `smol` role: a bug-fix prompt read `bug-fix.md` and copied its steps into `todo` in 3 of 5 single runs on 18 Oct 2026 at about $0.04–0.17 a run, a greeting stays plain at $0.0035, and the switch-off control never reads the playbook; a small model skips the playbook now and then on a one-line bug, so the test asks one follow in three runs).
- OMPdesk control tools (Settings › Tools & extensions › OMPdesk control tools, pref `agentControl`, default **on**; contract `src/shared/agent-control.ts`, rules `src/main/agent-control/service.ts`, tool schemas `tools.ts`, app seam `host.ts`; skill `resources/omp-builtin/skills/ompdesk/SKILL.md`): host tools like `gui_terminal_read`, registered with stock `set_host_tools` on every ready local sidecar (`SidecarManager.syncHostTools`, re-sent when the switch changes; the skill's root `resources/omp-builtin` is a `-e` directory only while the switch is on, so skill and tools come and go with the pref) and answered in `ipc.ts` (`hostToolExecutor` → `AgentControlRuntime.run`). They let the main agent see and steer **every** session of its window: `ompdesk_sessions` (state, current step from the transcript tail, running subagents, todo progress, a waiting ask, last answer), `ompdesk_session_open` (one or `sessions: […]`, optional `worktree: true | {branch, base: HEAD|default}` through the existing `worktree_create`, branch `omp/agent/<slug>`), `_send` (steer or follow-up), `_result` (several ids, `wait`, `until all|any`), `_read` (bounded transcript pages through `get_transcript_page`), `_answer`, `ompdesk_tab_focus` (the renderer's own pick/split via `AGENT_CONTROL_VIEW`), `ompdesk_notify`, `_stop` (`close`, `delete`). Everything runs through paths the GUI already has: prompts/steers/follow-ups through `dispatchRpcCommand` (a paired host's tab: over the link, where the host's grants and the per-device quota apply — `quota-commands.ts` lists `agent_control_confirm`/`agent_control_note` as free), the tab spawn path (`spawnTabForWindow`), the renderer's own close for closing, `deleteSession` for delete. **Subagents have no control tools** (stock registers host tools on the RPC session only; `host_tool_call` carries no caller id, so this is structural, checked by `e2e/real-agent-control.e2e.ts`: a subagent answers `NO_TOOL` and no tab opens). Safety: the session tools touch no accounts, settings or permissions; `ompdesk_app_list` / `ompdesk_app` (`app-catalog.ts` actions, `app-settings.ts` one `settings.<row>` action per Settings row, `app-control.ts` the one dispatch point, `app-guard.ts` and `app-channels.ts` the agent's own default-deny risk tables: `AGENT_CHANNELS` is typed over every `IpcChannel`, so a new channel does not compile until it is `never` with a reason or lists the actions that call it; `IPC_POLICY` is the web device's permission table, not the agent's) change the app itself in main, arguments validated against the action's schema: `read`/`change` free (a change is journaled in the caller's session through `agent_control_note`, shown as a quiet toast with one-step undo when a previous value exists, `AGENT_CONTROL_APP` to `lib/agent-app.ts`, and applied live there), `sensitive` (sign-in starts, proxy, auto-update switches, mobile web, plugin and MCP installs with the exact command or url on the card, starting pairing, memory purge, saved-session delete, updates, restart, quit) raises the same `[OMPdesk]` card unless `agentControlManage` is on, `autopilot.*` actions call `ompdesk_autopilot` (its card and folder rules; `get_autopilot`/`set_autopilot` are `never` for app actions), `never` refuses (the control tools switch, `agentControlManage`/`agentControlApprove`, `tools.approvalMode` and every approval, secrets, memory and computer-use switch, answering a card, `RPC_COMMAND_FOR_TAB` and any raw RPC passthrough, tokens and pairing codes) whether reached as a pref, a stock setting, an RPC command or one command inside another (`ActionContext` re-checks every primitive; an RPC command nested in another is `never`); closing the tab of a resting session (not busy, no open ask, not in the person's view, no draft or prompt of theirs since: `ControlTab.looked`/`userTouched`) raises no card whoever opened it, and a session in view or typed into is never closed (checked again right before the close); each close is journaled as one quiet `ompdesk.mail` note line in both sessions. Ownership survives a relaunch: `#openedBy` adds every tab whose `host.parentOf` is the caller (the pool's `openedBy`, else the tab holding `openedBySession`). Stopping a running session or deleting a session the caller did **not** open raises one confirmation card (stock extension-UI `confirm` through the calling tab's omp, bridge `agent_control.confirm`, title prefix `[OMPdesk]`, so it renders, survives a reload and reaches a paired device) unless the user turned on "Let agents manage other sessions" (`agentControlManage`, default off); sessions it opened itself need none. `_answer` answers asks (select, confirm, input, ask) and the answered session journals `ompdesk.agent-answer` and toasts "에이전트가 답함 (title)" (bridge `agent_control.note`); a **tool approval** ("Allow tool: …") is approved only when "Let agents approve for other sessions" (`agentControlApprove`, default off) is on, otherwise "사용자 승인이 필요해요" (deny always works), and a card titled `[OMPdesk] …` is never answerable. A message another session's agent sends arrives as an ordinary stock prompt wrapped in `<ompdesk-agent-message from="…">` (`wrapAgentMessage`, a body cannot forge or close it), shown as "에이전트가 보냄 · title" by `MessageBubble`/`promptDisplayText`. No count or rate caps (the user's rule, 19 Oct 2026): any number of sessions opened, messages sent, schedules and Autopilot runs; provenance ("opened by you") is main-owned state, never read from text or tool args, and only a self-send is refused (it means nothing). The user can hide a session from agents (Sidebar session menu › "다른 에이전트가 이 세션을 보지 못하게", `agentHidden` in the `sidebar` prefs blob, this computer's sessions): it lists as hidden and refuses read, send, wait, answer, show and stop. Autopilot hooks (optional host deps `isStopping`, `beforeFirstPrompt`; `AgentControl.childrenOf`, `clearQueue`, `openSessions(caller, sessions, tag)`) keep `src/main/autopilot*.ts` out of this module. Tests: `agent-control/*.test.ts`, `shared/agent-control.test.ts`, `AgentControlRow.test.tsx`, `e2e/agent-control.e2e.ts` (fake omp: create → send → wait → read, the card, an approval), `e2e/real-agent-control.e2e.ts` (opt-in: "세션 2개 만들어서 각각 시키고 결과 정리" on the small model, and the subagent probe); the fake omp implements `get_last_assistant_text`, `agent_control.confirm` and `agent_control.note`.
  - The skill is a kernel plus references: `SKILL.md` (at most 60 lines: the tools, when to open a session, definition of done, the brief template with Ownership and Observable acceptance, handoff vs supervise, reuse a finished child, "silence is a checkpoint, not failure") links `skill://ompdesk/references/<file>.md` (`orchestration`, `worktrees-review`, `schedules`, `across`, `autopilot`, `app-control`), which stock's skill protocol resolves against the skill's own `baseDir` inside an extension root (`internal-urls/skill-protocol.ts`). Each rule sits where it is used (the worktree-safety rule — never follow an absolute path in a child's answer into the main checkout — in the collect and compare steps). `src/main/agent-control/skill-drift.test.ts` fails when a tool or parameter in `tools.ts` and the skill disagree (renamed, added, or a word on a kernel tool line that is no parameter or enum value of it), when a `skill://` link names a missing file, or when the kernel grows past 60 lines.
  - Worker contract: `#openOne` sends each child `agent_control_worker {from}` (local handler → bridge `agent_control.worker`, free in `quota-commands.ts`) once it is ready and before its first prompt; the child journals one `ompdesk.worker` custom entry, and `resources/gui-bridge/agent-control.ts` registers the `ompdesk-worker` prompt section (`registerPromptSection`), which appends `WORKER_CONTRACT` (stay in your folder and the named scope, done = the message's acceptance checked, the final answer names changes, the check run and what is left) for the main agent of a session whose branch holds that entry. Journal-based, so a relaunched child keeps it, and a session the user opened, the parent and a worker's own subagents never get it. Pinned by `bridge-agent-worker.test.ts` and `service.test.ts` (fan-out); the fake omp answers `agent_control.worker`.
- Control tools, worktrees and the review queue (`src/main/agent-control/branches.ts`, `WorkBoard.tsx` review section, `stores/agent-review.ts`): `ompdesk_session_open` with `worktree` makes each child's checkout and branch through the existing `worktree_create` (`omp/agent/<slug>`, base `HEAD` or `default`). `ompdesk_compare` reads each child's branch (`git merge-base`, `diff --numstat` against it, commits ahead, uncommitted and untracked counts) plus its final answer; it **runs no tests** (a child reports them in its answer). **Where a branch lands** (`branchFacts`/`landingFacts`, one rule): the parent checkout's HEAD and the upstream its branch tracks (`<branch>@{upstream}`, else `refs/remotes/origin/HEAD`; refs as last fetched, the module never fetches and never touches the parent checkout). Agents push their own work (`git push origin HEAD:main`) while the parent's local main stays behind, so a tip in either one has **landed** (`BranchFacts.merged`) and its diff is measured from that tip (only uncommitted work is left); an unlanded branch is measured from its merge-base with the upstream when the upstream holds the parent's HEAD, else with the parent's HEAD, so a stale local main never lends a child the upstream's newer commits (it once showed every pushed child as "200 files · +40520 −425"). `ompdesk_merge` merges the branch into the parent checkout's current branch with `git merge --no-ff` (local only, never a push, no remote command anywhere in the module): it refuses a detached parent, uncommitted work in the child or the parent, and a branch with nothing to merge (`mergeProblem`), asks one `[OMPdesk]` card unless "Let agents manage other sessions" (`agentControlManage`) is on, and aborts a conflicting merge (`git merge --abort`) naming the files. Local sessions only (a paired host's worktree is on the host). The work board's "Needs review" section lists every session the agent opened (`AgentControl.reviewItems`, pushed as `AGENT_CONTROL_REVIEW` on every child activity after a 400 ms settle, asked once on mount through the `agent_control_review` command that `dispatchRpcCommand` answers itself) except one with nothing left to review: idle (no turn, no open ask), its branch landed, no uncommitted or untracked file, not failed and not waiting on an answer gives no item at all (its opened row reads merged); a landed branch with files left over lists only those (`uncommitted` counts tracked edits plus untracked files). Each item: branch → base, files and +/−, uncommitted, the answer's start; **Review** focuses the child and opens Changes, **Approve** merges (the click is the confirmation; a session without a branch is just accepted), **Discard** (second click) stops and closes the child and removes its worktree and branch (`discardWorktree`: only a registered linked worktree), and the command also has **dismiss**. A conflict keeps the item with its files. Children finishing or needing the user reach them through the existing batched notifications (they are ordinary tabs). **Auto-close** (`AgentControl.autoClose`, rules in `auto-close.ts` `autoCloseDecision`, run by `AgentControlRuntime` on tab activity after a 1 s settle, after a merge/Approve and every 10 s (`sweepMs`; `OMP_GUI_TEST_AGENT_SWEEP_MS` in e2e) because a merge or push outside the app moves no tab): an opened session (not the person's own, not an Autopilot child or run) closes its TAB (the session file stays; `AgentControlViewEvent.auto` goes through the renderer's normal `closeTab`, which refuses a tab on screen or with a composer draft: `lib/agent-auto-close.ts`) once it is idle with a final answer, nothing running (`isSettled`), and ends with the worker's own verdict `Result: ok` (`WORKER_CONTRACT` in `resources/gui-bridge/agent-control.ts` asks every opened session to end its final answer with `Result: ok` or `Result: problem — <reason>`; `parseWorkerResult` in `shared/agent-control.ts` reads that last line and never the prose: `problem` keeps the session open as a problem row (review item `failed`, the ✕ token, "Needs review"), a missing line leaves it done but open) and either has no branch or its branch has landed with a clean worktree (`landingFacts`: tip in the parent's HEAD or its upstream as above AND the branch has a commit of its own: an untouched branch has nothing to land; uncommitted or untracked files keep it open, reason `uncommitted`). Never: a tab the person looks at (`active`/`visible`/viewer), one with a draft (`SidecarPool.hasDraft`) or a prompt of their own (`noteUserPrompt` in `dispatchRpcCommand`; agent messages are wrapped), a failed ending, unmerged commits, files left in the worktree, and not before the opener read the answer (`result`/`read`/`compare`/`merge`) unless the opener is idle. The worktree goes with the close (`removeLandedWorktree`: `worktree remove` and `branch -d`, never `--force` or `-D`, and it checks landed and clean again). The close is recorded in the pref `agentClosedChildren`. Tests: `auto-close.test.ts`, `service.test.ts` (autoClose), `branches.test.ts` (real repos), `e2e/family-opened-close.e2e.ts` (fake omp with `OMP_GUI_TEST_SESSION_FILES=1`: three opened sessions finish, the plain and the merged one close and fold into "2 done", the one with unmerged commits stays a row with the failed mark; the merged worktree and branch are removed, the other kept). Tests: `branches.test.ts` (real temp repos; a bare remote with a stale parent main: a pushed child is landed with no diff, an unpushed one is one commit and its own file, a landed one with leftovers shows only them), `service.test.ts`, `WorkBoardReview.test.tsx`, `e2e/agent-review.e2e.ts` (real git: fan out three worktrees, compare, merge from the board and through the tool with its card, discard, a conflict).
  - Opt-in on the real omp (`OMP_GUI_REAL_WORK=1`): the third test of `e2e/real-agent-control.e2e.ts` has the main agent open one worktree child that commits a file, compare, merge (the test approves the card) and create and list a schedule, and records the spend (about $0.11 on the `smol` role). Stock presents the host tools as `xd://` devices, so the journal shows them as `write` calls to `xd://ompdesk_*`.
- Control tools beyond the caller's window (19 Oct 2026; research in `local://session-tools-research.md`: Codex `spawn_agent`/`send_input`/`wait_agent`/`resume_agent`/`list_agents` and app-server `thread/list`·`turns/list`·`resume`·`fork`, Claude Code `Agent`/`SendMessage`/background agents, Cursor background-agent runs; none gives the model a compare-and-pick tool). `AgentControlHost.tabs` returns every open tab of the app, the caller's window first (`ControlTab.here`, `device`, `sessionPath`; `SidecarPool.tabWindows`/`windowOfTab`), and every call to a tab goes through the window that owns it, so send, wait, answer, show and stop reach other windows and the tabs a paired device runs (its host's grants and `PeerQuota` apply there). `ompdesk_sessions` takes `scope` `window` (default) | `app` | `stored`, `device` (a paired host by name or id, `local`), `folder`, `status` (`running` | `waiting` | `idle`), `mine`, `query`, `limit`, and always ends with the paired devices and their state (`AgentControlHostDeps.remote.hosts`). `stored` lists the session index of this computer (`deps.sessions`: `SessionIndex.list`/`searchContent`) or a paired host (`sessions.list`/`sessions.search` over the link); rows are cached by stock id (`#stored`), so `ompdesk_session_read`, `reopen` and `ompdesk_compare` take a saved id (an open saved session goes through its tab). A saved transcript is stock `loadSessionMessagesReadOnly` (no writer, no lock) in the stock helper (`sessions.messages`, `StockSessionStore.messages`, `SessionIndex.readMessages`), or the host's `sessions.messages` (`LocalHostServices`, refusing a path the host's user hid: dep `agentHidden`, code `session_hidden`); the read pages from the end with an index cursor. `ompdesk_session_open` takes `device` (spawned through `spawnTab` with that `device`; folder absolute or that device's workspace; no worktree off the caller's machine; an offline device refuses) and `reopen` (a saved session's file on the device it is saved on, never with folder/worktree/mode, never marked a worker, refused when a tab holds it). `ompdesk_compare` reads per session the final answer, the check-like commands its transcript ran with their outcome (`checksRun`) and a worktree's branch facts; with `criteria` (≤ `JUDGE_MAX_CRITERIA` 12) main builds one judge prompt (≤ 60 000 characters of evidence) and sends `agent_control_judge` to the CALLER's omp (local handler → bridge `agent_control.judge` → `completeOnSmol` with `role: "slow"`, purpose `ompdesk-judge`, a model command in `quota-commands.ts`); `parseVerdict` (zod) turns its JSON into a ranked verdict, `yes`/`partly`/`no`/`unknown` per criterion and a winner, and a reply that is not the JSON is shown as is. A saved row or an untitled tab reads as its task (`storedTitle`, `agentTaskTitle`) or `(untitled, in <folder>)`, never the raw agent-message wrapper; an opened tab's `ControlTab.title` falls back to `IpcTabInfo.task`. Tests: `service.test.ts` (scopes, filters, stored list/read/paging, device open, reopen, judge, `checksRun`, `parseVerdict`), `host.test.ts` (another window's tab through its window, device spawn rules, stored rows local and remote), `host-services.test.ts` (`sessions.messages`, hidden refused), `e2e/agent-control-across.e2e.ts` (fake omp: another window, a judged compare, a closed session found, read and reopened; two apps paired: a session opened on the device, its prompt in the host's record, its saved transcript read back), and opt-in on the real omp the fourth test of `e2e/real-agent-control.e2e.ts` (19 Oct 2026: open ALPHA/BETA, judge, close, find, read, reopen; $0.36 / 355k tokens in all, the judge $0.007 on the `slow` role).
  - Sessions tell each other (the mailbox, 19 Oct 2026; the idea is Orca's orchestration mailbox: worker_done/heartbeat/escalation verbs, decision gates routed to the parent and the person and never auto-resolved, dispatch ids that fence a stale attempt, "silence is not evidence"). A session another session opened reports with `ompdesk_report` (`kind` progress | done | blocked | question | review | note, `text`, `files`, `checks`, `to` a sibling, `dispatch`, `id`); `src/main/agent-control/mail.ts` holds the rules (`Mailbox`, `deliveryFor`, `mailText`), `service.ts` `#report` the routing (opener by `AgentControlHost.parentOf`: this launch's `#parents`, else `IpcTabInfo.openedBy`, else the tab holding `openedBySession`; a sibling only under the same opener, written as `note`; nobody else). Delivery is ONE stock custom message in the receiver (`agent_control_mail` → bridge `agent_control.mail` → stock `pi.sendMessage`, customType `ompdesk.mail` from `resources/gui-bridge/agent-control-contracts.ts`): question/blocked `aside` + `triggerTurn` (between the receiver's steps, an idle receiver starts a turn), done/review `followUp` + `triggerTurn` (after its turn, or at once when idle), progress/note no turn (shown at once when idle, `nextTurn` while busy); progress from one sender is coalesced for `MAIL_COALESCE_MS` (2 s, the latest wins, `merged` counts the rest) and any other report supersedes the pending progress. The model reads `<ompdesk-mail from=… kind=… dispatch=…>` plus a one-line instruction (answer a question with `ompdesk_session_send`, a user-only choice goes to the user, never guessed); the transcript draws `MailNote` (`CustomMessageCard.tsx`, one muted line `⑂ Session report · <title>`, a question/blocked kind in the warning tint, files and checks in the tooltip, `data-agent-mail`), and the work board's opened rows show the last report (`AgentReviewItem.report`, `waiting` puts a child whose question is unanswered first with the waiting tint; `opened-children.ts`). Dispatch ids: every message an opener sends its child (open or send) carries `[ompdesk dispatch dN: …]` (and `[ompdesk request <id>]` for a `request_id`), a new dispatch marks the child's question answered, and a report naming an older dispatch is ignored (`stale`). Durability without a store of OMPdesk's: the journals are the record. A report's id defaults to a hash of the sender's and receiver's session files, kind, dispatch and text, so a call stock re-runs after a relaunch has the same id; the receiver's bridge drops an id its branch already holds (`hasMailEntry` over stock's `custom_message` entries) or this process already queued; a report counts as delivered (`#seen`) only after delivery succeeded, so a failed one is retried by the sender with the same id (progress is retried `MAIL_PROGRESS_RETRIES` times); after a relaunch main reads the child's dispatch and the request ids back from its transcript (`journalMarks`), so fencing and `request_id` idempotency of `ompdesk_session_send` hold across restarts. Concurrent sends with the same `request_id` (keyed by both session files) join one in-flight delivery. Receipts: a send answers `accepted` plus `turn started` when the target is seen running within `TURN_START_WAIT_MS` (1.5 s). No count or rate cap (the user's rule); only a self-send is refused. Worker contract (`WORKER_CONTRACT`) tells children to report done/question/blocked instead of waiting to be asked; the skill's kernel teaches the opener's loop (dispatch, end the turn, be woken, judge with `ompdesk_compare`, merge) instead of polling. Tests: `service.test.ts` (delivery per kind, question waiting until answered, stale dispatch, repeated id, coalescing, busy receiver, siblings, receipts, a failed delivery retried, the same id without one, fencing and request ids read back after a relaunch), `bridge-agent-mail.test.ts` (stock `sendMessage` call, journal and process dedupe), `CustomMessageCard.test.tsx`, `opened-children.test.ts`, `e2e/agent-mail.e2e.ts` (fake omp: two children report and finish, one asks and is answered, the parent's turns start on the done and question reports with no polling tool called; a report repeated after a relaunch arrives once).
- New worktree preparation (`src/main/local/git/worktree-seed.ts`, run by `createWorktree` after `worktree add`, so the worktree dialog, Autopilot and `ompdesk_session_open` worktrees all get it; stock omp 18.12.1 has no `worktreeinclude` and no worktree hook): `.worktreeinclude` at the main checkout's root lists paths or globs (`*`, `?`, `**`; `#` comments) copied from the main checkout only when `git check-ignore` reports them ignored (a tracked file never is), refusing absolute paths, `..`, `.git`, `!` and any match whose real parent leaves the checkout; a symlink inside a copied folder is recreated only when it points inside; budget `SEED_MAX_BYTES` 50 MB / `SEED_MAX_FILES` 10,000, existing files never overwritten, every skip reported (`WorktreeSeedReport.skipped`). Then `.ompdesk/worktree-setup` (`.cmd` on Windows), only when the file exists in the new worktree or the main checkout, runs in the worktree under the setup supervisor (`/bin/sh <file>`, env `OMPDESK_ROOT_PATH`/`OMPDESK_WORKTREE_PATH`, `SETUP_TIMEOUT_MS` 120 s, process tree killed at the timeout and its exit code then ignored, output logged to `<worktree git dir>/ompdesk-worktree-setup.log`); a failure is reported in `RpcWorktreeCreateResult.setup`, never fatal. The dialog toasts both (`announcePreparation`, `worktree.seed*`/`worktree.setup*`), `ompdesk_session_open` adds `describePreparation` to its notes. `worktree_create`'s budget includes it (`WORKTREE_PREPARE_BUDGET_MS`; renderer, agent-control and Autopilot wait 960 s). Pinned by `worktree-seed.test.ts` (real temp repos).
- Worktree names (`shared/project-origin.ts` rules, `main/local/git/origin.ts` git reads, `renderer/lib/project-origin.ts` `useProjectNames`, store `stores/project-origins.ts`, channel `project-origin:get` = `window.omp.projectOrigins.get(cwds)`): a linked worktree's folder (`~/.omp/wt/gui-<name>-<hash7>`) never shows as a project name. Git alone says what it is: `--git-common-dir` names the main checkout (`<main>/.git`, else the first `git worktree list` entry for a bare or relocated dir), `--git-dir` ≠ common dir marks a linked worktree, the branch gives its kind (`autopilot/…` → "오토파일럿"/Autopilot, `omp/agent/…` → "병렬 작업"/Parallel work, or the child's title where a one-line `project · title` is shown; any other branch shows as itself). No new store: main caches each cwd for 30 s (5 s when git cannot place it); the renderer asks once per cwd and retries an unknown one after 10 s. A folder git cannot place (removed worktree, not a repository, a paired host's path) shows its own name with the `gui-…-<hash7>` dressing stripped. Surfaces: the sidebar sets a worktree's group right under its project's group (indented, branch icon, `data-worktree-group`), labelled by what it is (`Autopilot`, `Parallel work`) — `acme-web · Autopilot` when the project's own group is not listed — with the full path and project as the tooltip; the title bar and pane header breadcrumb read `acme-web › Autopilot › <title>` (`data-titlebar-worktree`), the work board head carries `acme-web · Autopilot` (`data-board-project`), the window title is `<name> — acme-web · Autopilot`, an untitled worktree tab chip reads from `IpcTabWorktree` (`baseCwd` + branch, no git), and the session and workspace pickers use the same names. A new surface that shows a cwd's folder name goes through `useProjectNames`, never `basename`. An Autopilot run's children (`autopilot/<run>-c<N>-<area>`, `autopilotRunOf`) have no group of their own: their rows sit in the run's group under the run's row, one step in (`data-session-run-child`; `runParentOf` in `Sidebar.tsx`, from the branch git reports or, while git cannot place a child's new worktree, the child tab's `worktree.baseCwd`). A session another session's agent opened (`ompdesk_session_open`, an Autopilot child) reads as its task until stock names it: `agentTaskTitle` (`shared/agent-control.ts`: the body's `Title:` line by `assignmentTitle`, else its first line) of the indexed first message in `sessionDisplayTitle`, and before the index lists it the tab's `task` (`IpcTabInfo.task`, set by `AgentControl` through `SidecarPool.setTabTask` right after the spawn), never "New session". Tests: `project-origin.test.ts`, `origin.test.ts` (real temp repos: main, linked, detached, bare, removed), `Sidebar.test.tsx` "worktree groups", and the no-`gui-2026`/`gui-agent` assertions in `e2e/autopilot-cycles.e2e.ts` and `e2e/agent-review.e2e.ts`.
- Control tools and schedules (`ompdesk_schedule` in `src/main/agent-control/service.ts`; dep `schedules` of `AgentControlRuntime`, wired from `IpcDeps.schedules` ← `scheduleService` in `index.ts`): `ompdesk_schedule` `create`/`list`/`cancel` goes through the existing `ScheduleService` (`state()`, `command({action:"save"|"delete"})`, so the Schedules page, its log and its one-run-per-schedule rule apply): a create raises one `[OMPdesk]` card unless "Let agents manage other sessions" is on, names the schedule `[agent] <name>` (`AGENT_SCHEDULE_PREFIX`; main-owned provenance is the name the person sees in Settings › Schedules, and only such schedules can be cancelled by an agent), never sets `autoApprove` (a print-mode run cannot ask, so tools needing approval are denied), leaves the interval to the Schedules service's own minimum, refuses a relative folder, a caller on a paired host (runs start on this computer); runs stay capped by the service (`SCHEDULE_RUNS_KEPT` 200 in the log, a start while the previous run goes is recorded as skipped). There is no "remember" control tool: an agent that learned something durable saves it with ai-memory's own MCP tools (every session has them; the `ompdesk` skill points at ai-memory's `ai-memory-durable-pages` skill). Tests: `service.test.ts`, `e2e/agent-schedule.e2e.ts` (the card, the real `schedules.json`, list, cancel).
  - `ompdesk_autopilot` (`action` start | status | stop; `AutopilotSource` in `service.ts`, adapted by `host.ts` over `Autopilot.handleCommand` through the optional dep `autopilot`, wired in `ipc.ts`; skill reference `references/autopilot.md`): a start goes through the one `#start` path from a local tab of the caller's window that has the folder open (default the caller's own; a paired-host caller is refused), maps `ship` commit/push/release to the sheet presets (omitted: the stored `autopilotShip`) and `branch`/`advanced` 1:1 onto `AutopilotStartOptions`, and raises one `[OMPdesk]` card unless `agentControlManage` is on — always for ship release and for the default branch or `main`. There is no cap on agent-started runs. Stop is `stop` (End) or `abort` (Stop now: pauses).
- Remote devices (`src/main/remote/`): OMPdesk on a host (B) runs tabs for paired clients (A). Pairing is an 8-char code shown on B (10 min, single use, 5 wrong guesses rotate it) proved through an X25519 + HKDF handshake; later connections authenticate both long-term keys (Noise-KK-like) and run AES-256-GCM frames over TCP. Keys and peers live in `<userData>/remote-*.json` (mode 0600, no safeStorage). The pool holds a `SidecarLike` per tab: a `RemoteSidecar` (A) proxies `tab.*` requests to B's `LocalHostServices`, which runs real SidecarManagers from `sidecar-factory.ts` and forwards their events; ipc.ts routes every handler that touches the omp machine (sessions, fs reads, launch profiles, project switches) by the tab's `device`, and F-OWN keys are device + path. Settings, Usage and Stats follow the focused tab's `device`: tab RPC already reaches the host, and models.yml providers, the stats server and `omp bench` go through `machine-services.ts` (`LocalMachine` here, `host.*` calls on B), while app prefs, the updater and remote-devices stay this device's (`SECTION_SCOPE`, `components/remote/HostScope.tsx`). Host tool calls (`gui_open_url`, notify, clipboard) always run on A. `OMP_GUI_REMOTE_PORT` (TCP, default 47821, `0` = ephemeral) and `OMP_GUI_REMOTE_DISCOVERY_PORT` (UDP beacon, default 47822) let parallel instances coexist.
- Remote device guards: a paired device's per-day model limit (`remote/peer-quota.ts`, prefs on the peer) covers every RPC command that calls a model on the host, plus `host.bench.run` (models × runs requests) and `host.modelSetup.run`. `remote/quota-commands.ts` classifies **every** `RpcCommand` type as model, conditional (`switch_leaf` with `summarize`, `plan_approval` that runs or sends feedback, `set_goal` with an objective or `resume`) or free; a compile-time check and `quota-commands.test.ts` fail when a new command type is in none of the lists, and a type no list knows counts. `LocalHostServices` reserves the request (`PeerQuota.reserve`, no await between the check and the count; a weighted reservation that would take the day past its limit is refused whole) and refunds it when the work throws or fails; terminals (`terminal.request`) and the agent's `bash`/`eval` stay outside the limit. A paired host's tab never loses a user's answer: `RemoteSidecar.sendSideChannel` throws `remote_offline` for an `extension_ui_response` while the link is down, and otherwise returns its delivery (sent once a pending `tab.open` re-attach finished), which rejects with `remote_offline` when the link drops under it or with the host's error when the host refuses it; `routeSideChannel` and `EXTENSION_UI_RESPOND` await it, so the pool keeps the request route, the IPC call rejects and the card or sign-in prompt stays open with an error toast. It queues replies to the host's tool/URI calls and sends them once the host tab re-attaches, and hands a `tab.close` it could not send to `PendingTabCloses` (kept per host link in `devices.ts`, sent when the link is back). A remote tab's own link statuses (`error` on link loss, `starting` on reconnect; `statusFromLink`) never drop its open asks in the pool, so a reload still gets the card back; a status the host reports (its omp exited or restarted, or its refusal of the re-attach's `tab.open`/`tab.start` over a live link) does. What changed while the link was down comes with the re-attach: every `tab.open` of a tab the host still holds re-sends the asks its omp has open (the `openAsks` tab event; `LocalHostServices` keeps each ask's body until the device answers it or omp withdraws it), and the pool replaces the tab's open set with that list: a card whose ask is gone (the omp restarted or withdrew it meanwhile) closes through a synthesized `cancel`, and each open one is forwarded again, since the reconnect's `starting` cleared the page (`pushTabExtensionUiRequest` never doubles a card still up); one the tab already held goes out marked `resent` (`IpcExtensionUiPayload`), so the OS "needs you" notification is not repeated, while an ask raised while the link was down notifies as new; an ask whose answer is still on its way (`routeSideChannel`) is left as it is. `e2e/remote-resume.e2e.ts` cuts the link through a relay (`linkProxy` in `remote-harness.ts`) to cover both: the card comes back, and after the host's omp restarted none stays, not even after a reload.
- Packaged apps ship `resources/gui-bridge` **outside the asar** (`extraResources` in `electron-builder.yml` and `electron-builder.win.yml`; `src/main/packaging-config.test.ts` enforces it). The app contains no `omp` binary.
- Packaged apps keep only Electron's `en-US` and `ko` locale files (`electronLanguages` in both builder configs; never `en` alone, which deletes `en-US.pak` and blanks the window, issue #12). `scripts/after-pack.cjs` fails the package unless exactly those survive. Chromium's locale is then en-US for every other system language, so `src/main/system-languages.ts` seeds the Windows spellchecker (default and Preview sessions) and the Preview's Accept-Language from `app.getPreferredSystemLanguages()`; `e2e/preview.e2e.ts` checks the header under `--lang=de`. All three bundles are esbuild-minified with identifiers kept (`electron.vite.config.ts`), so `scripts/check-main-bundle.ts` reads the main bundle's imports with Bun's parser (against Node's builtins: Bun's own list also names `ws` and `undici`) and also refuses axe-core in the renderer, and any .woff/.ttf font in `out/renderer` (Chromium only loads the .woff2; the renderer's `woff2OnlyFonts` plugin drops KaTeX's and JetBrains Mono's older fallbacks from their @font-face lists). Vite bundles an optional peer dependency that is not installed as an empty object, not a `require` that throws, so a package's `try { require(…) } catch` fallback never runs: v0.34.0 crashed main on a paired phone's first WebSocket frame (`bufferUtil.unmask is not a function`, ws's `bufferutil`). The main build `define`s ws's own switches `WS_NO_BUFFER_UTIL`/`WS_NO_UTF_8_VALIDATE` (`MAIN_DEFINES`), the check refuses any `__viteOptionalPeerDep_*` stand-in in `out/main` and builds `scripts/ws-bundle-probe.ts` with the main config (`ws-bundle-probe.config.ts`, electron-vite under Node) for a round trip of masked frames in Node, and `e2e/mobile-web.e2e.ts` pairs a phone with the built app and sends one over `/ws` (`@smoke` in en light).
- Composer state uses stock `get_state` for session identity and resolved thinking, and stock `cycle_model` for forward cycling; the bridge adds only missing state, backward cycling, tool provenance/mounted tools and the GUI `/copy` tree around stock `pi-tui/overlays/copy-targets` extraction. Dependency-free contracts shared by the renderer and the packaged bridge (`auto-mode-contracts.ts`, `next-prompt-contracts.ts`, …) live in `resources/gui-bridge/`, never outside `extraResources`. The model pool was removed (its saved presets pref `modelPool` is deleted once at startup; `ompdesk.model-pool` journal entries are ignored).
- Composer working cue (`layout/composer/ComposerLive.tsx`, `composer-live.ts`, look in `styles/components.css`): a slow accent light travels the composer border while the main turn runs (`turn`), and a fainter, slower one plus a ringed Send with a hint tooltip shows while the turn is idle but this tab's subagents or background shells still run (`background`); both read the stores the work board and dock read (no polling), fade to a faint trace while the field holds a draft (the Send ring is dropped; `.omp-composer-box[data-composer-draft]`), never show beside a waiting question or approval, rest while the window is hidden, and animate `transform`/`opacity` only. Reduced motion and **software compositing** get the still form: a 1 px line on the box's top edge at 40 % (25 % for `background` or with a draft) and a still Send ring; main sets `html[data-gpu="software"]` after `app.getGPUInfo("complete")` reports `gpu_compositing` not enabled (`softwareCompositing()` in `gpu-fallback.ts`; `getGPUFeatureStatus()` read earlier says software even on a healthy GPU), and any new looping cue draws still under it.
- Composer modules (`layout/composer/`; reworked 19 Oct 2026, the sub-bullets below hold the behaviour): `InputArea.tsx` only wires state and lays the composer out; each concern has its own file, so a change lands in one place. Views: `ComposerToolbar` (the desktop/tablet toolbar row, memoised: scalar props and stable handlers via `use-stable-callback.ts`, so typing does not re-render it), `RunChip` (the one Run trigger and popover; `RunSettings.tsx` holds its speed and advanced fields), `ComposerSheet` (the phone's focus-keeping bottom sheet: the context breakdown), `ComposerQueue.tsx` (`ComposerQueuePill`, `ComposerQueueLive`), `ComposerQueueEdit` and `queue-lane.tsx` (the lane headings, shared with the QueuePanel), `ComposerRestoredQueue` (Stop's paused row), `ComposerUnderField` (the strip under the field: the dictation bar and failure sentence), `ComposerField` (textarea, ghost, Tab/→ hint, the hint row), `ComposerAttachments` (picture strip with one gallery per change and memoised thumbnails, upload chips, paste chips, file cards), `CompletionList`, `PasteMenu`, `ComposerPickers` (⌃R search, skill picker), `ComposerExpandedHeader` (⌃G header and the $EDITOR round trip). Hooks: `use-composer-field` (the one `textareaRef`, `placeDraft`/`ownEdit` — every write of the draft goes through `placeDraft` — list continuation, field height via `field-height.ts`), `use-composer-keys` (the field's keydown, the double-Esc hint), `use-composer-menu`, `use-composer-commands`, `use-composer-paste`, `use-composer-attach`, `use-composer-dictation`, `use-composer-draft` (persistence), `use-composer-history`, `use-composer-events` (`omp:insert-mention`, `omp:fill-composer`), `use-composer-suggestions`; sending stays `use-composer-submit`. Pure helpers: `listbox-nav.ts`, `strip-roving.ts`, `paste-marker-edit.ts`, `keep-focus.ts`; outside the folder `common/popover-focus.ts`, `lib/composer-actions.ts`, `lib/composer-blocked.ts`, `lib/tab-focus.ts`, `lib/hardware-keyboard.ts`.
  - Toolbar (desktop/tablet): `RunChip.tsx` is the one trigger and popover for Mode (Plan/Goal/Autopilot/Auto mode, `ModesSection` of `ComposerModes.tsx`), Thinking, Tool approval, Delegation (`DelegationField`), Fast/Advanced/Model roles; `data-run-settings-trigger` is its hook, `data-active` paints it accent (never a utility-class tie, unlayered CSS: a `.bg-transparent` once beat the accent wash), the aria-label spells the whole state. The model chip reads `Model · Thinking` (`data-model-chip`); the model picker has the thinking row. Steer/Stop/Send are reserved `.omp-composer-slot`s (`data-off` = inert and hidden, never unmounted: nothing moves when a turn starts, a draft appears or a mode changes). Labels fold by the toolbar's own `omp-toolbar` container (860/740/620/500 px), not the viewport. Popovers close on Tab (`common/popover-focus.ts`); the context ring opens on click/Enter only. The palette's History/Expand/Attach go through `omp:composer-action` (`lib/composer-actions.ts`). "Mode popover" elsewhere in this file means the Mode section of this popover.
  - Queue / Stop / Esc: the queue is one pill in the send cluster (`ComposerQueuePill`, `[data-queue-pill]`) opening a popover (a bottom sheet on a phone) with lane headings (`queue-lane.tsx`), the policy line from `steeringMode`/`interruptMode`, and rows named `Edit: <text>` with Steer now primary; nothing is added to the composer box while messages wait (`e2e/composer-queue-view.e2e.ts` checks 0 px). Edit queued (`composer.editingQueued`, `ComposerQueueEdit.tsx`) takes the field over; Cancel/Esc restore the exact draft snapshot; Send becomes a save ✓ (`composerQueue.editSave`). Stop pauses: `ComposerRestoredQueue` mounts as one 36 px row (chips, Resume, Discard), nothing auto-sends; `composer.stopping` allows one Stop per turn; Esc with text arms first ("Press Esc again to stop the turn", `composer.stopArmedAt`), `stopFromEscape`. `ComposerQueueLive` is the single polite status region. Placeholder priority: blocked (ask/approval/plan, `lib/composer-blocked.ts`) > editing queued > suggestion > follow-up > steer > idle. Shift+Tab is native focus navigation; the thinking cycle is ⌥⇧T (`thinking.cycle`). Activation of a tab/session focuses the composer (`lib/tab-focus.ts`).
  - Input mechanics: the field is uncontrolled while typing (React 19 re-syncs a controlled textarea's `defaultValue` on every key, which cut ⌘Z to one character per press): the draft store is the source of truth and every composer write goes through `placeDraft`. A `[Paste #N, +L lines]` marker is atomic: Backspace/Delete/word/line deletes that touch it remove marker and blob as one step (`paste-marker-edit.ts`), a dangling `[Paste #N` never reaches the agent. `@` lists up to 20 000 files (`MENTION_FS_MAX_ENTRIES`) at depth 16 (`MENTION_FS_DEPTH`) ranked by `pathMatcher` (file name, word starts, CamelCase, Hangul initials); ←/→ keep the menu while inside the token (`arrowKeepsMenu`). The completion menu and the history overlay share `listbox-nav.ts`; the field is `role=combobox` while a menu exists. Attachment strips (`strip-roving.ts`): one tab stop each, arrows, Delete/Backspace, Backspace on an empty field focuses the last attachment, "Remove all" from 2. The draft flushes on `visibilitychange`/`pagehide` and on unmount. A draft past 8 000 characters (`LONG_DRAFT_CHARS` in `ComposerField.tsx`) is set in the system face (`[data-long]`, `components.css`): the bundled Inter Variable was reshaped for the whole text on every key. Measured on this branch: key→paint p50 at 30 000 characters 20 ms → 6.6 ms (handler 15.9 → 4.5 ms), 0 characters with 100 attachments 11.3 → 2.4 ms. `compositionend` followed within 40 ms by Enter is ignored (iOS Safari's order, `POST_COMPOSITION_MS` in `lib/ime.ts`).
  - Dictation / steer box: the take shows as a bar under the field via `ComposerUnderField` (`DictationOverlay.tsx`, `use-composer-dictation.ts`, `lib/voice.ts`) and never covers the draft. A failed take keeps a `failure` kind (denied, no-device, in-use, busy, empty, no-model, failed) shown as one sentence plus a Details toggle (none for busy and empty); only no-model offers Open Settings. The + menu lists "Dictation" while `stt.enabled` is off and opens Settings (`agent:setting-stt.enabled`) without writing it. A shared tab shows no line under the field (see Multi-viewer). The subagent steer box (`SteerSubagentForm`) grows like the main field (`fitFieldHeight`) and is never `disabled` while sending (`aria-busy`).
  - Phone composer (user's rule, 19 Oct 2026: **composer controls never move outside the composer box, and the phone composer never folds**): the box is always open, a full-width field row over ONE compact controls row inside the box, the tablet's toolbar at phone size: `+`, the Run chip (a glyph: the mode's icon, the mode's name while one is on, a warning pip while tool approval is full access; its sheet is the one-line Run sheet below), the mode extras, its own Thinking and Delegation chips (`PhoneRunChips.tsx`: Brain + the level's short name as a tiny badge, Network + how many subagents run at a time; the full value is the accessible name; each opens its field in a focus-keeping `ComposerSheet`), the model chip (its provider mark, `ProviderMark`, and a name that ellipsizes inside its own box, 3.5 em; the mark alone under 340 px of toolbar and while a turn runs), the context chip (`ContextUsagePopover variant="chip"`: `34 %`, its breakdown a bottom sheet through `ComposerSheet`), Stop/Send|mic. Labels fold to icons by the toolbar's own width (`omp-toolbar` container); everything before Stop/Send is a sideways scroller (`omp-composer-scroll`, a phone-only wrapper in `ComposerToolbar`; 8 px of padding taken back by the margin keeps the 44 px reach; its chips sit 4 px apart with `--hit-gap-x: 4px`, so no chip's reach covers a neighbour) and Stop/Send/the queue pill sit outside it at the row's end, never scrolling or moving, so nothing folds and no ⋯ toggle, status row or settings sheet outside the box exists (a ⋯ fold toggle and a 44 px status chip row above the box were both rejected by the user). **Nothing runs under Stop/Send** (reported on a Galaxy, 390 px, ko: the model chip's name was cut at the scroller's edge and the context chip sat under Send, 67 px of overflow with the labelled chips, the Run chip's three glyphs and 8 px gaps): idle at 390 and 412 the whole row fits with no scroll (the scroller ends 4 px before Send), and while a turn runs (Stop and the lane chip appear, the lane chip is its icon alone) it fits at 390 too, the model being its mark; at 360 the model is its mark alone and a running row may scroll, clipped at the scroller's own edge before Stop (`e2e/phone-run-sheet.e2e.ts` "keeps clear of Stop and Send" at 360/390/412, en and ko: the scroller ends before Stop/Send, the model chip ≤ 130 px with an ellipsis, every control keeps ≥ 90 % of its own taps). The `+` sheet carries attach/photos/history/expand/goal/skills only. Controls keep the field focused with `onMouseDown` `preventDefault` only, never `pointerdown` (it swallows the click in WebKit; `keep-focus.ts`). The field spans the box's inner width at every width: a 30-character Korean sentence stays on one line at 390 (`e2e/mobile-composer.e2e.ts` "wraps a long Korean line only at the box's inner width"; reported on a Galaxy S25 Ultra, Samsung Internet, v0.38.2: the old one-row grid's field wrapped Korean at about half the box). The field is capped at `min(40 % of the pane, visible − 120 px)` (`field-height.ts`) and refits on pane/visualViewport height changes. Touch sets `--omp-scrollbar-size: 0`. Measured at 390 px on this branch: idle chrome 14.3 % without suggestions and 21.0 % with the chip row (budget 22 %, 18.6 % before); keyboard-up transcript 351 → 379 px; 20 attachments box 309 → 104 px; key latency at 4× CPU with 20 attachments p50 78 → ~20 ms. Enter on a touch keyboard: see the next bullet.
  - The Run sheet is one line per row (user's rule, 19 Oct 2026, from phone screenshots: descriptions under every row, a full-width select with a caption, loose paragraphs, three 2-line radio rows for approval and five for delegation; the popover on the desktop and tablet is the same content at its own width, `w-96`): every row of the sheet, the Mode section and Advanced is a lead glyph and its control on ONE line, and every explanation sits behind an (i) glyph (`SettingInfo`, the text kept in the DOM): no `data-setting-desc`, no `<p>`, no footer line. Switches are **tiles** (`common/Switch.tsx` `SwitchTile`: lucide glyph, a short label that truncates, (i), the switch; the whole tile toggles) two to a row in a `data-switch-grid` (`grid-cols-2`): Fast | Slow, Ultrafast (`SpeedFields`), then in Advanced (`AdvancedAndRoles`, opens by itself while a value is not the default) Auto-compact | Extended, Auto-retry | Prewalk, Batch steers | Interrupt; glyphs Zap, Turtle, Gauge, Shrink, Maximize2, RotateCw, Rocket, Layers, OctagonX; the "not on this model" badge of Fast became a dimmed glyph and a sentence in the (i) text. Choices are **segmented rows** (`ChoiceRow.tsx`: the group's glyph, a radio row of short labels, (i) holding the group's name and the held option's effect; the full name is each segment's accessible name and tooltip; arrows move focus, `selectOnArrow` also picks): thinking level (`ThinkingLevelField`, short names `input.thinking.short.*`, variant `row`; the model picker keeps `segmented`), tool approval (`ApprovalModeField`, shield glyphs, `input.approval.short.*`, full access still asks first through the host's dialog) and delegation (`DelegationField`, `input.delegation.short.*`; its "applies from the next turn" note joined the (i)). The compaction point is one line with its value in the label ("압축 시점 · 850k", `ContextSizeField`, the only select left, compact at the right, its other-layer note in the (i)). The short labels are the long names' abbreviations (ko/en parity); tile labels ("Auto-compact", "자동 압축") replaced the old sentences, which became the (i) texts (`input.more.*Desc`). `mobile.css` bounds the reaches (`[data-choice-row]`, `[data-switch-grid]`: `--hit-gap` 4 px) and gives a segment and a tile the touch row height. Tests: `RunChip.sheet.test.tsx` (no visible description or paragraph, an (i) per explanation, tiles two to a row with their glyphs, three segmented rows, one select), `ThinkingControl`/`ApprovalControl`/`DelegationField`/`ContextSizeField` tests, `ComposerToolbar.phone.test` (icon-first chips) and `e2e/phone-run-sheet.e2e.ts` (OMP_GUI_RUN_SHEET=<dir> captures at 390 light/dark × en/ko: the composer idle and running, the sheet, its full height, the Goal and Autopilot tabs, and the desktop popover; at 360/390/412 the sheet has no overflow, every tile label stays on one line, tiles pair up, a segmented row is ≤ 56 px, and every switch and segment keeps ≥ 85 % of its own taps; the desktop popover keeps its width and cuts no label).
  - Composer tests: `e2e/composer-toolbar.e2e.ts`, `e2e/composer-queue-view.e2e.ts`, `e2e/mobile-composer.e2e.ts`, `e2e/mobile-enter.e2e.ts`, `e2e/phone-run-sheet.e2e.ts`; component tests `InputArea.queue-run`, `InputArea.touch-enter`, `ComposerToolbar.test` / `ComposerToolbar.phone.test`, `RunChip.sheet.test`, `ComposerStatusRow.test`, `ComposerQueue.test`, `ComposerRestoredQueue.test`, `field-height.test`, `use-completion-menu.menu.test`.
  - Narrow and first-paint edges of the rework: below a 440 px toolbar (`@container omp-toolbar`, a pane split in four, a 200 % zoom) the reserved slots that are off give their room back (`display: none`), because three of them cost Send its place (`e2e/desktop.e2e.ts` "narrow windows and 200 percent zoom"); only there does the row move when a turn starts. The phone's idle chrome measured 18.4 % with the always-open composer and `e2e/responsive-smoke.e2e.ts` gates it at 19.4 % (measured + 1; 16 % before the status chip row, 22 % with it). The first-paint shell follows the phone composer: `COMPOSER_BOX_PX.phone` 77 px and `--omp-scrollbar-size: 0` under `(pointer: coarse)` in `scripts/boot-shell.ts` (regenerate `web.html` with `bun run gen:icons`; `e2e/no-loading.e2e.ts` compares the boot box with the React box within 2 px).
  - Composer design record (19 Oct 2026): three real design choices were prototyped three ways on the dev harness (real components, tokens and locale strings over fake state; Playwright measurements, Chromium plus WebKit for the phone; light/dark × en/ko at 1280/900/834/390 and narrow split widths) and scored 0-3 on: fewest taps, state visible without opening, density, no layout jumps, touch + a11y, ko + narrowest width, stock semantics with no capability lost. **Toolbar → one Run chip with reserved slots and a toolbar-owned container** (19 of 21 against 14 for the repaired eight-control row and 16 for a Codex-style row): 6 controls instead of 8, Thinking in 2 taps instead of 3, nothing moves when a turn starts (the old row wrapped to 70 px at 340 px and shifted controls by up to 128 px in x and 29 px in y), and Plan mode moves nothing to its left; the Codex-style variant lost the always-present Mode chip. **Phone → the status chip row** (17 of 21 against 15 for a Run-controls sheet and 10 for a repaired one-row): state visible without opening, 2 taps for model/thinking/approval/delegation (3 before), 1 tap for Plan, voice and moving or removing a queued item, no layout shift when a sheet opens (52 px for the repaired row); it costs idle height (19.8 % in the prototype against 14.1 %), which the 22 % budget absorbed. **Superseded the same day by the user's rule**: composer controls never leave the composer box and the phone composer never folds, so the status row and the ⋯ sheet variant are both gone; the phone box is the tablet toolbar at phone size under a full-width field (measured 18.4 % idle chrome, preferred over a lower share), see "Phone composer". **Queue → a pill and popover** (15 of 21, tied with an in-flow tray and a queue-in-the-box, but the only one that wins the two heaviest criteria): the transcript moves 0 px when the first item queues, against 75 px (desktop) / 83 px (phone) for the tray and 40 / 44 px for the queue in the box, and 0 px at ten items against 33 or 104-112 px; the price is 2-3 taps for lane switching and editing where the box variant needed 1-2. Not decided by prototype: undo granularity, atomic paste markers, `@` ranking, keyboard route to attachments, paste menu anchoring, strip cap, drop target, focus after a tab switch, draft flush on page hide.
- Enter on a touch input (`useShellInput() === "touch"`; desktop byte for byte unchanged): a soft keyboard's return key adds a line and only the Send button (or ⌘↵ / the follow-up chord) sends, `enterkeyhint="enter"` on the field. A physical keyboard on a tablet gets the desktop's Enter back: `lib/hardware-keyboard.ts` latches the first key only such a keyboard makes (Esc, Tab, arrows, Home/End/Page keys, F-keys, a chord with ⌘ ⌃ ⌥, Shift with a named key such as Shift+Enter; never a letter, Backspace or Enter, which some soft keyboards report with a `code`) for the page's life. Settings › Appearance & input › "Enter sends on touch keyboards" (`displayPreferences.mobileEnterSends`, per device, default off) makes touch Enter send. The IME guard runs first, so a composition's Enter is never a send or a newline rule. In `!`/`$` shell drafts the field turns autocorrect, autocapitalize and spellcheck off. Pinned by `lib/hardware-keyboard.test.ts`, `InputArea.touch-enter.test.tsx` and `e2e/mobile-enter.e2e.ts` (Chromium + WebKit, en/ko).
- Interrupted turns continue by themselves (`src/main/turn-resume.ts`, `src/main/connectivity.ts`, Settings › General › Continue interrupted work after relaunch, pref `resumeInterrupted`, default on). **Two paths, one coordinator (`TurnResume`), driven by `SidecarPool.onRunningChange` (pool facts via `resumeFacts()`).** ① Relaunch: eligibility is a marker file `<userData>/interrupted-turns.json` ({window, tab, peer, sessionPath, cwd, kind, startedAt, reason}) rewritten atomically on every run-state transition — never in a quit hook, because SIGKILL/crash runs none. It exists while a turn runs (`running`), is cleared on normal completion and on the user's Stop (`SidecarPool.noteStopped`, called by the RPC dispatcher for `STOP_COMMANDS` `abort`/`abort_and_prompt`/`abort_and_restore_queue`, so a kill before the run's end event still leaves no marker), and a turn blocked on an ask/approval keeps it as `asking` (the relaunch re-runs the cut-off call, which reopens the card; it is not counted as "continuing"). An omp that exits or restarts takes its open asks/approvals with it: the pool forgets their owners on any non-ready status, so no `asking` outlives its process. A quit (`quitApproved()`) freezes writes: tearing the tabs down must not read as the turns ending. Closing the last shown window on Windows (close-to-tray off) is such a quit, asked for before the window goes (`lastWindowCloseAction` in `quit-guard.ts`): the confirmation runs and the markers stay, instead of the window's tabs ending first and `window-all-closed` quitting afterwards. At launch (`TurnResume.launch()`, after the saved windows exist) each marker finds its tab by session path — a deferred/background tab is started with `SidecarPool.startTab`, a tab missing from the layout is opened — and continues through stock: `restore_interrupted` (local handler → bridge `composer.restore_interrupted` → stock `restoreSession`, the code behind `omp --restore`, which is TUI-only as a flag) for a cut-off turn, `retry` (`/retry`, the quota-resume path) for a failed one. A killed OMPdesk's omp usually closes the turn itself before it exits (stdin EOF: a pending ask/approval fails with "RPC client disconnected before extension UI response completed" and the run is journaled aborted), so `restoreSession` finds nothing unfinished; the relaunch then runs stock `retry`, which replays that failed tool batch (the card reopens) or the aborted reply, and does nothing for a turn that really ended. A continuation waits for its tab's omp to be ready (at most `RESUME_READY_TIMEOUT_MS`; an omp that already failed drops the marker at once), and the resuming row's Stop, the waiting row's Cancel and a new prompt on a held tab call it off even during that wait (a per-tab generation it checks before every command). **The host owns the turns it runs for a paired device.** On the device (A) a remote tab (`device`) is skipped entirely: no marker, no judging, no takeover by A's connectivity. On the host (B) `index.ts` composes `TurnResume`'s pool from `SidecarPool` and `LocalHostServices` (`resumeFacts`/`startTab`/`setAwaitingConnection`/`noteStopped`/`tabForSession`, ids `peer:<deviceId>:<rtab>`, kept when the tab is re-keyed; run bookkeeping shared through `trackRunEvent` in `sidecar-run-state.ts`; asks tracked from the tab's `extensionUi` and the device's `extension_ui_response`; the device's Stop through `tab.command`), so B writes the marker (with `peer`), continues the turn after its own crash or quit — the marker revives the hosted tab, unstarted and waiting for its device in the grace period (`RemoteDevices.reviveHostTab`, only while hosting is on) — and judges a network failure by B's connectivity. That grace period (`HOST_TAB_GRACE_MS`, 10 min from the device's link loss, `LocalHostServices.#startGrace`) closes only an idle hosted tab: one that runs out while the tab runs a turn (a revived one included) or holds it for B's connection starts another, so the turn runs to its end and keeps its marker until then. A turn blocked on an ask that waits for the device therefore keeps its omp until the device answers or B quits; there is no overall cap. A only mirrors that wait (the `awaitingConnection` tab event, emitted ahead of the failing events and re-sent on every `tab.open`; the row reads "Waiting for {device}'s internet connection…") and sends its Cancel to B (`RemoteSidecar.cancelHostWait` → `tab.cancelWait`). A relaunched device reopens its tabs under new rtabs: `tab.open` with a session path takes over the tab an earlier connection of that device left on it (`#adopt`), so no second omp starts on the same session. One session file is one host tab and one omp, whatever the device or connection: an open of a file a tab of this same connection still shows under another rtab, or another device's tab holds, is refused `session_open_in_tab`; `#open` re-checks the holder after its awaits, so concurrent opens of one file start one omp; and a tab whose omp moves onto a file another host tab holds (`#dedupe`, on its `session` identity) stops the other one, keeping a tab that runs a turn, and tells its device `exited` (`host-services.test.ts`, three cases written fail-first). A host's session closes and deletes like a local one: its sidebar row (`SidebarDevices.tsx`) has Close (the tab; the session stays on the host) and Delete (the confirm names the host, `remote.sidebar.deleteMessage`; the tab closes first, then `sessions.delete` runs on the host). The host's `#deleteSession` releases the asking device's own tabs on that file that run no turn (one left by an earlier connection, a duplicate) and waits for their omp to exit, and refuses while one runs a turn or another device's live tab holds it (`host-services.test.ts`, `Sidebar.test.tsx`, the delete test of `e2e/remote-resume.e2e.ts`: gone on the host and after both apps relaunch). A relaunch announces itself with no toast (the "Continuing N interrupted tasks" toast and its Stop covered the screen and are gone, with `resume.takeNotice`): each resuming tab shows its own calm row ("Continuing where it left off…", `data-resuming`, one per tab) and the Stop the toast had sits on that row (`resume.stop(tabId)` → `TurnResume.stopResumed(tabId)`: that tab only, noted as the user's Stop so its marker goes and Autopilot's goal resume ends `cancelled`). Tabs continue in rank order, the window's focused tab first, then the other panes of its split, then the background tabs (`ResumePool.resumeRank` ← `SidecarPool.resumeRank`), so the work the user is looking at starts before the rest compete for spawns. With the setting on, a quit (⌘Q, tray, the last window on Windows) and "Restart to update" ask nothing (`quitNeedsConfirmation(risk, resumes)` in `quit-guard.ts`, `confirmQuit()` in `app-quit.ts`, the updater's `UPDATER_APPLY`); with it off they ask before stopping running work. ② Connectivity: `ConnectivityMonitor` (net.isOnline() sampled by edge, the windows' `online`/`offline` events via `resume.reportOnline`, `powerMonitor` suspend/resume with a DNS probe until the wake is real, and `RESUME_WINDOW_MS` after a wake) decides `shouldWait()`. A turn that ends with a network-class error (`isNetworkError`, a subset of stock's retryable pattern) while `shouldWait()` is held, not failed: the tab shows `awaitingConnection` (TurnStatusRow's calm "Waiting for the internet connection…" row with Cancel → `resume.cancelWait`; TurnErrorNotice stays hidden), keeps a `network` marker, and runs stock `retry` once online; a new run on the held tab (a prompt sent meanwhile, not the taken-over retry saga still ending) replaces the wait. Tests: `connectivity.test.ts`, `turn-resume.test.ts` (marker transitions incl. the crash case, skip rules), `remote/host-services.test.ts` (hosted facts, revive, adoption, a revived turn outliving the grace, the open asks each re-attach re-sends), `e2e/interrupted-resume.e2e.ts` (SIGKILL and normal quit relaunch, Stop, setting off, offline wait/Cancel/online; en/ko × light/dark captures), `e2e/remote-resume.e2e.ts` (host crash, device crash, the host's offline wait across a lost link and its Cancel, a question across a cut link with and without a host omp restart; two apps, en/ko × light/dark), `e2e/real-resume.e2e.ts` (opt-in, the real omp); the fake omp implements `composer.restore_interrupted`, `fixture network error` and `/retry` for it.
  - One conversation stays one session across a relaunch: a tab whose session file exists starts on it (`--session`), the continuation answers into that file, and the sidebar lists it once. `e2e/interrupted-resume.e2e.ts` asserts it after the kill and the normal quit (the answer in the cut-off session's file, no second session file, one indexed sidebar row); on the real omp `e2e/real-update-restart.e2e.ts` (a)/(a2) records the sidebar rows and wants one, and the SIGKILL test of `e2e/real-resume.e2e.ts` wants one row (17 Oct 2026: one row each; (a) had no file at the quit and continued as one fresh session, (a2) and the SIGKILL kept writing their own file). The two rows once seen in the README resume clip were the fake omp writing a resumed journal to a second file of its own (see the fake omp under Build, test), never OMPdesk.
- What a relaunch continues beyond the cut-off turn (measured on the real omp by `e2e/real-update-restart.e2e.ts`, opt-in `OMP_GUI_REAL_WORK=1`, "Restart to update" = an approved quit while work runs; each observation records its spend): a foreground bash call is journaled "outcome unknown" and runs again; subagents (awaited or left in the background after the turn yielded) are revived by stock `restoreSession` (their cut-off call reruns and the parent gets the result, a waiting `wait` included, when stock continues the parent's turn); an open ask/approval reopens. Four things stock leaves behind are handled by OMPdesk: background bash jobs (`async: true`) the last process started and never settled are named to the agent in one follow-up (`ompdesk-restart-jobs`, bridge `composer.restore_interrupted` → `resources/gui-bridge/restart-core.ts` `interruptedJobs`, reported back as `jobs`) so it runs again what is still needed, quietly: the model gets the text `restartFollowUp` builds (each job as `- <id>: <intent> — \`command\``, the intent being the call's own `i` argument, then "start again only the jobs the work still needs, say nothing about the restart or this notice, carry on"), the transcript gets one muted line ("Re-running N background task(s) the restart stopped", `RestartNote`, `data-restart-jobs`, the intents as its hover title) because the message is displayed with `details` holding only the job ids and intents — never a user bubble and never a raw command, which a hidden (`display: false`) message left to chance (read from the entries journaled before this process started, from after the second-to-last `session_exit` among them, never cut at a `session_exit` alone; a job an earlier relaunch already named counts as settled; pinned on stock by the SIGKILL test of `bridge-autopilot-stock.test.ts`, whose journal has no `session_exit`); the subagents the restore revived for a session whose own turn did not go on are named in that same one follow-up (see "Revived subagents of an idle session are told" below); a goal that was active until the last process ended (`goalActiveAtExit`: the last `mode_change` journaled before this process started, `performance.timeOrigin`, is `goal`; never read from a `session_exit`, which stock does not journal for a SIGKILL or a crash and not always when its stdin closes mid-turn — omp 18.11.3 journaled only the aborted turn in `bridge-autopilot-stock.test.ts`, and an Autopilot run then came back paused `app_closed`) and that stock paused at startup comes back as `goalPausedByExit`, and `TurnResume` resumes it once after the restore (`resumeGoal`, injectable for autopilot;
