default local `set_goal` `resume`; `goalSettled` then tells Autopilot `resumed`, `none` or `cancelled`); a new session's first turn cut off before stock wrote its file (it does at the first assistant message) left nothing on disk, so the pool keeps that prompt (`unsavedPrompt`, marker `prompt`) and the relaunch sends it again when restore and retry find nothing. The saved layout drops that missing path (`tab-layout.ts`), so its tab comes back as a fresh session in the same folder, and `TurnResume.launch` takes that tab for the marker (`#unwrittenSessionTab`: a local tab in the marker's folder with no session file yet, one per marker), never a hidden second one; only without such a tab does `openTab` open one, and a spawn whose `--session` file does not exist starts fresh in its folder instead (`#spawn` in `sidecar.ts`, with the fresh-launch config so no older session auto-resumes; stock exits "Session not found" on it, and this also covers an omp restart of a tab never used). The prompt leaves the marker before it is sent, so it is sent once. While a launch marker waits for its turn the tab shows a calm "Continuing where it left off…" row with a Stop (`setResuming` → tab `resuming` → `TurnStatusRow`, `data-resuming`; not for a reopened card), the only resume indicator. Measured on the real omp (18 Oct 2026, `e2e/real-update-restart.e2e.ts` (b2), `OMP_GUI_REAL_WORK=1`, 59 s, $0.155 / 91k tokens): one resuming row, no toast, the restart notice one muted line, no bubble for it and none of its text on screen, the transcript at the bottom, and the only text the model wrote after the notice was `STARTED`, the word the prompt asked for (no narration of the restart); the observation records `ui` and `saidAfterNotice`. Pinned by `turn-resume.test.ts` (incl. the rank order), `sidecar.test.ts`, `bridge-restart.test.ts` (intent, notice wording, details without a command), `MessageBubble.test.tsx` (the muted line, not a bubble), `ChatStream.turn-status.test.tsx` (the row's Stop), `quit-guard.test.ts`, `e2e/interrupted-resume.e2e.ts` (a relaunch with a background job: no toast, one resuming row, one muted notice line and no bubble for it, the transcript at the bottom; a session idle while its background subagent works: one muted subagents line and a turn; and for the setting off: the quit asks, "Quit anyway" leaves no marker).
  - Revived subagents are seen: the bridge's `composer.restore_interrupted` hands stock `restoreSession` the buses a live `task` run reports on (the task tool's stock `ToolSession` `eventBus`/`subagentEventBus`; TaskTool keeps it in a TS-private `session` field, `getToolByName("task")` is in `capabilities.ts`), as `omp --restore` passes main.ts's own. Without them a revived subagent ran and delivered its result but omp's RPC subagent registry (`get_subagents`, the `subagent_*` frames the work board, the dock and the composer's background cue read) never heard of it (reported on v0.35.1). Pinned by `src/main/bridge-restore-subagents-stock.test.ts` (installed omp, scripted loopback provider, no spend: a background subagent cut off by SIGKILL is listed `running` after the restore).
  - Revived subagents of an idle session are told: when stock's restore revived subagents but `turn` is false, the session would sit silent until a subagent's async result arrives (maybe hours later). Two ways this happens, both seen on 18 Oct 2026 in the user's main session: the turn had already ended and only the background subagents kept the marker (v0.38.1: `restored: subagents:6`, then nothing), or stock declined to continue a cut-off turn (v0.37.0 on omp 18.12.1: the parent was in `wait` on its background subagent; the session had used `eval`, so stock's `eval-state-context` custom message follows the interrupted-turn record and both `restoreInterruptedTurn` and `/retry` answer "Nothing to retry", `retryableAssistantTurnEnd` wanting the abort last — reproduced on a copy of that session and pinned on stock; reported as oh-my-pi#16195 and fixed in omp 18.12.2, whose `conversationEnd` looks past trailing eval-state-context messages, so there the turn is restored and its `wait` reruns; MIN_OMP_VERSION still admits older omp, where the follow-up is what wakes it). The bridge then adds the revived subagents to the relaunch's one follow-up (`restartFollowUp(jobs, outcome.turn ? [] : revivedSubagents(branch, outcome.subagents))`; customType `ompdesk-restart-subagents`, or `ompdesk-restart-jobs` when jobs come with them, so one message and one turn): the model gets each subagent as `- <id>: <intent>` (the intent is its `task` assignment's `Title:` line, else its first line, `assignmentHeadline`, cut at 120 characters; a child of a subagent has its id alone) and is told to check them, tell the user in a sentence or two what is still in progress, keep supervising, and say nothing about the restart or how the work came back; the transcript shows one muted line ("Continuing N background task(s) after the restart" / "재시작 뒤 백그라운드 작업 N개를 이어서 진행해요", `RestartNote`, `data-restart-subagents`, the intents as its hover title) from `details.subagents`. A restored turn gets none (its cut-off `wait` reruns and finds the revived job). The bridge reports `followUp: true`; `TurnResume` counts revived subagents as continuing, so nothing is retried on top, and a launch calls the restore once per marker, so the follow-up comes once per relaunch. Measured on the real omp (18 Oct 2026, `e2e/real-update-restart.e2e.ts` (c1), `OMP_GUI_REAL_WORK=1`, 58 s, $0.384 / 162k tokens): one muted subagents line, no toast, none of the notice's text on screen, the parent answered "SleepWriter가 아직 실행 중입니다. 40초 대기 후 `sub-done.txt`에 기록하는 작업이고, 끝나면 결과가 자동으로 전달됩니다." (no restart narration), then the subagent's result arrived as before. Pinned by `bridge-restore-subagents-stock.test.ts` (no spend: an ended turn gets one follow-up the parent answers; a cut-off `wait` in an eval journal is restored by stock ≥ 18.12.2 with no follow-up and gets one follow-up on older omp, the test reading the installed `omp --version` (checked on 18.12.1 and 18.12.2); a cut-off `wait` stock continues gets none), `bridge-restart.test.ts`, `turn-resume.test.ts`, `MessageBubble.test.tsx`, `e2e/interrupted-resume.e2e.ts`.
  - Every relaunch decision is logged to `<userData>/logs/gui-runtime.jsonl` (source `turn-resume`, `TurnResume`'s injected `log`): `launch` {markers, enabled}, one `marker` per marker {session, reason, tab, via: layout | unwritten | opened | none, peer}, one `continue` per continuation {tab, action, restored: turn | subagents:N,jobs:N | nothing | failed | skipped, retried, prompt, goal, started, followUp, cancelled, and why a command did not answer: restoreError/restoreCode, retryError/retryCode} or {ready: false} when its omp never came up. `retried: false` means no retry continued anything, not that none was sent: a failed restore always falls back to stock `retry`, and both errors land on the line. `restore_interrupted` and `retry` get `RESUME_COMMAND_TIMEOUT_MS` (2 min), not the RPC client's 8 s default: stock's restore reads every subagent transcript of the session first, and on v0.37.0 a session with 577 of them (1.1 GB) answered after 8 s, so the reply was cut and logged `failed` while stock went on reviving (`Restored interrupted subagents` in omp's log 0.9 s after OMPdesk logged `failed`). A tab that "did not continue" is explained from the user's own files; pinned by `turn-resume.test.ts`.
- Branch from here (`sessions.fork_from`, the message row's Branch and the tree's "Open in new window"; rules in dependency-free `resources/gui-bridge/session-fork-core.ts` `forkCut`, used by the bridge and the fake omp) never ends the copy on something nothing will answer, which stock reads as a turn cut off (`findUnfinishedTurn`: the next omp journals "Previous OMP process exited before completing the turn." and a restore would run it). From a user prompt the copy ends before it and the prompt comes back as `draft` (stock `branch` semantics): `forkSessionFromEntryInNewTab` puts text and pictures into the new tab's composer, and `fileBranchDraft` files the text as the new session's saved draft (`draft-persistence.ts`) for the tree's new window and a branch that could not open its tab. Inside a tool batch the copy runs through the recorded results (stock `fork(entryId)` `#resolveForkLeaf`); a batch with a call still awaiting its result is cut before its assistant message. On touch (`useShellInput() === "touch"`) Branch sits in the message row's ⋯ `MenuButton`, never as a direct button a scroll-stop tap can hit. Reported on v0.35.1: a Branch tapped on the phone under a just-sent prompt made a same-titled session ending on that prompt, which looked like a turn the restart never continued. Pinned by `bridge-session-fork-stock.test.ts`, `messages.test.ts`, `MessageBubble.test.tsx` (touch), `e2e/branch-from-prompt.e2e.ts`.
  - A fork shows where it came from, so one that kept its parent's title never reads as a second copy of it (the user's two "추천 프롬프트 중복" rows): stock's `parentSession` header (`SessionInfo.parentSessionPath`, a path or, after a move, the parent's id) → `sessionForkOrigin` (`lib/session-fork.ts`) gives the parent's title, the words for screen readers and one tooltip line "분기 · <parent> · <time>" / "Branch · <parent> · <time>" (`session.fork.*`). The sidebar row draws the ⑂ (`GitFork`, `data-sidebar-fork`) and a muted "← <parent>" after its title (`layout/ForkParent.tsx`, `data-fork-parent`); the title bar puts the same pair after the session name (`data-titlebar-fork`). A parent the index no longer lists leaves the ⑂ and "Branch · <time>". Pinned by `Sidebar.test.tsx` ("marks a fork") and `TitleBar.test.tsx`.
- Dev server → Preview (`src/renderer/lib/panel-auto-open.ts`, fed per tab event batch by `use-rpc-events.ts`; pref `autoPreviewDevServer` default **off** since 19 Oct 2026 (the user found panels opening by themselves disruptive; only a stored `true` turns it on, a phone never auto-opens whatever the pref), read from main prefs at event time, Settings › Agent behavior › Smart automation): a bash result holding a loopback address **with a port**, from a server-looking command or banner (`devServerUrl`; never a `curl`/`wget` client), opens the Preview with it when the switch is on; otherwise (and always) the bash card shows a quiet "Open Preview"/"미리보기 열기" link under its output (`data-bash-open-preview`, `BashRenderer.tsx`) that moves no layout. Guards, pinned by `panel-auto-open.test.ts` and `e2e/panel-auto-open.e2e.ts`: only the focused tab on the session page (`activeTabId`; a split's other pane never); never a paired host's tab (the Preview blocks its loopback); never over unsaved Files edits (`fileEditDirty`/`pendingFileSwitch`); never moves focus (the composer's focus is restored); a URL is offered once per session at its first detection, so re-detection and a preview the user closed stay closed; an open panel is replaced only when it shows a passive view left alone for `PANEL_IDLE_MS` (terminal, files, preview and subagent views never).
- Edits → Changes (same module, pref `autoOpenChanges` default **off**, same opt-in and phone rule (the phone rule sits in `openPanel`, so it also holds for the Autopilot screen opening at a run's start); with it off the work card's "Open Changes" chip is the way in): a turn that edited files (an `EDIT_TOOLS` call ended without error, or `get_git_fingerprint` moved against the baseline read at `agent_start`; the tool-only case also needs `get_git_status` to show changes) opens Changes at its terminal `agent_end`, never mid-stream. Guards: only the focused tab (never a split's other pane), only while the side panel is closed (never over another view), not over unsaved Files edits, once per turn, and after the user closes the panel it opened, not again for that session until they open the panel themselves.
- Batched notifications (`src/main/notification-batch.ts`, pref `batchNotifications` default on, Settings › Agent behavior › Smart automation): `SYSTEM_NOTIFY` carries `kind` (`completion`|`error`|`ask`, passed by `maybeNotify`) and main batches per window: the first event of a window shows at once and opens a fixed 4 s window, followers are recorded one per tab (ask > error > completion), and at the end with followers the first banner is closed and one summary ("3 tasks finished · 1 needs you", strings `batch.*` in `i18n-text.ts`) replaces it, a click focusing needs-you first, then failed, then the latest finished. Never batched: a notify without `kind` or tab, pref off; the 1.5 s dedupe and the renderer's `resent` ask rule run before it. Timers are `unref`'d and cleared with the window and at quit.
- Scheduled retry (`TurnResume` in `src/main/turn-resume.ts`, pref `scheduledRetry` default on, Settings › Agent behavior › Smart automation, `ScheduledRetryRow.tsx`): once stock's own auto-retries are spent and a turn ends in a transient error (`isTransientError`: stock's retryable-error pattern with word-bounded 5xx codes, minus auth, quota/rate limit, bad request, context overflow, certificates), main arms stock `retry` after 30 s, 2 min, 10 min (three attempts; `OMP_GUI_RETRY_DELAYS_MS` shortens them for e2e), shown on `TurnErrorNotice` ("Retrying in 0:30…", Cancel through `RESUME_CANCEL_WAIT`, Retry now) from `scheduledRetry` on the pool entry (`IpcTabInfo`, so a renderer reload re-reads it). Guards: never a hosted/peer tab, a tab without a session file, after Stop, unless stock's `retry.enabled` reads `true` (`get_settings`), or for a network error the connection wait already holds (offline/post-wake); called off by any run that is not its own retry, Cancel, a session change or a `TOUCH_COMMANDS` edit (`touched` on the pool entry), the pref off, tab close and quit; a retry due while offline goes to the connection wait. The attempt count resets when a retry ends in anything but a transient error or the user runs something new. Pinned by `turn-resume.test.ts`, `TurnErrorNotice.test.tsx`, `e2e/scheduled-retry.e2e.ts`.
- Closing the last shown window hides it instead of ending the app (`lastWindowCloseAction` in `quit-guard.ts`, the `close` handler in `window.ts`, pref `closeToTray`, default **on**: only an explicit `false` keeps the Windows quit, read through `closeToTrayEnabled`). Windows needs a tray (`hasTray()`; headless runs have none, so they quit) and shows a one-time "OMPdesk keeps running in the tray" Notification the first time (pref `closeToTrayNoticeShown`). macOS hides regardless of the pref (its Settings row only describes it), so the window, its tabs and omp processes keep running; `app.on("activate")` shows the same hidden window (spawns one only if none exists). A non-last window (another window is visible) closes as before and ends its tabs: hiding it would leave no Dock or tray way back to it. A hidden window stays in `getAllWindows()`, so the tab inventory, notifications (a click shows it), keep-awake, badge and tray counts keep working. Real quits (menu, ⌘Q, tray, logout/update restart) still go through the quit guard; once `quitApproved()` every window closes. Tests: `quit-guard.test.ts`, the macOS `desktop-tabs` e2e (close during a fake turn, then `activate`), the `@smoke` tray test in `desktop-surfaces` (default pref).
- Quit watchdog (`src/main/quit-watchdog.ts`, electron-free with an injected clock/log/exit; wired in `app-quit.ts`): once a quit is committed — `before-quit` lets the teardown run (after `finalize`, which may still be asking the user), or `before-quit-for-update` fires — the process force-exits (`app.exit(0)`, a line in the runtime log with `source: "background-task"`, `details.watchdog: "quit"`) if it is still alive after 30 s (`QUIT_EXIT_TIMEOUT_MS`), because a wedged teardown otherwise strands the updater's swap helper or installer waiting for this pid. `app.exit` skips `will-quit`, so the relaunch swap registers `onQuitForcedExit` and starts through one once-only call (`updater.ts` `launchStagedSwap`). A failed `quitAndInstall()` (the autoUpdater `error` event) disarms the update's countdown. The idea is Orca's `update-install-exit-watchdog.ts` (MIT), reimplemented.
- Main-thread hang watchdog (`src/main/hang-watchdog.ts` wiring, `hang-watchdog-core.ts` pure rules with an injected clock, `hang-watchdog-worker.ts` the worker thread, built as its own `electron.vite.config.ts` main input `out/main/hang-watchdog-worker.js`; wired in `index.ts` right after `app-ready`, packaged builds or `OMP_GUI_HANG_WATCHDOG=1`, never under vitest or in a second instance): main posts a heartbeat to an in-process, unref'd `worker_threads` Worker every 1 s; the worker ticks every 1 s and records a stall once no heartbeat came for more than 5 s, in `<userData>/logs/main-thread-hang.json` ({detectedAt, pid, unresponsiveMs, selfRecovered}), rewritten with `selfRecovered: true` when heartbeats resume. A worker tick gap over 3× its interval is a system suspend, not a hang (the wait restarts), and `powerMonitor` `resume` resets it too. **Observation only**: nothing kills or restarts main. The next launch consumes the marker and writes one `gui-runtime.jsonl` line (`source: "main-thread-hang"`, `details` {unresponsiveMs, selfRecovered, detectedAt, previousPid}). The idea (worker + heartbeat, tick-gap sleep rule, marker reported next launch) is Orca's `src/main/hang-watchdog/` (MIT), reimplemented. Pinned by `hang-watchdog-core.test.ts`.
- Copy diagnostics (Settings › Updates & about, `settings/DiagnosticsRow.tsx`; channel `diagnostics:build` = `window.omp.runtime.diagnostics()`, `host-admin` in `ipc-policy.ts`; builder `src/main/diagnostics.ts`, registered in `index.ts` with `registerDiagnosticsIpc`, electron-free like `setup/ipc.ts`): the row's Preview shows one redacted text built in main on every open — OMPdesk/omp/Electron/Chrome/Node/OS versions, the machine setup items (phase, detected version and path, failure), and the last 100 lines of `<userData>/logs/gui-runtime.jsonl` (topped up from `.1`, each line cut at 3,000 characters) — and Copy puts exactly that text on the clipboard. **Nothing is uploaded or written**; no network. `src/main/redactor.ts` (`redactDiagnosticsText`, pure) hides, in order: URL userinfo, PEM blocks, JWTs, vendor tokens (`gh*_`, `github_pat_`, `sk-`, `xox*-`, `AKIA…`, `AIza…`), `Bearer` values, `key=value` whose key names a secret (`tokens` counts and `true/false/null/numbers` stay), hex runs ≥ 32 and mixed-case base64-ish runs ≥ 32, emails (`git@host` stays), tailnet addresses (100.64.0.0/10, `fd7a:115c:a1e0::`, `*.ts.net`), LAN addresses (10/8, 172.16/12, 192.168/16, `fe80::`, `fc00::/7`) and home folders (`/Users/<name>`, `/home/<name>`, `C:\Users\<name>`, also JSON-escaped, spaces in a Windows name) → `~`. Versions (semver, `10.0.22631.4890`), UUIDs, short hashes, loopback and public addresses and ordinary words stay; `redactor.test.ts` pins both sides. A new kind of secret or address a log can hold gets a rule and a test there; the rule list's idea (five families) is Orca's `observability/redactor.ts` (MIT), reimplemented. `runtime:log-tail` and the diagnostics share `tailLogLines` (`runtime-log-core.ts`).

### Where a new feature goes

1. Stock RPC already has it (`omp://rpc.md`) → call it; add a local handler only to reshape.
2. Stock implements it (an exported function or class in `@oh-my-pi/*`, an `omp <subcommand>`) → reuse that implementation through the bridge or the CLI; never port or re-derive its rules in OMPdesk.
3. Data the GUI can get on its own (git, files, `omp <subcommand>`) → local handler in main.
4. Needs the live session → bridge method + thin local handler.
5. Needs a change inside omp → contribute upstream; never fork.

**No duplicate of stock logic.** Before adding domain logic, search the stock packages (`~/.bun/install/global/node_modules/@oh-my-pi/`) for an implementation. If one exists, call it, even when that means a bridge method, and add its internals to `capabilities.ts`. Keep an OMPdesk copy only in two cases, and say why in a comment that names the stock counterpart:
- the copy is measurably better and stock cannot be used as is;
- it must run where stock cannot: the renderer, main before omp starts, or a remote client.

A test pins such a copy to stock behaviour: it compares against the stock function where the test can import it (bridge tests on a machine with omp), and otherwise uses the same cases stock's own tests use. Two OMPdesk copies of the same logic are never acceptable; merge them into one shared module.

When an omp update breaks a bridge method, fix the method against the new internals and raise `MIN_OMP_VERSION` if the fix requires it.

## Build, test

- Typecheck: `bun run check:types`. Lint/format: `bunx biome check src e2e resources scripts`. Surface policy (the SURFACE POLICY block in `src/renderer/styles/global.css`): `node scripts/lint-surfaces.mjs`; the same script fails a renderer `.ts`/`.tsx` function that tests a key for `"Enter"` without the shared IME guard (`isImeKeyEvent`, `src/renderer/lib/ime.ts`, so the Enter that commits a Korean composition never submits; rule in `scripts/lint-ime.mjs`, tested by `scripts/lint-ime.test.mjs`; a handler that can never see a text field says why with `// ime-ok: <reason>`).
- Supply chain: `bunfig.toml` sets `[install] minimumReleaseAge = 259200` (Bun skips a package version published less than 3 days ago when it resolves; bun.lock's pins are untouched, and `bun add`/`bun update` of a fresher release waits or needs `minimumReleaseAgeExcludes`), and every workflow's `oven-sh/setup-bun` pins one exact `bun-version` (never `latest`; bump all together). `src/main/supply-chain.test.ts` pins both.
- Unit/component tests: `bunx vitest run` (full suite must stay green; `vitest.setup.ts` pins `navigator.language` to en-US and `navigator.platform` to MacIntel, so keymap defaults and shortcut labels are the macOS ones on every runner — Windows behavior is tested by passing `"windows"` to the `lib/keymap.ts` functions); it also loads `scripts/vitest-real-home-guard.ts`, which makes any fs write anywhere under the **real** home (taken from `os.userInfo().homedir`, so a faked `HOME` does not hide it; only this checkout and the OS temp dir are exempt, never another `~/.omp/wt` worktree) throw and fail the test even when the code swallows the error — give the test a temp HOME, or set `OMP_GUI_ALLOW_REAL_HOME=1` in a suite that runs a real omp on purpose; child processes are outside the guard).
- Vitest has two projects (`vitest.config.ts`): `renderer` (`src/renderer/**`) runs on worker threads with `lucide-react`, `linkedom` and `react-dom` pre-bundled by the deps optimizer; `node` (everything else) keeps a child process per file, since those tests spawn processes, open sockets and edit `PATH`/env. They run one after the other (`sequence.groupOrder`; side by side each would take every worker), with as many workers as cores on GitHub's runners (`GITHUB_ACTIONS`; never key on `CI`, which agent shells on this Mac export too) and at most 8 locally (as fast as 14 here). Both keep per-file isolation; never turn `isolate` off.
- Windows test scope: release.yml's `win` job runs `bun run test:platform`, the suite minus `src/renderer/**`. Anything that can behave differently on Windows (paths, PATH lookup, shells and `.cmd` shims, spawn, sockets and named pipes, file modes) lives in main, shared, the bridge or scripts, so its tests run there by default. Renderer tests never read the host platform (pass `"windows"`/`win32` explicitly instead); `src/main/platform-test-scope.test.ts` fails on a renderer test that checks `process.platform` and pins the script and the workflow step.
- Build: `bun run build` (`scripts/build-cache.ts`: electron-vite → `out/`, then `scripts/check-main-bundle.ts`). It reuses `out/` when it was built from identical inputs: a SHA-256 over `src/` and `resources/` (test and bench files outside `src/renderer` excepted; Tailwind scans renderer tests for class names), the electron-vite config, `tsconfig*.json`, `package.json`, `bun.lock`, `.gitignore`, `.env*`, the build's own scripts and the build env (`NODE_ENV`, `MODE`, `NODE_PATH`, browserslist and the `*VITE_*` vars) is stored with the size and mtime of every `out/` file in `out/.build-stamp.json`, written only after the build and the bundle check passed (about 20 ms to check against ~8 s to build). A missing `out/` or stamp, a different hash, or a removed, added or rewritten `out/` file rebuilds; `OMPDESK_FORCE_BUILD=1` always does. It prints one `[build] reusing …`/`[build] building out/: <reason>` line. CI and packaging start from a fresh checkout (no stamp), so they always build; a local `package:*` reuses only a byte-identical-input `out/`, and electron-builder leaves the stamp out of the package.
- E2E: `bunx playwright test`. `playwright.config.ts` sets `OMP_GUI_HEADLESS=1`: windows are transparent, click-through, off the Dock/taskbar, and never focused, and the app creates no menu-bar tray item and never registers the `omp://` handler (both belong to the user's installed OMPdesk; a test's tray once stalled the main thread before the first window reached Playwright), so tests never cover the user's screen or rewrite their LaunchServices state. Set `OMP_GUI_HEADLESS=0` only when you must watch. `e2e/real-core.e2e.ts` and `e2e/deep-audit.e2e.ts` run against the real system omp (isolated agent dir, no credentials); the other suites use the fake omp in `e2e/sidecar-fixture.ts`, installed per test by `fixtureOmp()` (`e2e/harness.ts`: an `omp.cmd` shim on Windows) and passed as `OMP_GUI_OMP`; it speaks stock `--mode rpc-ui` and serves the gui-bridge methods those suites reach on `OMP_GUI_BRIDGE`. Tear apps down with `exitApp()` (same file; it kills a main process that does not act on the exit within 10 s); shortcuts in tests use `ControlOrMeta`. `e2e/performance.e2e.ts` only runs with `OMP_GUI_PERFORMANCE=1` (optional `OMP_GUI_BASELINE_DIR` compares another built checkout). `e2e/event-replay.e2e.ts` runs the fake omp with `OMP_GUI_TEST_REPLAY_FRAMES=1` (every message and tool frame sent again behind the next one) and main with `OMP_GUI_TEST_BATCH_INTERVAL_MS=400`, so snapshot loads overtake held frames; `src/renderer/hooks/event-replay.test.tsx` is its seeded unit counterpart. Renderer stores apply a frame for a message or call they already hold (by stock timestamp and call id) as a no-op. `OMP_GUI_TEST_INSTALLER=<executable>` turns on automatic machine setup in a test launch and replaces every install plan with `<executable> <item> <method>` (archive installs skip their release lookup); `e2e/machine-setup.e2e.ts` uses it with `OMP_GUI_OMP` naming a not-yet-existing fake omp (missing omp → automatic install fails → Retry → detected → the tab restarts ready), and `OMP_GUI_SETUP_CAPTURES=<dir>` adds its light/dark × en/ko and narrow captures. Real installs never run in a test against the user's profile: `scripts/setup-install-check.ts` runs the real Bun installer, PortableGit (Windows) and `bun install -g` omp through the same runner in a child with a temp HOME/USERPROFILE and BUN_INSTALL, checks `omp --version` against the minimum, and deletes the temp dir.
  - The fake omp's entry `e2e/sidecar-fixture.ts` only reports the version and dispatches on the invocation, in the order stock would: `--version`, `stats` (`e2e/fixture/stats.ts`), `update --check` (`update-check.ts`), `worktree … --json` (`worktree.ts`), `-p` (`print.ts`), then `--mode rpc-ui` (`rpc-ui.ts`: stock RPC on stdin/stdout and every gui-bridge method the suites reach; one closure, because its fake session state is shared by every command). The version it reports is `fixture/version.ts`. A new fake RPC command or bridge method goes into `rpc-ui.ts`, a new CLI invocation into its own `e2e/fixture/` module and the entry's dispatch. A fixture switch or branch no spec uses any more goes with the spec that used it.
  - With `OMP_GUI_TEST_SESSION_FILES=1` the fake's journal is a stock-shaped session file: a new session writes its own `sessions/fixture/fixture-<pid>.jsonl`, and a stored file that `--session` or `switch_session` opened keeps its header and takes every later entry, as stock's `SessionManager.open` does (`e2e/fixture/rpc-ui-session-file.test.ts`). Writing it elsewhere makes a relaunch's continued turn a second session the sidebar lists beside the first.
  - A run with no build never reaches Electron: Electron with a missing entry opens a native "Error launching app: Unable to find Electron app at …/main/index.js" dialog on the user's screen for every test, and `OMP_GUI_HEADLESS` does not stop it. `playwright.config.ts`'s `globalSetup` (`e2e/global-setup.ts`) resolves `<OMP_GUI_E2E_OUT ?? out>/main/index.js` once (`scripts/e2e-app-entry.ts`) and fails the whole run before any worker starts ("No build: … Run `bun run build` or `bun run check:changed`"); `launchApp` (`e2e/harness.ts`) asserts the same before `electron.launch`, which covers a spec run with a custom config. A spec that calls `electron.launch` itself uses `assertAppBuilt()` the same way. Pinned by `scripts/e2e-app-entry.test.ts` (Playwright on an empty `OMP_GUI_E2E_OUT`: fast non-zero exit, no process on that path). Never run Playwright in a fresh worktree before `bun run build`.
- E2E tiers (18 Oct 2026). `@smoke` (the tag in a test's title, ~20 tests) runs on every release's `mac` and `win` packaging jobs against the build they just made; it is the only e2e a release waits for. The default run (`bunx playwright test`: 204 ran, 170 skipped without their env) is the full suite: locally on demand (`bun run gate`, `check:changed` picks specs from it), on hosted runners nightly and on demand (`e2e-nightly.yml` and ci.yml's `e2e=true`, both through `e2e-shards.yml`; informational, never a release block). Variants and captures are opt-in: `variants(all, keep?)` in `e2e/harness.ts` keeps one variant (en light; for the mobile matrix one engine × device per shell mode) and does not register the rest unless `OMP_GUI_VARIANTS=1` (688 listed then), so they neither run nor show as skipped; `OMP_GUI_*=<dir>` capture tests register only with their dir set; CPU and compositing budgets run only with `OMP_GUI_PERFORMANCE=1`. Rules: no theme, language or size variant matrix as blocking tests (en light runs, the rest goes behind `variants()`); prefer a unit or component test (vitest with linkedom) for anything that needs no real Electron window, Chromium layout, real processes or the wire between them; an e2e asserts a flow across those, and its header names the component tests that cover the rest. To move an e2e down, write the unit case first, confirm it fails when the behaviour breaks (break the guard, run it, put the guard back), then delete the e2e and any fixture branch only it used.
- E2E parallelism: locally Playwright runs 4 workers (`playwright.config.ts`; 1 on GitHub's runners). Every test launches its own app on `mkdtemp` userData, agent dir and fake omp, with ephemeral ports (`OMP_GUI_REMOTE_PORT=0`, a fresh bridge socket per spawn), so a new spec never uses a fixed path, port or shared profile. The full suite (204 tests ran, 170 skipped without their env; 235 s on 18 Oct 2026, down from 10.4 min with 535 ran on 17 Oct once checks moved down to vitest and the variants went behind `OMP_GUI_VARIANTS`) is bound by its total work, not its slowest test: all four workers stay busy to the end (918 s of test time over 4 workers), and apps running at once slow each other down. A launch on the fake omp costs about 1 s here and 2 s on a hosted runner, so sharing an app between tests saves little; fewer tests is the lever. Split a long test into tests that run side by side only while it ends the run. `e2e/deep-audit.e2e.ts` (`mode: "parallel"`) splits its settings rows by measured cost over `SETTINGS_SHARDS` = 3 apps (~57 s each now); 6 shards ran ~24 s each but added ~26 s of work and left the wall time unchanged. 5 workers measured ~9% faster than 4 (80–84 s vs 88–92 s, three runs each) and 6 ~10%, at more load, so 4 stays.
- Responsive audit (`e2e/responsive-audit.ts` engine, `e2e/responsive-surfaces.ts` the ~86 named surfaces of the sidebar, transcript, composer, panels, Settings, centers, dialogs, host sheets, banners and toasts, `e2e/responsive-audit.e2e.ts` the matrix, `e2e/responsive-smoke.e2e.ts` the default run): the dev harness (`harness.html`; `?stress=1` adds the overflow-prone turns, `?host=1` a fake web host so the host sheets mount, `?pwa=push|ios`; `window.__stores` exposes the ui/toast/extension-ui/plan/updater/host-ui stores so a surface opens the way a click would) is visited at 320/360/390/412 (portrait) and 768/834/1024 (portrait 1.4 × and landscape 0.72 × the width), en and ko, light (dark at 390), with simulated safe areas (`--safe-*`: 47/34 px phone, 24/20 px tablet). Each surface reports page overflow, escaping elements, truncated controls, clipped text, off-screen popovers, hit boxes under 44 px (40 on a list row, 36 on a field or a cluster that declares `--hit-gap-*`), text under 11.5 px, overlaps, stolen taps (nine `elementFromPoint` samples across the 44 px hit box) and content under the insets; anything covered by a layer or scrolled out of its scroller is not measured, and `session-idle` carries the density figures (app chrome share, header + dock + composer without the insets; text gutter; body size). `OMP_GUI_RESPONSIVE=<dir>` (plus `_WIDTHS`, `_LANGS`, `_THEMES`, `_ORIENT`, `_AREA`, `_ONLY=<regex>`, `_SHOTS=all|none`) writes `<dir>/report.json`, `summary.md` (ranked surface × width) and `shots/`; run it one look per `bun run locked` hold (`-g "responsive 390P-light-en$"`, ≈ 1.5–2 min each) with `--trace off --output <private dir>`, because a Playwright locator action has no default timeout (the audit sets 8 s) and a shared `test-results/` is wiped by other agents' runs. The default smoke (390 + 834, en light, 10 surfaces, about 20 s) refuses page overflow, an escaping element, an off-screen popover and, at 390, an app chrome above 16 % of the screen.
- Build lock and gate: the machine-wide lock is one counting semaphore per kind of heavy work, because too much at once overloads this Mac until load-sensitive tests fail (`scripts/lock-slots.ts`, `scripts/build-lock.ts`). Slots: `build` 1 (builds into `out/` never overlap), `playwright` 4, `vitest` 1 (a full run with the static checks), `other` 1; the numbers are measured (see Local timings). A slot is the directory `/tmp/ompdesk-locks/<kind>.<i>` with an `owner` file (first line as before, `name HH:MM:SS pid N`; refreshed every minute, one older than 15 minutes counts as stale and the next waiter removes it); waiters keep a ticket in `<kind>.queue/` and are served first come, first served, a late short job never overtaking an earlier one. A Playwright run never reads `out/`: every `bun run build` (which takes the `build` slot itself) also publishes an immutable copy `out-<first 12 hex of the input hash>/` (an APFS clone; gitignored; deleted 30 minutes after a run last touched it; `scripts/out-snapshot.ts`), and `playwright.config.ts` pins `OMP_GUI_E2E_OUT` to the current one once per run, so a build another agent starts mid-run never changes the app under it. Every spec launches `path.resolve(process.env.OMP_GUI_E2E_OUT ?? "out", "main/index.js")` (a new spec does the same, or uses `launchApp`). A Playwright run that finds others holding slots gets fewer workers: `OMP_GUI_WORKERS` = min(4, ⌊8 ÷ runs holding a slot⌋), so 4 each for one or two runs and 2 each from three (set `OMP_GUI_WORKERS` or pass `--workers` to choose). `bun run gate` (`scripts/gate.ts`) is the full local check and holds no lock as a whole: typecheck, biome, the surface policy and the full vitest suite side by side in one `vitest` slot while the build takes the `build` slot, then, only if all passed, the full Playwright suite in a `playwright` slot on that build; its holds are exempt from the max hold. It is optional: the release gate is release.yml (see CI below). Run any other build, Playwright run or full vitest run as `bun run locked -- <command>` (e.g. `bun run locked -- bunx playwright test e2e/smoke.e2e.ts --output /tmp/<you>-results`); the kind comes from the command line (`--kind build|playwright|vitest|other` overrides; a compound `sh -c "bun run build && bunx playwright …"` counts as Playwright and its build takes the `build` slot by itself). It waits for a slot (printing `[lock] waited 42.3s for <holder>` past 1 s), stops the command's whole process tree and releases the slot when interrupted, and a nested locked command of a kind an ancestor holds runs inside it and logs nothing (`OMPDESK_BUILD_LOCK_HELD` lists the kinds held). **Max hold:** a non-gate hold past 15 minutes prints `[lock] WARNING …` and `lock-stats` flags it; split long work with `bun run locked -- --chunk <n> -- bunx playwright test …` (also vitest): it runs the command as `--shard=1/n` … `n/n`, one hold per chunk so waiters get a turn in between, all chunks on one pinned build. **Migration:** the old single lock directory `/tmp/ompdesk-build.lock`, while it exists, counts as holding every slot of every kind (old holders and `until mkdir` shell users keep working; new waiters queue behind them), and an entry without a `slot` in the log is from that time. Each release appends one JSON line (owner = `OMPDESK_LOCK_OWNER` or `user@checkout`, command, kind, cwd, `waitedMs`, `slot`, `via` (`gate`, `check:changed` or `chunk` for a phase of one), `heldMs`, exit code, ISO time) to `/tmp/ompdesk-build.log` (Windows: the temp dir; `scripts/lock-log.ts`, trimmed to its newest 4000 lines past 5000); `bun run lock-stats [--since 2h]` (default 24h, `all`) prints totals with average, p50 and p90 of wait and hold per owner and per kind, per slot with how busy it was, the holds over the limit, and the slots held right now. `OMPDESK_LOCK_SLOTS=playwright=3,vitest=2` (and `OMPDESK_LOCK_ROOT`, `OMPDESK_LOCK_POLL_MS`) change the lock for a measurement or a test, never for daily use.
- Parallel agents: the lock lets six heavy holds run at once (4 Playwright runs, 1 full vitest or check run, 1 build), so run up to six agents at once on this repo, each on one disjoint slice; a seventh only queues. Waiters are served first come, first served and a non-gate hold past 15 minutes is warned about and flagged, so one long job no longer holds up every short one the way the single lock did. Agents iterate with targeted vitest outside the lock, run their builds and e2e as short `bun run locked -- …` holds (several specs in one command, a long sweep split with `--chunk`), commit their verified hunks locally as they go, and leave the full gate to CI. `bun run lock-stats` shows whether agents are waiting more than working (avg wait over a minute means too many at once or a hold that should be split) and which slot each run used.
  - Fresh worktrees install through the repo's own `.ompdesk/worktree-setup` (`.cmd` on Windows; OMPdesk runs it after `worktree add`, run it yourself after a plain `git worktree add`): `bun install --frozen-lockfile` then `bun scripts/ensure-electron.ts`, which the `postinstall` script also runs on every `bun install`. Cause it fixes: bun runs electron's `install.js` with the `node` on PATH, and on Node 26 `extract-zip@2` never settles, so `install.js` exits 0 after unpacking only `dist/LICENSES.chromium.html` (no `path.txt`, no binary, "Electron failed to install correctly"; not the lockfile path, `minimumReleaseAge`, trust or `ELECTRON_SKIP_BINARY_DOWNLOAD`); `ensure-electron.ts` re-runs the installer under bun's runtime from the `~/Library/Caches/electron` zip. Never copy `node_modules` or `dist` from another checkout.
- Default agent loop: `bun run check:changed [--base <ref>] [--no-e2e] [--list]` (`scripts/check-changed.ts`), not the gate. It holds no lock as a whole: in a `vitest` slot it runs typecheck (whole project), biome on the changed files, the surface policy and `vitest related` (every test that imports a changed file, transitively) side by side, then `bun run build` (the `build` slot; reused when `out/` matches) and, in a `playwright` slot, the Playwright specs that touch the change: a changed spec, or a spec whose file name, or (for a hyphenated or camel-case name) text, mentions a changed source file's name (`layout/work-board/WorkBoard.tsx` → `e2e/work-board.e2e.ts`; `auto-title-core.ts` → `auto-title`; a plain word such as `window.ts` matches file names only). "Changed" = working tree against HEAD plus untracked files under `src/ e2e/ resources/ scripts/`. It is a name match, not a dependency graph: a change whose specs are named otherwise, or one in e2e support files, needs `bun run locked -- bunx playwright test <spec>` by hand; `--list` shows what it would pick without taking a slot. It picks from the default run only (no variants), so a typical 1–3 spec pick takes about 5–30 s. Cost: about 10 s of checks here (types 10 s, biome and surfaces under 1 s, related vitest 6 s) plus the matched specs, against 39 s for the full vitest alone and ~4.5 min for the gate. `bun run gate` runs every spec locally when someone wants it; neither the full vitest nor the full Playwright suite is needed between edits.
- Local timings, so they are not measured again (18 Oct 2026, 15-core Mac): gate ≈ 4.5 min (checks ~40 s, then Playwright 235 s: 204 ran, 918 s of test time). The slowest specs by test time: `deep-audit` 184 s over 3 shards, `mobile-shell` 45 s, `a11y-audit` 45 s, `autopilot-ship` 40 s, `remote-resume` 36 s, `autopilot-cycles` 36 s (`MEASURED_SECONDS` in `scripts/e2e-shard.ts`); ~4.5 s per test, ~1 s per launch on the fake omp. Full vitest 39 s wall (645 files, 6,855 tests, 186 s summed over files; it was 59 s on 17 Oct): `bridge-autopilot-stock` 13 s and `stock-helper-extensions` 12 s (the `*-stock` files run stock for real and stay ungated), `autopilot` 10 s (one template git repo per file in `beforeAll`, copied per test with `fs.cp`), `sidecar` 10 s, `ssh/service` 7 s (it waits on `SshTools.settled()`, never on fixed sleeps). A test waits on an event, a promise or fake timers, not on wall-clock time. The renderer project's deps optimizer saves ~4 s of 42 on two workers, so it stays. `bun run build` takes 8.5 s cold and 0.1 s on a hit. In the 24 h of 16 Oct, 307 Playwright runs held the lock 7 h 31 min (p50 27 s, p90 3 min 24 s) while others waited 6 h 28 min in all: the waits are the full suites in front of short holds, so iterate with `check:changed`. **Concurrent runs, measured 18 Oct 2026 to size the lock's slots** (other agents' work kept the load average at 9–35, so these are not clean-room numbers; a test that failed in every concurrent run of a round was a peer's edit, not load, and is not counted): Playwright, 15 default-run spec files (65 tests, ~190 s of test time) as K identical runs at once, round wall then wall per run (round ÷ K) and tests failed or flaky: 1 run × 4 workers 74 s (3 rounds; 1 of 195 tests); 2 × 4: 113 s, 56 s per run (2 of 520); 3 × 4: 153 s, 51 s per run (8 of 585, the `mobile-shell` long-press touch tests); 4 × 4: 175 s, 44 s per run (2 of 780); 3 × 2: 138 s, 46 s per run (1 of 390); 4 × 2: 144 s, 36 s per run (1 of 520). Four runs at once add no flakes beyond the solo baseline and 4 runs × 2 workers is the fastest per run, so `playwright` has 4 slots and 8 workers shared out. Full vitest (7,157 tests) as K runs at once, runs that lost a test beyond the two every run lost: 1 run × 8 workers 58 s (3 rounds; 0 of 3 runs); 2 × 8: 91 s, 45 s per run (3 of 6 runs lost a `*-stock` test: `bridge-accounts-stock`, `bridge-autopilot-stock` or `stock-helper`, real omp subprocesses that time out under load); 2 × 4: 132 s (3 of 6); 3 × 8: 128 s (3 of 3); 4 × 8: 180 s (4 of 4); 4 × 4: 161 s (4 of 4); 4 × 3: 175 s (4 of 4). Fewer workers did not cure it, so `vitest` has 1 slot. Before the split (6 h of 18 Oct, one lock for everything): 138 locked runs waited 11 h 12 min in all (avg 4 min 52 s; Playwright wait p90 12 min 55 s); one responsive-audit held the lock 93 min (5,602,285 ms) while another agent waited about 71 min.
- Opt-in model audits (`OMP_GUI_REAL_WORK=1`, they spend tokens): `e2e/real-work-map.e2e.ts` (two parallel subagents, records frames), `e2e/real-work-scenarios.e2e.ts` (edit, todos, failing command, silent command then stop, detached agent then stop, 20 commands, text only; light/dark captures plus a Korean relaunch) and `e2e/real-advisor.e2e.ts` (three turns with the advisor on: records the wire frames, asserts each custom message an `agent_end` commits — advisor card, background job result — carries its journal `entryId`, which the sidecar's `sessions.attach_entry_ids` stamps, and that its card is in the live transcript; `e2e/fixtures/real-advisor-frames.json` is a trimmed recording of it). All copy `config.yml` and `agent.db` into a private 0700 agent dir through `e2e/real-omp.ts` and run the main model on the user's `smol` role; `OMP_GUI_REAL_WORK_ARTIFACTS` picks the capture directory.
- CI (`.github/workflows/ci.yml`, `release.yml`, `e2e-shards.yml`, `e2e-nightly.yml`): the repo is public, so every job runs on a GitHub-hosted runner at no cost and the layout follows wall time, not billing. **Pre-flight, every push to `main`** (ci.yml, except a `release: vX.Y.Z` push, whose checks release.yml runs; docs-only pushes are skipped): the release's blocking checks side by side, so a release finds nothing red that a push did not already show — `static` (types, biome, surface policy), `check` (vitest as three jobs: `node`, `renderer 1/2`, `renderer 2/2`), `build`, `win-platform` (`bun run test:platform` on Windows) and `mac-smoke` (`@smoke` on macOS against a fresh build); a pull request runs only the Linux jobs. A failed job writes its failing step and the first error lines to its job summary at once (`scripts/ci-failure.sh`, the last step `if: failure()`, read-only `actions: read` token), so `gh run view` and the run page show what broke when the job ends, not when the run does. A failure never cancels independent jobs: `publish`'s `needs` already blocks the release, and `gh run rerun <id> --failed` then reruns only what failed. ci.yml cancels a superseded run of the same ref; release.yml cancels a running run only for a newer release push (never for a manual dispatch). **release.yml is the release gate**, nothing is run on this Mac first, and every job starts right after `prepare`: `build` (only `bun run build` and the `out/` upload: its end is what `win` and `mac` wait for), `static`, `check` (the three vitest jobs), `win-platform`, `mac-real` (`e2e/real-core.e2e.ts` against the latest omp, on build's `out/`), `mac` (package build's `out/`, verify, `@smoke`) and `win` (package build's `out/`, `@smoke`). `mac`, `mac-real` and `win` install their dependencies meanwhile and wait for `build`'s `out/` artifact with `scripts/ci-fetch-out.sh` (polls this run's artifacts, `gh run download`, fails if build ends without one) instead of `needs: build`, which would start them only after build's job is over; none builds a second time, so the packaging tests exercise the one shared build (`out/` is byte-identical on every OS). `publish` needs prepare, build, static, check, `win-platform`, `mac`, `mac-real` and `win`. The full Playwright suite is **not part of a release**: `e2e-nightly.yml` (03:17 UTC and on demand) and ci.yml's `e2e=true` run it in 4 `macos-latest` shards from `e2e-shards.yml` (`scripts/e2e-shard.ts` deals whole spec files by estimated cost, because `playwright --shard` counts tests that skip inside their body and leaves a shard nearly empty; `deep-audit` keeps its own `SETTINGS_SHARDS` split inside its shard; each shard installs omp with `bun install -g` since the runner is ephemeral), informational, and the local `bun run gate` is the full-suite proof. A public repo runs at most 5 macOS jobs at once; raise the shard count only together with that cap. The real Windows install of machine setup is manual only (`gh workflow run ci.yml -f install_check=true`): the `install-check` job on `windows-latest` runs `bun scripts/setup-install-check.ts --hide-system-git` (the runner's preinstalled Git for Windows hidden, so PortableGit installs) against a temp USERPROFILE and asserts omp is detected at the minimum, the PortableGit download reported byte progress, the registry PATH refresh found Bun, and stock omp's shell resolves to that Git Bash, not cmd.exe. The Windows setup itself is one click, per user (`electron-builder.win.yml`: `oneClick`, no `perMachine`; `resources/installer.nsh` removes an all-users copy the old assisted installer left, after one question and one UAC prompt — the only case that asks anything), checked by hand with `gh workflow run ci.yml -f installer_check=true`: the `installer-check` job packages it and runs `scripts/windows-installer-check.ps1` silently, as a double-click (it must finish with only a progress bar and start the app), and over `legacy_tag`'s all-users install, checking the folder, the Start-menu and desktop shortcuts, the HKCU uninstall entry, the app's window and the uninstall.
- CI timing notes (hosted 2-core runners, 17 Oct 2026; the before is v0.34.0's release run: `build` 1:25 with types and biome inside it, vitest 2:56, `win` 3:55, `mac` 4:31, ~6:40 to `publish`). Parallel graph, dry run 39054429844 (the Windows `@smoke` failure in it was a test, not the graph): `build` 0:39 (the `out/` upload is its last step, ~1:00 earlier than before), `static` 0:53, vitest `node` 1:09 and `renderer` halves 1:04 and 1:14 (one job took 2:56), `mac-real` 2:07, `mac` 4:53 (install 11 s, `out/` in hand 18 s later, package 48 s, verify 28 s, `@smoke` 2:45), the last gate ending 5:08 after the run started; `win` ended 6:14 because `test:platform` held it for 2:01 before packaging. That is why `win-platform` is its own job: with it split (run 39054966147, `mac` slowed by other runs sharing the macOS runners) `win` ended 3:46 in and `win-platform` 3:17. So the macOS path (package + verify + `@smoke`, ~4:50) is the floor for the time to `publish`: about 5 minutes against 6:40, and no dry run has been fully green yet, so the end-to-end number to `publish` itself is not measured. Levers left: fewer or faster macOS `@smoke` specs, and running `Verify bundle` beside `@smoke`. vitest: the renderer project is most of the work, and two renderer shards beside the node job cut the wall time from ~3 minutes to ~1:10; a third shard would save little against the ~25 s of setup. bun install: a cold install takes 2-10 s on Linux and ~10 s on macOS, so `actions/cache` keyed on `bun.lock` saves nothing there (a restore costs about as much); on Windows a restore (17 s) plus install (18 s) measured no faster than the 28-41 s of a cold install on the workspace volume, and its upload costs 42 s per lockfile change, so no job caches. Windows `win` spent 61 s of 262 s on one 7z pass: electron-builder packs the NSIS payload at `-mx=9` whatever `compression:` says, so `win`'s Package step sets `ELECTRON_BUILDER_COMPRESSION_LEVEL=4` (about 27 s for a setup about a quarter larger, ~110 MB instead of 88 MB; the Mac DMG is untouched; never below 4). `win`'s lever is fewer `@smoke` specs, since every `@smoke` spec guards a Windows-only failure. `ELECTRON_SKIP_BINARY_DOWNLOAD` on Windows fails (gpu-fallback and sidecar tests import electron) and saves nothing. ci.yml skips docs, `LICENSE`, `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.txt`, issue templates and the health-check workflow.
- Public-repo trust rules for workflows: no workflow uses `pull_request_target`; a pull request (fork or not) runs only ci.yml's Linux jobs (`static`, `check`, `build`), with a read-only `GITHUB_TOKEN` and no secrets; release.yml starts only from a push to `main` that touches `package.json` or from a manual dispatch (write access); the signing secrets `MAC_SIGNING_P12_BASE64` and `MAC_SIGNING_PASSWORD` are read only by `mac`'s Package step and only for `main` (a dry run from another ref signs ad hoc); every job has `permissions: contents: read` (plus `actions: read` where it lists the run's artifacts or jobs, for `ci-fetch-out.sh` and `ci-failure.sh`) except `publish` (`contents: write`); every action is pinned to a commit SHA. Repo settings that back this: Settings › Actions › General › "Require approval for all outside collaborators" and workflow permissions read-only. The former self-hosted runner (`ompdesk-mac`, `~/actions-runner-ompdesk`) is retired: on that Mac `./svc.sh stop && ./svc.sh uninstall`, then Settings › Actions › Runners › remove it; the secret `RUNNER_STATUS_TOKEN` is unused and can be deleted.
- Windows e2e (`@smoke`, never run by a human): the fake-omp specs tagged `@smoke` in their title cover the setup takeover (`machine-setup`), split select (`split-select`, en light), auto-resume after a kill and after a normal quit (`interrupted-resume`; the kill goes to the app's own main pid, because on Windows Playwright's `app.process()` is a launcher one level above it and killing that leaves the app running), the Model roles page, the Files edit and Mica plus close-to-tray (`desktop-surfaces`; the tray test runs without `OMP_GUI_HEADLESS` on a real desktop and returns the window with a second launch), a new window opening in the saved light and dark theme like the first (`window-theme`: `nativeTheme.themeSource`, pre-paint and the surface colors of both windows, with Playwright's color-scheme emulation off), the packaged app's window really drawn on screen (`first-launch-window`: `dist/win-unpacked/OMPdesk.exe`, or `OMP_GUI_E2E_EXE`, without `OMP_GUI_HEADLESS` on an empty profile and on a saved maximized window, the screen pixels under the window captured and checked for one flat color — a Mica window shown maximized stayed blank, black with a GPU, white on the runner, while its renderer had drawn the app, so `WindowManager.createWindow` gives a window that opens maximized no Mica), the Changes commit (`source-control`, no stand-in `gh`, which is a shell script), Preview, and a message sent while a turn runs (`steer-send`, 8 s there: Enter, the Send button and the platform's send chord — ⌘↵, Ctrl+Enter on Windows — reach the fake omp as `steer`; only the follow-up chord `composerFollowUpChord` in `lib/keymap.ts`, ⌃↵ on macOS and ⌥↵ on Windows because ⌃↵ is ⌘↵ there, sends `follow_up`; Ctrl+Enter once read as the follow-up chord on Windows, so a Windows steer waited in the follow-up lane for the turn to end). release.yml's `win` job runs them all after packaging: about 105 s there, against 18 s for the boot spec alone (two `--shard` halves over two jobs would save 30 s of wall time but pay a second checkout and install, so they don't). Tag a new spec `@smoke` only when it is worth ~10–25 s there. `gh workflow run ci.yml --ref <branch> -f win_e2e=true [-f win_e2e_grep=<regex>]` runs them, preceded by `scripts/omp-update-lock-check.ts`, on windows-latest with captures in the `e2e-windows-captures` artifact (`OMP_GUI_DESKTOP_CAPTURES`, `OMP_GUI_SETUP_CAPTURES`, `OMP_GUI_SPLIT_SELECT_CAPTURES`, `OMP_GUI_SCM_CAPTURES`, `OMP_GUI_PREVIEW_SCREENSHOTS`). The lock check is manual only (18 s, so it stays out of `win`) and is omp's auto-update while omp runs: a fake npm registry, the real `bun install -g` into a temp BUN_INSTALL, one process holding the package's files open with a DLL loaded from it, then the app's own `SetupService.updateOmp`; on windows-latest the update goes through (Bun replaces the files; the running omp keeps its loaded code, its lazy assets stay readable, `omp --version` reports the new version), so the app needs no EBUSY handling beyond the recorded failure.
  - Since 18 Oct `@smoke` also covers pairing two devices and a prompt on the host (`remote-devices`), the browser sign-in (`oauth-login`, en light), deleting sessions (`session-delete`), Autopilot's first cycles (`autopilot-cycles`, en light), the boot (`smoke`) and, on macOS only, the real terminal (`terminal`, light: the Windows e2e jobs install no stock omp, whose PTY natives it runs) and Mobile web (`mobile-web`, en light; the fake Tailscale is a bun shebang script). On windows-latest the set ran 19 passed in 2.1 min (ci.yml run 39054830701). A path inside a CSS attribute selector goes through `cssString()` (`e2e/harness.ts`): a raw Windows path's backslashes read as CSS escapes and the locator matches nothing, which was the whole first Windows failure of `remote-devices` and `session-delete`. Dispatch a Windows run from a branch nobody pushes to (ci.yml cancels a superseded run per ref, so a push to `main` cancels a dispatch on `main`).
  - A spec that launches Playwright's own Chromium (`chromium.launch()`: `device-prefs`, `pwa-install`) is not `@smoke` (18 Oct pre-flight 39110504337: `mac-smoke` had no browser, "chromium_headless_shell … Executable doesn't exist"). The release jobs install none (a headless shell is ~196 MB unpacked, a download on every `mac` and `win` run for two specs that guard a feature, not a packaged build: `mobile-web` already walks a phone through the packaged app's serve path), so they run in the nightly and on-demand suite, whose `e2e-shards.yml` installs `chromium webkit`. Give such a spec `@smoke` only together with a `bunx playwright install --only-shell chromium` step in ci.yml's `mac-smoke` and release.yml's `mac` and `win`.
- Never drive the app with synthetic OS input (osascript keystrokes etc.); use Playwright.
- Dev: `bun run dev` (HMR against the system omp; opens a visible window).
- Never reinstall `/Applications/OMPdesk.app` from inside the app (you may be running *inside* OMPdesk; quitting it kills this session). `scripts/reinstall-mac.sh` (`bun run package:mac -- --publish never && bash scripts/reinstall-mac.sh`) runs **only when the user explicitly asks for a local reinstall**. **Never** schedule a swap with `launchctl submit` (launchd keeps submitted jobs alive and reruns them, so the app quits and relaunches forever) or any `KeepAlive` job.
- Child processes the app spawns must not outlive it: they get an open stdin pipe and exit when it closes (see `resources/gui-bridge/stats-host.ts`), because a crash or force-kill skips the quit handlers. The one process meant to outlive the app is ai-memory's own per-user service (launchd LaunchAgent / Windows logon entry, serving the terminal omp too): it is not OMPdesk's child but the service `ai-memory setup` installs. That `setup` is supervised like any installer and dies with the app; it runs isolated (`SpawnSpec.isolated`) so the service holds no handle of the app's (see "Memory is ai-memory" above). A new command whose descendant outlives the app by design runs isolated the same way.

## Change workflow

Changes are committed and pushed as they land, and released in small batches without waiting to be asked (the repo is public, so CI and releases cost nothing):

1. Each finished fix or feature: run its own checks (`bun run check:changed`), commit it with a normal descriptive message (no version bump, no CHANGELOG release section) and push it to `main`. ci.yml's `check` runs on the push; a red `check` is fixed before anything else lands.
2. Release when a few user-visible fixes or features have gathered, about once or twice a day, never several times an hour: every release prompts every user to update and restart. A defect in the released version that makes the app unusable ships right away on its own. No local gate: ci.yml's pre-flight already ran the blocking checks on every push, and release.yml is the gate (types, biome, surface policy, vitest, the Windows platform tests, `mac` and `win` packaging with `@smoke`, real-core); the full Playwright suite runs nightly and on demand (e2e-nightly.yml), not in a release.
3. Release with the steps below: one dated `CHANGELOG.md` section (Korean summary plus English summary) covering every commit since the last release, bump the version once, commit `release: vX.Y.Z`, push, and watch the release run to the end (rerun only the failed jobs). Tell the user when a release went out.

A release run takes about 5 minutes to `publish`, bound by `mac` (the v0.34.0 run took 6:40: build 1:25, check 2:56, win 3:55, mac 4:31; every blocking job now starts right after `prepare`, see the CI timing notes). Never leave an `Unreleased` section behind on `main`. Do not reinstall the local app unless the user asks — the in-app updater picks the release up.

## Release

- Version `X.Y.Z` semver; tag `vX.Y.Z`; release commit `release: vX.Y.Z`. Commit messages carry no AI attribution trailers.
1. Pick the version from the commits since the last release tag (features → minor, fixes only → patch). Bump `package.json` `version`, add a dated `## [X.Y.Z] - YYYY-MM-DD` section to `CHANGELOG.md`: the Korean summary first, then a line holding only `<!-- en -->`, then the English summary of the same content (release.yml turns the marker into a `---` rule, so the GitHub notes carry both, Korean first; the What's new window and the changelog window show the part for the UI language and fall back to Korean when a release has no English part — `changelogSection`/`changelogReleases` in `src/renderer/lib/whats-new.ts`). **Open each section with one plain sentence**: the What's new card (`layout/WhatsNewCard.tsx`, one in-flow row under the title bar mounted by `AppShell`, never over a composer, transcript or the side panel, once per new version, never during the setup takeover, first run or the tour) shows that first paragraph (`changelogSummary`) as its summary, "자세히 보기" opens the section in the dialog, and after skipped versions one card names the newest while the dialog lists every release since the last seen one (`changelogSince`); the card stays until dismissed or opened and hides itself after 60 s of focused, unhovered time. Update README version mentions. Confirm `MIN_OMP_VERSION` is current.
2. `git commit -am "release: vX.Y.Z" && git push origin main`. Do not tag. The release tag lands on the commit the run starts from, and that commit's own diff must not touch `.github/workflows/*`: `publish` creates the tag with the run's `GITHUB_TOKEN`, which never has the `workflows` permission, so GitHub refuses the ref with HTTP 403 "Resource not accessible by integration" although the token holds `contents: write` (v0.35.1's first run on 1b85eda). A workflow change ships in its own commit, followed by an ordinary commit (the release commit, or an empty `chore: release point …`) before the release is dispatched.
3. `.github/workflows/release.yml` does the rest, as the release gate (see the CI bullet above for its jobs and rules); every job starts right after `prepare`: `prepare` reads the version and notes; `build` builds the app bundle `out/` once and uploads it (the bundle is byte-identical whichever OS builds it); `static` runs types, biome and the surface policy; `check` runs vitest as three parallel jobs (the node project and two renderer shards); `win-platform` runs `bun run test:platform` on Windows (the Windows test scope above); `mac-real` runs `e2e/real-core.e2e.ts` on build's `out/`; `mac` and `win` install while `build` runs, wait for its `out/` artifact (`scripts/ci-fetch-out.sh` polls the run's artifacts instead of `needs: build`, which would wait for the whole job), package that same `out/` (`mac`: the arm64 DMG, signed with the pinned certificate on `main`, codesign + designated requirement + `gui-bridge` verified; `win`: the x64 NSIS setup, since `makensis` cannot run on Apple Silicon) and run `@smoke` on the fake omp. The full Playwright suite is not part of a release (`e2e-nightly.yml`). Only if everything passed, `publish` creates tag `vX.Y.Z` and the GitHub Release (title `vX.Y.Z`) with exactly two assets, `ompdesk-mac-arm64.dmg` and `ompdesk-windows-setup.exe` (version-free, so `/releases/latest/download/<name>` links stay stable); notes = changelog section + minimum omp version + the update metadata comment `<!-- ompdesk-update {"version","releaseDate","files":{"mac"|"windows":{"name","size","sha512"}}} -->` (sha512 in base64), which the "Append update metadata" step computes from the files it uploads and GitHub does not render. No `latest*.yml`, ZIP, blockmap or portable exe. An existing tag or an `Unreleased` heading makes the run a no-op. The package run artifacts only carry the files to `publish` and expire after 1 day (7 on a dry run); the Release keeps the assets.
   - **Legacy feed switch**: `LEGACY_UPDATE_FEED` at the top of release.yml, `"false"` since v0.18.1. Installs up to v0.17.x updated from electron-updater's `latest-mac.yml`/`latest.yml`, fetching `ompdesk-X.Y.Z-arm64-mac.zip` (relaunch), `ompdesk-X.Y.Z-arm64.dmg` (manual) and `ompdesk-X.Y.Z-setup.exe` by exact name. Set to `"true"`, the mac job also builds the ZIP (`electron-builder --mac dmg zip`) and both jobs add the yml plus every file it lists, so such installs update once into the new updater; v0.18.0 carried that bridge. While it is `"false"`, `publish` refuses any release that does not have exactly the two assets.
4. Watch it to the end: `gh run watch <run-id> --exit-status`. A failure the code didn't cause (runner, network, flaky test): `gh run rerun <run-id> --failed` reruns only the failed jobs — never start a whole new run for it. Otherwise fix the cause on `main` and re-run with `gh workflow run release.yml` (on main). Rehearse without releasing from any branch: `gh workflow run release.yml --ref <branch> -f dry_run=true` (packages land as run artifacts for 7 days).

Manual fallback: `bun run package:mac -- --publish never` on a Mac, `bun run package:win -- --publish never` on Windows, copy `dist/ompdesk-X.Y.Z-arm64.dmg` to `ompdesk-mac-arm64.dmg` and `dist/ompdesk-X.Y.Z-setup.exe` to `ompdesk-windows-setup.exe`, append the metadata comment to the notes the way the "Append update metadata" step does (a release without it is not offered as an update), then `gh release create vX.Y.Z --target main --title vX.Y.Z --notes-file <notes> ompdesk-mac-arm64.dmg ompdesk-windows-setup.exe`.

The repo is public, so the in-app updater works without credentials (`src/main/update-auth.ts`): the release lookup and the asset download carry no Authorization header. A token is optional and only added when one is configured (`GH_TOKEN`/`GITHUB_TOKEN` → `gh auth token` → a safeStorage-encrypted token from Settings › Updates): it raises GitHub's rate limit (anonymous is 60 requests an hour per address; a check is one request every 5 minutes, 12 an hour) and keeps updates working if the repo is ever private again. A token GitHub answers 401 (expired `GH_TOKEN`, stale `gh` login) is dropped and the check retried anonymously, since 401 fails even public reads; only an anonymous request answered "not found" (the repo is private again) ends in the `auth-required` state, and a 403 with `x-ratelimit-remaining: 0` reads as a failed request, not as missing access. Each check reads the latest release (`/releases/latest`, or `/releases` with pre-releases on) through the API, and `src/main/update-release.ts` validates the metadata comment with zod against the release itself (tag = version, asset present with that size) exactly the same with or without a token. A missing or malformed comment, or one that contradicts the release, means no update and an error state ("no valid update information"); a release without this platform's file reports a missing installer. `src/main/update-provider.ts` hands electron-updater a custom provider (`setFeedURL({ provider: "custom", updateProvider })`) whose UpdateInfo points at the API asset endpoint (plus the token when there is one; `githubApiHeaders` in `updater-state.ts` builds both header sets); differential download is off (no blockmaps). Never bundle a token. Exercise it unpackaged with `OMP_DEV_UPDATE_CHECK=1 OMP_DEV_UPDATE_MODE=manual` (plus `OMP_DEV_UPDATE_PRERELEASE=1` to accept a throwaway pre-release), launching Electron on the repo root so the version is `package.json`'s. `OMP_DEV_UPDATE_MODE=relaunch OMP_DEV_UPDATE_BUNDLE=<writable copy of OMPdesk.app>` runs the relaunch path instead, staging the release against that stand-in bundle and swapping it on quit (unpackaged only).

Install modes (`src/main/updater.ts`): Windows uses electron-updater's NSIS flow (background download, SHA-512 checked, applied on restart or quit). Every macOS build, ad-hoc or certificate-signed, avoids Squirrel, which would need the ZIP the release no longer ships. When the bundle sits in a writable location it runs **relaunch** mode: download `ompdesk-mac-arm64.dmg` in the background into `<userData>/updates/ompdesk-X.Y.Z-arm64.dmg`, verify its SHA-512, `hdiutil attach -nobrowse -readonly` it, copy the `.app` out with `ditto`, detach, check the bundle's `codesign --verify --deep --strict`, bundle id and version (`stageBundleFromDmg` in `updater-state.ts`), then on "Restart & install" (or any approved quit) a detached `/bin/bash` helper (`SWAP_SCRIPT`) waits for the app to exit, swaps the bundle with rollback, and reopens it on restart. Read-only locations (DMG, App Translocation) fall back to the same DMG, verified and opened in Finder from Downloads (**manual** mode).

OMPdesk installs its own update by itself only when the user turns on Settings › Updates & about › Install updates automatically (pref `appAutoUpdate`, `APP_AUTO_UPDATE_PREF`, default off; `AppAutoUpdateRow.tsx`). Off, everything above stays as it is: check, background download, "Restart & install", applied on the click or any approved quit. On, once a verified update is downloaded and `restartableUpdate()` (relaunch mode or Windows NSIS; never the manual DMG, whose switch is disabled with the reason, `updater:installMode`), `AutoInstallScheduler` (`src/main/app-auto-update.ts`, electron-free, injected clock, ticked every second by `updater.ts`) decides: a quiet moment is no tab running a turn or compacting and no ask open (`resumeFacts`/`tabInventory`, this computer's tabs and the ones it hosts) and either every window unfocused or no user input for 2 min (`powerMonitor.getSystemIdleTime`). Still not quiet after 30 min: with Continue interrupted work (`resumeInterrupted`) on it goes ahead anyway, with it off it never forces the restart and keeps waiting for idle. It then announces the restart: `UpdateStatus.downloaded.restartAt` (epoch ms, 60 s ahead) shows one quiet line with a countdown and "Now / Later" in the sidebar update card and Settings › Updates & about — never a modal; "Later" (`updater:postpone`) postpones to the next idle window and restarts the 30 min wait. At the deadline it calls `restartIntoUpdate()` — the existing quit path (`requestQuit`/`quitAndInstall`, so drafts, layout and resume markers survive) — except that with resume off and work that started during the countdown it withdraws the notice instead of asking. Dev seams (unpackaged only): `OMP_DEV_UPDATE_STAGED` (+ `OMP_DEV_UPDATE_STAGED_VERSION`) treats a bundle as the verified staged update in relaunch mode (with `OMP_DEV_UPDATE_MODE=relaunch`, `OMP_DEV_UPDATE_BUNDLE`), `OMP_DEV_UPDATE_AUTO_COUNTDOWN_MS` shortens the countdown; `e2e/app-auto-update.e2e.ts` covers pref on (installs unclicked) and off (waits).

Every update decision is written to `<userData>/logs/gui-runtime.jsonl` (source `app-update`, `updater.ts` `logUpdate`, the existing runtime log writer; `details.level: "info"`; never the update token or a URL — failure reasons go through `scrubUpdateText` in `update-log-core.ts`, which drops URLs and the token and runs the diagnostics redactor), so a restart nobody clicked is explained from the log (Copy diagnostics carries the last 100 lines, redacted as usual). `message` names the area, `details.event` the step: `check` {event: `no-update` {current} | `found` {version, current, mode} | `error` {kind: a release-metadata code, `auth-required:<reason>`, `check-failed` or `no-result`, reason}} on every check (a background one every 5 min); `download` {event: `started` {version, mode, bytes} | `verified` {version, mode, bytes, ms} (SHA-512 matched; relaunch mode: bundle staged and verified) | `failed` {version, mode, reason}} (`mode`: relaunch, manual, or nsis for electron-updater's own download); `scheduler` (`AutoInstallScheduler`'s injected `log`: transitions only, never one line per 1 s tick): `waiting` {reason: `busy turn` | `open ask` | `focused+active input`, waitedMs} once per reason and again when the reason changes, `quiet` {waitedMs, idleMs, focused} (the quiet moment was found), `forced` {waitedMs, maxWaitMs, resume: true, reason} (30 min, Continue interrupted work on), `countdown` {restartAt}, `postponed` {by: user} (Later), `withdrawn` {reason: `pref off` | `update not ready` | `work started, resume off`}, `install` {trigger: `auto` (the scheduler logs it) | `user click` (Restart & install, or a paired device's) | `quit` (an approved quit with an update staged)}; `swap` {event: `helper` {mode: `relaunch` (the macOS swap script, + relaunch) | `nsis`, from, to, trigger}} when the helper starts (it also writes `<userData>/update-install.json`), and on the next launch one `swap` {event: `result`, outcome: `installed` (the new version runs) | `rolled back` (the old one still does) | `changed`, previous, expected, current, mode, trigger, launchedAt, and on macOS swapAt + swapOutcome, the closing line of `swap.log`'s last entry, e.g. `install failed; previous app restored`}. Pinned by `app-auto-update.test.ts` (transitions under the injected clock, no duplicate lines across ticks) and `update-log-core.test.ts`.

macOS signing: `scripts/mac-sign.cjs` (electron-builder `mac.sign`) signs every bundle with OMPdesk's own self-signed certificate, pinned by SHA-1 in `scripts/mac-signing.json`, so the designated requirement (`identifier "com.seuput.ompdesk" and certificate root = H"…"`) is the same in every release and macOS keeps its privacy grants (folders, local network, microphone) across updates; an ad-hoc signature's requirement is the build's cdhash, so each update re-asked them all. The identity comes from the repository secrets `MAC_SIGNING_P12_BASE64` / `MAC_SIGNING_PASSWORD` (release.yml sets `OMPDESK_MAC_SIGNING_REQUIRED=1` and checks the requirement), else the maintainer's local copy in `~/.ompdesk-signing/` (p12 + password; the only backup — losing it means a new certificate and one more round of prompts for every user), else an ad-hoc fallback with a warning. It is imported into a throwaway keychain passed to codesign with `--keychain`; the user's keychain search list is never touched. Never replace the certificate. Builds carry no Apple Team ID, so keychain items still bind to the cdhash (hence safeStorage stays untouched unless a stored token exists), and nothing is notarized: downloaded DMGs need right-click → Open once, locally built apps run without Gatekeeper prompts.

## Code conventions

- TypeScript: no `any`, no inline `import("…")` types, `#private` fields, Bun APIs where cleaner.
- i18n: the UI ships English and Korean only (`UI_LANGUAGES` in `src/shared/ui-language.ts`). Every user-visible string goes through `useT()` with the same key in `src/renderer/locales/en.ts` and `ko.ts` (`locales.test.ts` enforces identical key sets). Settings-schema text lives in `src/renderer/components/settings/schema-ko.ts` and `schema-option-ko.ts`; main-process strings in `src/main/i18n.ts` and `tray-labels.ts`.
- Model output renders through `MarkdownRenderer` (`src/renderer/lib/markdown.tsx`, rehype-sanitize) — never `dangerouslySetInnerHTML` with model text. Text a person typed (prompt bubbles) passes `userText`: single-dollar math stays literal and HTML tags show as typed (`remark-html-text.ts`).
- A path a reply or tool card links is never handed to `shell.openPath` when opening it would run something: `openLocalPath` (`src/main/open-path.ts`) reveals launcher types (`.app .command .sh .jar .exe .bat .cmd .ps1 .lnk .url .msi`, scripts an interpreter's install may run on open on either platform — `.py .pyw .pyz .pl .rb`, python.org's Python Launcher runs a `.py` on macOS —, the Windows script, scriptlet, shortcut, help, add-in and theme types (`.chm .xll .wsc .sct .theme .settingcontent-ms` and the like), macOS `.terminal .workflow .pkg .dmg .scpt .webloc` and the like), anything an executable bit marks on POSIX, and any symlink to either, in Finder/Explorer instead. A new caller that opens a model-supplied path goes through it.
- Tool-rendered text uses the helpers in `src/renderer/lib/format.ts`; preview ceilings come from `src/renderer/lib/preview.ts`; subprocess output renders through `AnsiText` (`src/renderer/lib/ansi.tsx`).
- Component tests use the linkedom harness (see `src/renderer/components/chat/ThinkingBlock.test.tsx`): mount through `I18nProvider`, reset zustand stores in `afterEach` via their `reset()`/setters — never `mock.module()`.  - The manifest sets no `orientation` (pinned by `manifest.test.ts`): Chromium's WebAPK maps `"any"` (and the sensor values) to a full-sensor lock that overrides the user's rotation lock, and `natural`/`portrait` would stop tablets rotating, so the system setting decides; never call `screen.orientation.lock`. An installed app picks up a manifest change only after Chrome's WebAPK update check (up to about a day) or a reinstall.
